Non-regular trianguline representations
Martina Fruttidoro

Abstract
. Let G ℚ p G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}} be the absolute Galois group of ℚ p \mathbb{Q}_{p} and let L L be a finite extension of ℚ p \mathbb{Q}_{p} . Moreover let ρ ¯ : G ℚ p → G ​ L n ​ ( k L ) \bar{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(k_{L}) be a continous representation of G ℚ p G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}} , where k L k_{L} is the residue field of L L . We find sufficient conditions for which a trianguline representation ρ : G ℚ p → G ​ L n ​ ( L ) \rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(L) lifting ρ ¯ \bar{\rho} is a point of the trianguline variety associated to ρ ¯ \bar{\rho} .



Introduction

The
p
-adic local Langlands programme (for
p
a prime number) conjectures a correspondence between

p
-adic Galois representations and certain
p
-adic representations of reductive groups over
p
-adic local fields.
The theory of
p
-adic automorphic forms allows us to attach a
p
-adic Galois representation
to a certain
p
-adic automorphic form; thus, this approach provides a plausible candidate for the
correspondence between
p
-adic Galois representations that arise in this way
and
p
-adic automorphic representations of
GL_{n}
.
One nice aspect of this theory is that
p
-adic automorphic forms come in families parametrised by
geometric objects called
eigenvarieties
.
In
[
7
, §3.2]
, Breuil, Hellmann and Schraen construct an analogue of an eigenvariety
called
patched eigenvariety
using the
patching module
constructed by
Caraiani, Emerton, Gee, Geraghty, Paškūnas and Shin in
[
10
]
.
In
[
7
]
, the authors also construct an analogue of an
eigenvariety in the world of Galois representations, called
trianguline variety
.
Both the patched eigenvariety and the trianguline variety are defined as
subspaces of
\mathfrak{X}_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}^{n}
(see below for definitions of these spaces)
and conjecturally they coincide (up to power series-rings over deformation rings at auxiliary places away from
p
).
One of the main results of this thesis is the description of the underlying set of points of the
trianguline variety in some special cases: we will show that in these cases,
for a trianguline representation
\rho
, the representation
\rho
is a point of the trianguline variety.
Despite what the name might suggest, the above statement is highly non-trivial:
as we will see, the trianguline variety is by definition the closure of the set of trianguline
representations with a regularity condition on the parameters and
it is unclear whether such closure adds all trianguline representations.
In the following, I will give a more precise summary of the main results.

Let
L
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
A
be an affinoid
L
-algebra.
Let us denote by
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
the absolute Galois group of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let us fix a natural number
n\geqslant 1
.
One of the main results in the theory of
p
-adic Galois representations is that there
exists a fully faithful and exact functor

\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig}}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\text{continous representations }\\
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(A)\end{array}\right\}\rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\text{\'{e}tale }(\phi,\Gamma)\text{-modules}\\
\text{over }\mathcal{R}_{A}\text{ of rank }n\end{array}\right\}.

A continous representation
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(L)
is

trianguline
if
\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\rho)
is
triangulable
,
i.e. it is a successive extension of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules of rank 1.
This kind of representations constitute a very important category of representations
in the framework of the
p
-adic local Langlands programme: they first appeared in the
work of Colmez
[
12
]
, who showed that 2-dimensional trianguline representations
are Zariski dense in the deformation space of 2-dimensional
p
-adic Galois representations.
This result was crucial for proving the
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence
for
GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

In
[
22
]
, Kedlaya, Pottharst and Xiao prove that it is possible to
classify
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules of rank 1 in a nice way:
they show that there exists a bijection

\displaystyle\{\text{continous characters }\delta:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}\}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\{\text{free }(\phi,\Gamma)\text{-modules of rank }1\text{ over }\mathcal{R}_{A}\}

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{R}_{A}(\delta).

The functor
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times},-)
of continous characters of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
is representable by a rigid space
\mathcal{T}
,
which constitutes then a nice moduli space for
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules of rank 1.
Now let us fix a residual representation
\bar{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(k_{L})

(where
k_{L}
is the residue field of
L
) and let us denote by
\mathfrak{X}_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}
the
rigid analytic generic fibre of its universal framed deformation space.
Let us define

U_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})\coloneqq\left\{(\rho,\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{n}\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\rho\text{ is trianguline}\\
\text{with graded pieces}\\
\text{of a filtration of }\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)\\
\text{ given by }(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\end{array}\right\}\subset\mathfrak{X}_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}^{n},

where
\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{n}
is a subspace of
\mathcal{T}^{n}
of tuples of characters satisfying a certain
regularity condition (see (
1.4
) in the body of the paper).
The
trianguline variety
is the Zariski closure of
U_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})
inside
\mathfrak{X}_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}^{n}

and is denoted by
X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})
.
This space was first introduced by Hellmann in
[
17
]

and its geometry has been studied in
[
6
]
,
[
7
]

and
[
8
]
, yet there still are some properties to be investigated.
Consider for example a trianguline representation
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(L)

and let
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
be
parameters
for
\rho
, which means that

\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
has a filtration with graded pieces
\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{i})
;
it is well-known that up to conjugation by a matrix in
GL_{n}(L)
, we can assume
\rho

has values in
GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
.
Let
\bar{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(k_{L})
be the reduction of
\rho
to the residue field of
L
.
We can then ask ourselves whether
(\rho,\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})
is a point of
X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})(L)
or not.

Conjecture
.

Let
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(L)
be a trianguline representation with parameters

\underline{\delta}\coloneqq(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
and let

\bar{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(k_{L})
be the reduction of
\rho
to the residue field of
L
.
Then
(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})(L)
.

Of course if
\underline{\delta}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}(L)
, then we have that

(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in U_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})(L)
, hence in particular
(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})(L)
.
But as remarked before,
the above conjecture is not obvious at all in case
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\notin\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}(L)
.
We will prove that
(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})
if either
n\leqslant 4
, or the number
of non-regularities in
\underline{\delta}
is at most 5, or if
\underline{\delta}

doesn’t present non-regularities of type
\mathcal{T}^{+}
(see
1.3
for a definition of
\mathcal{T}^{+}
).
More precisely, we will prove the following Theorems.

Theorem A
.

Let
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})

be a trianguline representation of parameters
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
.
Moreover let
\bar{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(k_{L})
be the reduction of
\rho
to the residue field of
L
.
If
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)
for all
i<j
, then

(\rho,\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})(L)
.

Theorem B
.

Let
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})

be a trianguline representation of parameters
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
.
Moreover let
\bar{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(k_{L})
be the reduction of
\rho
to the residue field of
L
.

(1)

If
n\leqslant 4
, then
(\rho,\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})(L)
.

(2)

If
|\{\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}\colon i<j\}|\leqslant 5
, then
(\rho,\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})(L)
.

For
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules there is a notion of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology, which coincides with Galois cohomology
in case the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module comes from a family of Galois representations.
In the statement of Theorem
A
, the space
\mathcal{T}^{+}
is the subspace of
\mathcal{T}
consisting of
characters
\delta
such that the second
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology is non-trivial.

The strategy used to prove the above results is the following: given a trianguline representation

\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(L)
and
\underline{\delta}\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
characters associated
to a filtration of
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
, we will show that
(\rho,\underline{\delta})

is in the Zariski closure of some “regular family”.
More precisely, for each given
\rho
, we will construct a rigid space
X_{n}
with a family of triangulable

(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{n}
over
X_{n}
such that

•

the family
\mathbb{D}_{n}
specializes to
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
at some point
x\in X_{n}(L)
;

•

the triangulation of
\mathbb{D}_{n}
gives rise to characters
(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(X_{n})

such that there exists an open dense
U\xhookrightarrow{j}X_{n}
for which
j^{*}\widetilde{\underline{\delta}}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{n}(U)
.

For a detailed explanation of how the strategy implies the desired results, refer to Theorem
3.3
.

Following a construction in
[
13
]
, when two

(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules satisfy some condition on their
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology,
we will be able to construct
a space with a family of extensions of the two given
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules.
More precisely,
given two
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}
such that
H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\otimes D_{2}^{\vee})=0
, in
Section
4.1
we explain how to construct a vector bundle
X

over
\mathrm{Sp}(A)
with a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}
over
X
such that for any any rigid space
Y\xrightarrow{g}\mathrm{Sp}(A)

over
\mathrm{Sp}(A)
, we have a surjective map

\displaystyle\Psi_{Y}:X(Y)

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(g^{*}D_{2},g^{*}D_{1})(Y)

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto f^{*}\mathbb{D}

which is functorial in
Y
(see Theorem
4.2
).
It follows from this that if we are in the hypothesis of Theorem
A
, which means
that
H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{i})\otimes\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{j})^{\vee})=0

(i.e.
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)
) for all
i<j
, then there exists a vector bundle
X_{n}

over an appropriate neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{T}^{n}
of
\underline{\delta}

with a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{n}
specializing to
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
, as stated in Proposition
4.5
.
Since the regular locus is open and dense inside
\mathcal{T}^{n}
, we have that the pullback of such locus to
X_{n}

is open and dense, as
X_{n}
is simply a vector bundle over
\mathcal{U}

(in fact the preimage of a dense subspace is dense if the map is open).

The above is enough to conclude the argument in the hypothesis of Theorem
A
,
but things complicate a great deal when
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)
for at least one pair

(i,j)
with
i<j
.
Indeed in this case, we cannot use Theorem
4.2
:
in fact the fiber dimension of the second
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology is not locally constant,
hence we cannot find a family such that the base change for the first
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology
is surjective.
In order to get around this problem, we will make use of an equivalence of categories
which is an analogue for
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules of the Theorem of Beauville-Laszlo;
more precisely, the equivalence is between
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{A}
and the data consisting of pairs of

(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]
(where
t
is a particular element of
\mathcal{R}_{A}
)
and
\Gamma
-stable lattices of
A((t))
.
It should be emphasized that the above statement is a simplification (and false as stated) and the reader
should refer to Section
5.1

for the correct statement and a detailed explanation.
The main point in using this equivalence is that passing from

(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{A}
to
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]

annihilates the second
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology; thus it is possible to use Theorem
4.2

again in order to construct a rigid space
X_{n}^{\prime}
with a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}

specializing to
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)[1/t]
at some point
x^{\prime}\in X_{n}^{\prime}(L)
.
As a remark, this is not enough for our purpose, as we want a family of

(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules specializing to
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
.
We will then define the space
X_{n}
inside
X_{n}^{\prime}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{n}
(where
\mathrm{Gr}_{n}
is the affine Grassmannian)
consisting of pairs
(D^{\prime},\Lambda)
, where
D^{\prime}
is given
by the universal
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}
over
X_{n}^{\prime}
and
\Lambda\subset D^{\prime}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]}A((t))

is a
\Gamma
-stable lattice
(see Theorem
6.8
and Theorem
7.6
).
If we let
\mathbb{D}_{n}
be the sheaf on
X_{n}
corresponding to the pullback to
X_{n}
of the pair of
\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}
and
of the universal lattice over
\mathrm{Gr}_{n}
, we have that
\mathbb{D}_{n}
specializes to
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
at some point
x\in X_{n}(L)
,
by virtue of the equivalence written above.
We still need to show that the family
\mathbb{D}_{n}
is densely regular,
i.e. there exists a Zariski open dense of
X_{n}
such that the pullback of
\mathbb{D}_{n}

to such open has regular parameters.
Now the main issue is that
X_{n}
is not a vector bundle over an open
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathcal{T}^{n}
,
hence the pullback of the regular locus
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}
to
X_{n}
might not be dense in
X_{n}
:
indeed
X_{n}
is not even flat over
\mathcal{T}^{n}
, as we will see in Remark
6.11
.
In Section
6.2
and Section
7.2
,
we will study the geometry of the space
X_{n}
and give a bound to the dimension of the non-regular locus
inside
X_{n}
when
n\leqslant 4
or
|\{\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}\colon i<j\}|\leqslant 5

as in the hypothesis of Theorem
B
.
This will allow us to conclude that
X_{n}
is irreducible and that
the pullback of the regular locus
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}
is dense inside
X_{n}
, concluding the
proof of Theorem
B
.

Structure of the paper:

In section
1
we recollect basics and well-known results concerning
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules.
section
2
is devoted to defining trianguline representations and the trianguline variety.
The outline of the strategy we use to prove Theorem
A
and Theorem
B

is explained in section
3
.
In section
4
we first explain how to construct a family of extensions of two
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
(§
4.1
) and in §
4.2
we prove Theorem
A
.
The rest of the paper is dedicated to the proof of Theorem
B
:
section
5
provides all the tools that we will need in the course of the proof;
section
6
gives a proof of Theorem
B
when
n=2

and finally section
7
finishes the proof of Theorem
B
generalizing (when possible)
the results in section
6
.

1.
Preliminaries on
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules

One of the most powerful tools in the study of the theory of
p
-adic Galois representations
is the theory of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules, providing a tool of investigating such representations
in terms of semi-linear algebra objects.
The category of these modules (with some additional condition) is in fact equivalent
to the category of
p
-adic Galois representations, as will be more precisely stated in this section.
In this section we discuss some basic notions on
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules and their cohomology
as well as their variation in families.
Indeed, in
[
1
]
Berger and Colmez introduce the notion of families of overconvergent
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
associated to families of
p
-adic Galois representations.
The connection between these two categories will be better investigated in the second section.

From now on, let
p
be a prime number.
Let
L
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
\mathcal{O}_{L}
its ring of integers and
k_{L}
its residue field.

1.1.
The Robba ring

For
r=p^{-a},s=p^{-b}\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}
with
0\leqslant r\leqslant s<1
, let

\mathbb{B}^{[r,s]}=\{T:r\leqslant|T|\leqslant s\}=\mathrm{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle p^{-b}T,p^{a}T^{-1}\rangle,\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle p^{-b}T,p^{a}T^{-1}\rangle)

be the closed annulus of inner radius
r
and outer radius
s
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
Let
\mathcal{R}^{[r,s]}
denote the ring of analytic functions of
\mathbb{B}^{[r,s]}

and let
\mathcal{A}^{[r,s]}
denote the subring of functions whose valuation has maximal value 1.
For
0\leqslant r\leqslant s^{\prime}\leqslant s<1
, we have
\mathcal{R}^{[r,s]}\subset\mathcal{R}^{[r,s^{\prime}]}
simply defined by restriction.
We let

\mathcal{R}^{r}=\bigcap_{r\leqslant s<1}\mathcal{R}^{[r,s]}

be the ring of analytic functions converging on
the half-open annulus

\mathbb{B}^{r}=\{T:r\leqslant|T|<1\}

and
\mathcal{A}^{r}
the subring of
\mathcal{R}^{r}
consisting of functions whose valuation has maximal value 1.
As in
[
20
]
, for
r\leqslant s<1
, we can define the valuation

w_{s}\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{n}T^{n}\right):=\mathrm{min}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\{v_{p}(a_{n})+ns\};

then
\mathcal{R}^{r}
is complete with respect to the Fréchet topology defined by these valuations.

Definition 1.1
.

The
Robba ring over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is the ring

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{0\leqslant r<1}\mathcal{R}^{r}

\displaystyle=\left\{f(T)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{n}T^{n}\colon\begin{array}[]{l}a_{n}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p},a_{n}r(f)^{n}\xrightarrow[n\rightarrow-\infty]{}0\text{ for some }0\leqslant r(f)<1\\
\text{ and }a_{n}s^{n}\xrightarrow[n\rightarrow+\infty]{}0\text{ for all }r(f)\leqslant s<1\end{array}\right\}

of power series converging on some annulus.
We equip the Robba ring with the direct limit of the Fréchet topologies on
\mathcal{R}^{r}
.
Moreover let
\mathcal{A}^{\dagger}\coloneqq\bigcup_{0\leqslant r<1}\mathcal{A}^{r}

and equip it with the subspace topology.

We can extend the definition of the Robba ring in order to have coefficients in some affinoid
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra
A
, so we define

\begin{array}[]{lr}\mathcal{R}_{A}\coloneqq\mathcal{R}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A,&\mathcal{A}^{\dagger}_{A}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{\dagger}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A,\end{array}

where the completed tensor product is the completion of the ordinary tensor product
with respect to the
p
-adic topology on
A
and the topology on
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\dagger}
respectively.
For more precise definitions, we refer to
[
18
, §6.1]
.

We want to equip the Robba ring with a Frobenius and some action of
\Gamma:=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.
This will be useful later to define the semi-linear structure of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over the Robba ring.
We define the
A
-linear endomorphism

(1.1)

\phi:\mathcal{R}_{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{A},T\mapsto(1+T)^{p}-1

and an
A
-linear action

\Gamma\times\mathcal{R}_{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{A},(\gamma,T)\mapsto(1+t)^{\gamma}-1=\sum_{n\geqslant 1}\binom{\gamma}{n}T^{n}.

The two operators commute with each other and they are both continuous.

Moreover we set

t\coloneqq\mathrm{log}(1+T)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{n+1}\frac{T^{n}}{n}\in\mathcal{R}.

Then
\phi(t)=pt
and
\gamma\cdot t=\gamma t
for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
.

More generally, one can define the notion of
relative Robba ring
over some rigid space
X
in the following way:
for
r,s\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}
with
0\leqslant r\leqslant s<1
, let
\mathbb{B}_{X}^{[r,s]}=X\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{B}^{[r,s]}

and
\mathbb{B}_{X}^{r}=X\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{B}^{r}
; then define
\mathcal{R}_{X}^{[r,s]}=(\mathrm{pr}_{X})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{B}_{X}^{[r,s]}}

and
\mathcal{R}^{r}_{X}=(\mathrm{pr}_{X})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{B}_{X}^{r}}
. The relative Robba ring over
X
is the sheaf

\mathcal{R}_{X}\coloneqq\lim_{r\rightarrow 1}\mathcal{R}^{r}_{X}.

The sheaf
\mathcal{R}_{X}
is endowed with a continuous
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear endomorphism
\phi:\mathcal{R}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{X}

and a continuous
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear action
\Gamma\times\mathcal{R}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{X}
.
In the same way we can define
\mathcal{A}_{X}^{[r,s]}=(\mathrm{pr}_{X})_{*}\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathbb{B}_{X}^{[r,s]}}

and
\mathcal{A}^{r}_{X}=(\mathrm{pr}_{X})_{*}\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathbb{B}_{X}^{r}}
. Finally, let

\mathcal{A}^{\dagger}_{X}\coloneqq\lim_{r\rightarrow 1}\mathcal{A}^{r}_{X}.

1.2.
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules

Now we introduce the notion of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules as in
[
11
]
.

Definition 1.2
.

Let
L
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

(i)

A
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{L}
is a locally free
\mathcal{R}_{L}
-module
D
of finite presentation endowed with

•

a
\mathcal{R}_{L}
-semilinear endomorphism
\Phi:D\rightarrow D
such that
\mathcal{R}_{L}\cdot\Phi(D)=D
;

•

a continuous
\mathcal{R}_{L}
-semilinear

\Gamma
-action commuting with
\Phi
.

(ii)

A
morphism of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
is a morphism of
\mathcal{R}_{L}
-modules respecting the actions of
\Gamma
and
\Phi
.

Remark 1.3
.

Let us explain in more detail what we mean by
continuous
action of
\Gamma
on
D
in the above definition.
We ask that there exists an
\mathcal{R}_{L}
-basis
\mathcal{E}=\{e_{1},\dots,e_{d}\}
of
D
and
r\in[0,1]
such that the map

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Mat}_{d}(\mathcal{R}_{L})

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{Mat}_{\mathcal{E}}(\gamma)

has values in
\mathrm{Mat}_{d}(\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r})
and is continuous.

The motivation behind the study of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules is the following well-known Theorem
due to Fontaine, Cherbonnier, Colmez and Berger.

Theorem 1.4
.

There is a fully faithful functor

\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}:\{L\text{-linear continuous representations of }G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\}\rightarrow\{(\phi,\Gamma)\text{-modules over }\mathcal{R}_{L}\}

commuting with base change.

For a reference look at
[
5
, Theorem 2.2.2]
.

Definition 1.5
.

The
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules in the essential image of the funtor
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}
are called
étale
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
.

The consequence of Theorem
1.4
is that the functor

(1.2)

\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}L\text{-linear continuous}\\
\text{representations of }G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\end{array}\right\}\rightarrow\{\text{\'{e}tale }(\phi,\Gamma)\text{-modules over }\mathcal{R}_{L}\}

is an equivalence of categories.
Let us remark that the above functor is not an equivalence when we work with families of Galois representations
and families of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules because it is not essentially surjective, as we will see in §
2.1
.

As mentioned before, the theory of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules is compatible with its variation in analytic families.

Definition 1.6
.

Let
X
be a rigid space.
A
family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
X
is an
\mathcal{R}_{X}
-module

\mathbb{D}
which is locally on
X
finite projective over
\mathcal{R}_{X}
with

•

a
\mathcal{R}_{X}
-semilinear endomorphism
\Phi:\mathbb{D}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}

such that
\Phi:\phi^{*}\mathbb{D}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}
is an isomorphism;

•

a continuous
\mathcal{R}_{X}
-semilinear
\Gamma
-action commuting with
\Phi
.

A family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}
over
X
is said to be
étale
if there exists
a covering
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
X
and free
\mathcal{A}_{U_{i}}^{\dagger}
-submodules

D_{i}
of
\mathbb{D}_{|U_{i}}
such that

D_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{U_{i}}^{\dagger}}\mathcal{R}_{U_{i}}=\mathbb{D}_{|U_{i}}

and
\Phi
induces an isomorphism
\phi^{*}D_{i}\rightarrow D_{i}
.

In
[
18
, Corollary 6.11]
it is proved that étaleness is an
open condition and that it can be checked fiber-wise. More precisely:

Remark 1.7
.

(i)

Given a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}
over a rigid space
X
, the subset of
X

where the specialization of
\mathbb{D}
is étale is open.

(ii)

A family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over a rigid space
X
is étale if and only if
all fibers are étale.

1.3.
The
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology

The
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology was first introduced by Herr in order to compute the Galois
cohomology of
p
-adic Galois representations on the side of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules and it is
a very useful tool in the study of these objects.

Definition 1.8
.

Let
X
be a rigid space and let
D
be a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
X
.

•

If
p\neq 2
, the
Herr complex
of
D
is the complex of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules

0\rightarrow D\xrightarrow{(\Phi-1,\gamma-1)}D\oplus D\xrightarrow{(\gamma-1)\oplus(1-\Phi)}D\rightarrow 0,

where
\gamma
is a topological generator of
\Gamma
.

•

If
p=2
, the
Herr complex
of
D
is the complex of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules

0\rightarrow D^{\Delta}\xrightarrow{(\Phi-1,\gamma-1)}D^{\Delta}\oplus D^{\Delta}\xrightarrow{(\gamma-1)\oplus(1-\Phi)}D^{\Delta}\rightarrow 0,

where
\Delta\subset\Gamma
is the torsion subgroup and
\gamma
is a topological generator of
\Gamma/\Delta
.

We will refer to the Herr complex of
D
as
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(D)
.
Moreover we denote by
H^{\bullet}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)
the cohomology of the Herr complex,
called the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology
of
D
.

Proposition 1.9
.

Let
X
be a rigid space and
D
a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
X
.
The
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
H^{i}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)
are coherent for all
i=0,1,2
.

The proof of this Proposition can be found in
[
22
, Theorem 4.4.2]
.

One of the most important properties of the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology is that it computes extensions:

Lemma 1.10
.

Let
X
be a rigid space and let
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
be two families of

(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
X
. Then for
i=0,1
there are natural isomorphisms

H^{i}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{2}^{\vee}\otimes D_{1})\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{i}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{2},D_{1}).

Proof.

Notice that

H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{2}^{\vee}\otimes D_{1})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{X},D_{2}^{\vee}\otimes D_{1})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{2},D_{1})=\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{2},D_{1}).

As for
i=1
, we refer to
[
11
, Lemme 2.2]
.
∎

It is possible to classify all the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules of rank 1 in a very nice way.
Let
\mathcal{T}
be the rigid space representing the functor

\displaystyle\{\text{rigid analytic spaces over }\mathbb{Q}_{p}\}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\underline{Sets}

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times},\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times})).

We also denote by
\mathcal{W}
the rigid space representing the functor

\displaystyle\{\text{rigid analytic spaces over }\mathbb{Q}_{p}\}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\underline{Sets}

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times})).

The representability of the above functors is proved in
[
22
, Proposition 6.1.1]
.
There is a natural projection
\mathcal{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}
given by the restriction of a character to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
;
this map has a section, identifying
\mathcal{T}
with

\mathcal{W}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{an}}
via
\delta\mapsto(\delta_{|\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}},\delta(p))
.

Definition 1.11
.

Let
\delta\in\mathcal{T}(X)
for some rigid space
X
.

•

We define
\mathcal{R}_{X}(\delta)
to be the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module of rank 1 over
\mathcal{R}_{X}
with basis
e

such that
\Phi(e)=\delta(p)e
and
\gamma\cdot e=\delta(\gamma)e
for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
.

•

If
X
is an affinoid
L
-rigid space
\mathrm{Sp}(A)
, then we define the
weight of
\delta

following
[
22
, Notation 5.3.2.]
as the element

\mathrm{wt}(\delta)\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{log}(\delta(a))}{\mathrm{log}(a)}\in A

for non-torsion
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
close enough to 1.

The following Theorem gives a classification of all
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules of rank 1 (up to twist by a unique line bundle).

Theorem 1.12
.

Let
D
be a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module of rank 1 over
\mathcal{R}_{X}
for some rigid space
X
.
We have that
D\cong\mathcal{R}_{X}(\delta)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D(\delta^{-1}))

for a uniquely determined
\delta\in\mathcal{T}(X)
.
Moreover we have that
H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D(\delta^{-1}))
is a line bundle over
X
.

Proof.

For the proof look
[
22
, Construction 6.2.4, Theorem 6.2.14]
.
∎

The
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules of rank 1 does not behave always in the same way.
In the following we partition the space
\mathcal{T}
keeping in mind this behaviour.
Let
x
be the identity character and
\chi=x|x|
the cyclotomic character in
\mathcal{T}
.
Let us define the following subspaces of
\mathcal{T}
:

(1.3)

\begin{split}\mathcal{T}^{+}&\coloneqq\{\chi x^{n}\colon n\in\mathbb{N}\}\\
\mathcal{T}^{-}&\coloneqq\{x^{-n}\colon n\in\mathbb{N}\}\\
\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}&\coloneqq\mathcal{T}\setminus(\mathcal{T}^{+}\cup\mathcal{T}^{-}).\end{split}

The subsets
\mathcal{T}^{+}
,
\mathcal{T}^{-}
and
\{x^{n}\colon n\in\mathbb{Z}\}
are closed in
\mathcal{T}
,
making
\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}
open.
Moreover
\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}
is dense in
\mathcal{T}
.

Definition 1.13
.

We say that a character
\delta\in\mathcal{T}
is
regular
if
\delta\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}
.

Theorem 1.14
.

Let
\delta\in\mathcal{T}(L)
.
We have that

(i)

\mathrm{dim}_{L}H^{1}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta))=\begin{cases}1\text{ if }\delta\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}(L)\\
2\text{ if }\delta\in(\mathcal{T}^{+}\cup\mathcal{T}^{-})(L)\end{cases}

(ii)

\mathrm{dim}_{L}H^{0}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta))=\begin{cases}0\text{ if }\delta\in(\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{-})(L)\\
1\text{ if }\delta\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(L)\end{cases}

(iii)

\mathrm{dim}_{L}H^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta))=\begin{cases}0\text{ if }\delta\in(\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{+})(L)\\
1\text{ if }\delta\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)\end{cases}

For a proof see
[
12
, Proposition 2.1, Theorem 2.9]
and
[
23
, Corollary 2.13]

More generally, we have:

Theorem 1.15
.

Let
A
be an affinoid
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra and let
\delta\in\mathcal{T}(A)
.

(i)

H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{A}(\delta))\neq 0
if and only if there exists
i\in\mathbb{N}
and
0\neq a\in A

such that
a\cdot(\delta-x^{i})
is the constant zero function on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
.

(ii)

H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{A}(\delta))\neq 0
if and only if the closed subspace of
\mathrm{Sp}(A)

given by the equation
\delta=\chi x^{i}
is non-empty for some
i\in\mathbb{N}
.

(iii)

If
\delta\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}(A)
, then
H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{A}(\delta))=H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{A}(\delta))=0

and
H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{A}(\delta))
is free of rank 1 over
A
.

The reader can find the proof of this Theorem in
[
11
, Corollaire 2.11, Théorème 2.29]
.

Let us moreover define

(1.4)

\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}\coloneqq\{(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}\text{ for all }i<j\}\subset\mathcal{T}^{n},

which is an open dense subspace of
\mathcal{T}^{n}
and

\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}\coloneqq\{(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{W}^{n}\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\notin x^{\mathbb{Z}}\text{ for all }i<j\}\subset\mathcal{W}^{n},

which is a dense subspace of
\mathcal{W}^{n}
.

Lemma 1.16
.

For every
\underline{\delta}\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(K)
(for some extesion
K
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
), there exists
an open neighbourhood
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\subset\mathcal{T}^{n}
of
\underline{\delta}
such
that the following properties are satisfied.
Let
(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{n})
be the characters
corresponding to the open
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}
.
For all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n

•

for all
u\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}
, we have

\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}\widehat{\otimes}k(u)\notin\{x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\}
,
unless
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=x^{a_{ij}}
for some
a_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
;
in this case, we have
\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}\widehat{\otimes}k(u)\in\{x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\}

only if
\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}\widehat{\otimes}k(u)=x^{a_{ij}}
.

•

for all
u\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}
, we have

\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}\widehat{\otimes}k(u)\notin\{\chi x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\}
,
unless
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=\chi x^{b_{ij}}
for some
b_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
;
in this case, we have
\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}\widehat{\otimes}k(u)\in\{\chi x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\}

only if
\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}\widehat{\otimes}k(u)=\chi x^{b_{ij}}
.

Proof.

For all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n
, we will define a neighbourhood

\mathcal{V}_{ij}\subset\mathcal{T}^{n}

of
\underline{\delta}
in the following way:
let

\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}:\mathcal{T}^{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}

be the map sending
(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(A)

(for some affinoid algebra
A
) to
\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}
.

•

If
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\notin\{x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\}\cup\{\chi x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\}
, then we define

\mathcal{V}_{ij}\coloneqq(\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}\setminus(\{x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\}\cup\{\chi x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\})),

which is open, since
\mathcal{T}\setminus(\{x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\}\cup\{\chi x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\})
is.

•

If instead
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=x^{a_{ij}}
for some
a_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
, then we define

\mathcal{V}_{ij}\coloneqq(\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}\setminus(\{x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{a_{ij}\}\}\cup\{\chi x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\})).

•

Finally, if
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=\chi x^{b_{ij}}
for some
b_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
, then we define

\mathcal{V}_{ij}\coloneqq(\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}\setminus(\{x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\}\cup\{\chi x^{m}\colon m\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{b_{ij}\}\})).

Finally, the open
\bigcap_{(1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n)}\mathcal{V}_{ij}

is a neighbourhood of
\underline{\delta}
and satisfies the wanted properties.
∎

In what follows we state some well-known properties of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology.

The following Theorem is proved in
[
23
, Theorem 0.2]
.

Theorem 1.17
.

Let
D
be a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{L}
. Then

\sum_{i=0}^{2}(-1)^{i}\dim_{L}H^{i}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)=\mathrm{rk}(D).

Theorem 1.18
.

Let
A
be an affinoid
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra and let
D
be a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathrm{Sp}(A)
.
The Herr complex of
D
is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded complex of projective
A
-modules

M^{0}\rightarrow M^{1}\rightarrow M^{2}

that universally computes the cohomology of the Herr complex,
i.e. for all affinoid
A
-algebras
B
, the complex
M^{\bullet}\otimes_{A}B

is quasi-isomorphic to
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(D\widehat{\otimes}_{A}B)
.

This result follows from
[
22
, Theorem 4.4.3,4.4.5]
.

2.
Trianguline representations

Trianguline representations are a certain class of
p
-adic Galois representations,
just like crystalline, semistable and de Rham representations.
They were introduced for the first time by Colmez in order
to study the functoriality of the
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence for
GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

2.1.
Definitions and basic properties

Definition 2.1
.

A Galois representation
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(L)
is a
trianguline representation
if
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
is
triangulable
,
which means that there exists a complete filtration

0\subset\mathrm{Fil}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho))\subset\dots\subset\mathrm{Fil}^{n}(\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho))=\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)

of sub-
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules of
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
such that for all
i

\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho))/\mathrm{Fil}^{i-1}(\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho))\cong\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{i})

for some
\delta_{i}\in\mathcal{T}(L)
.
We say that
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})
are the
parameters
of the triangulation

\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho))
of
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
and they are said to be
regular
if

(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}(L)
.

Analogously to the theory of
families
of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules, there is also a
theory of
families
of
p
-adic Galois representations.

Definition 2.2
.

Let
X
be a rigid space.
A
family of Galois representations over
X
is a vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
over
X

endowed with a continuous
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-action.

Theorem 2.3
.

There is a fully faithful and exact functor

\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\text{families of Galois}\\
\text{represetations over }X\end{array}\right\}\rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\text{families of \'{e}tale }\\
(\phi,\Gamma)\text{-modules over }X\end{array}\right\}

commuting with base change.

For a reference of the above Theorem, look at
[
22
, Theorem 2.2.17.]
.

2.2.
The trianguline variety

In the following, let
k_{L}
be the residue field of
L
and
fix a continuous Galois representation
\bar{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(k_{L})
.

Definition 2.4
.

•

Let
A
be a complete local Artinian ring with residue field
k_{L}
.
We say that a continuous representation
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(A)
is
a
deformation
of
\bar{\rho}
if the composition of
\rho
with the induced map

GL_{n}(A)\rightarrow GL_{n}(k_{L})
is exactly
\bar{\rho}
.

•

We say that two deformations
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(A)
are
equivalent

if
\rho_{1}=M\rho_{2}M^{-1}
for some matrix
M\in\mathrm{ker}(GL_{n}(A)\rightarrow GL_{n}(k_{L}))
.

The functor that associates to a complete local Artinian ring
A
the set of
deformations of
\bar{\rho}
is pro-representable by a
complete local Noetherian
W(k_{L})
-algebra
R_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}
, called the

universal framed deformation ring of
\bar{\rho}
.
This was proved by Mazur in
[
24
]
.
We let
\mathfrak{X}_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}=(\mathrm{Spf}R_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square})^{\mathrm{rig}}

be the rigid space associated to the formal scheme
\mathrm{Spf}(R_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square})
.
We have that
\mathfrak{X}_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}
represents the functor which associates
to an affinoid rigid space
\mathrm{Sp}(A)
over
W(k_{L})[1/p]
the set of
deformations
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(A^{+})
of
\bar{\rho}
, where
A^{+}
denotes
the subring of power-bounded elements of
A
(in particular it is a
W(k_{L})
-algebra).

Definition 2.5
.

The
trianguline variety

X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})
is the rigid analytic space
over
L
defined as the Zariski-closure of the rigid space

U_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho}):=\{(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}\colon\rho\text{ is trianguline of parameters }\underline{\delta}\}

inside
\mathfrak{X}_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}^{n}
.

This space was defined and studied in
[
7
]
.
In particular we recall the following results.

Theorem 2.6
.

(i)

The space
X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})
is equidimensional of dimension
n^{2}+\penalty\ \frac{n(n+1)}{2}
;

(ii)

the subset
U_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})
is Zariski-open and Zariski-dense in
X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})

(iii)

if
(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})
, then
\rho
is trianguline.

It is then natural to ask ourselves whether all trianguline representations "are
in" the trianguline variety.
This is the question that motivates this thesis.

3.
Strategy

First we show that, given a Galois
representation on a rigid space, its reduction to the residue field of the ring of
integers of a point is locally constant.
Recall that, given an affinoid ring
A
, we denote by
A^{+}
the subring of power-bounded
elements and by
A^{++}\subset A^{+}
the ideal of topologically nilpotent elements.

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
L
and let
\mathcal{V}
be a family of
Galois representations over
X
, so that we have
\widetilde{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL(\mathcal{V})
.
Let
x\in X(L)
and let
\rho
be the reduction of
\widetilde{\rho}
to the point
x
.
After eventually shrinking
X
, we can choose a basis of
\mathcal{V}
such that

\rho
has values in
GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
.
Let
\bar{\rho}
be the reduction of
\rho
to the residue field
k_{L}
of
L
.
Then there is an open neighbourhood
U=\mathrm{Sp}(A)\xhookrightarrow{j}X
of
x
such that (with the
basis chosen above) we have
\widetilde{\rho}_{U}:=j^{*}\widetilde{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(A^{+})
.
Moreover
\widetilde{\rho}_{U}
reduces to
\bar{\rho}
, which means that there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{n}(A^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{n}(k_{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{n}(A^{+}/A^{++}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\rho}_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}}

where the lower horizontal arrow is induced by the inclusion
k_{L}\hookrightarrow A^{+}/A^{++}
, since
A
is an
L
-algebra.

Remark 3.2
.

Let us remark that the representation
\overline{\rho}
depends on the choice
of a basis for the vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
; however the isomorphism class of
the semisimplification of
\overline{\rho}
is well-defined independently of the chosen basis,
as showed in
[
16
, Proposition 5.11]
.

Proof.

First of all we show that there is an affinoid neighbourhood

V\coloneqq\mathrm{Sp}(B)\xhookrightarrow{i}X
of
x
such that

\widetilde{\rho}_{V}\coloneqq i^{*}\widetilde{\rho}
has values in
GL_{n}(B^{+})
with respect
to the basis of
\mathcal{V}
fixed above.
By
[
19
]
we know that
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is topologically finitely generated, hence
let
\{g_{1},\dots,g_{t}\}
be a set of topological generators of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.
For every
k=1,\dots,t
, let
a_{ij}^{k}
be the
(i,j)
-th entry of the matrix

\widetilde{\rho}(g_{k})
and
d^{k}\coloneqq\mathrm{det}(\widetilde{\rho}(g_{k}))
.
We then have that
a_{ij}^{k}(x)\in\mathcal{O}_{L}
, so
|a_{ij}^{k}(x)|\leqslant 1

and
|d^{k}(x)|=1
because
d^{k}(x)\in\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}
.
Let us define the affinoid open neighbourhoods
V_{ij}^{k}\coloneqq\{y\in X:|a_{ij}^{k}(y)|\leqslant 1\}

and
V_{d}^{k}\coloneqq\{y\in X:|d^{k}(y)|=1\}
of
x
, so that we let

V=\mathrm{Sp}(C)\coloneqq\bigcap_{i,j=1,\dots,n\atop k=1,\dots,t}V_{ij}^{k}\cap V_{d}^{k}.

In particular for all
k=1,\dots,t
and
i,j=1\dots,n
, we have

|a_{ij}^{k}|=|a_{ij}^{k}(y)|\leqslant 1
and
|d^{k}|=|d^{k}(y^{\prime})|=1
for some
y,y^{\prime}\in V

thanks to the Maximum Principle, so
a_{ij}^{k}\in B^{+}
and
d^{k}\in(B^{+})^{\times}
.
This means that
i^{*}\widetilde{\rho}(g_{k})\in GL_{n}(B^{+})
for all
k=1,\dots,t
.
Now we show that
\widetilde{\rho}_{V}\coloneqq i^{*}\widetilde{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(B^{+})
.
Let
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and observe that for any open neighbourhood
Z
of
\widetilde{\rho}_{V}(g)\in GL_{n}(B)
,
there exists
h\in\langle g_{1},\dots,g_{t}\rangle
such that
\widetilde{\rho}_{V}(h)\in Z
.
So there is a sequence
(\widetilde{\rho}_{V}(h_{k}))_{k}
converging to
\widetilde{\rho}_{V}(g)
,
where
(h_{k})_{k}\subset\langle g_{1},\dots,g_{t}\rangle
.
Let
b_{ij}
be the
(i,j)
-th entry of
\widetilde{\rho}_{V}(g)
and
b_{ij,k}
the

(i,j)
-th entry of
\widetilde{\rho}_{V}(h_{k})
.
For all
i,j=1,\dots,n
we have a sequence
(b_{ij,k})_{k}\subset B^{+}
converging
to
b_{ij}
, so
b_{ij}\in B^{+}
.
In the same way, we get a sequence
(d_{k})_{k}\subset(B^{+})^{\times}
converging to

\mathrm{det}(\widetilde{\rho}_{V}(g))
, which allows us to conclude that
\widetilde{\rho}_{V}(g)\in GL_{n}(B^{+})
.
Finally it is left to show that
\widetilde{\rho}_{V}
reduces to
\overline{\rho}
modulo

B^{++}
, after eventually shrinking
V
.
Let
f_{ij}^{k}\coloneqq a_{ij}^{k}-a_{ij}^{k}(x)\in B^{+}
; fix
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and
consider
U_{ij}^{k}\coloneqq\{y\in\mathrm{Sp}(B):|f_{ij}^{k}(y)|\leqslant\epsilon\}

for all
i,j=1\dots,n
and
k=1,\dots,t
. We have
x\in U_{ij}^{k}
because
f_{ij}^{k}(x)=0
, so

U\coloneqq\mathrm{Sp}(A)\coloneqq\bigcap_{i,j=1,\dots,n\atop k=1,\dots,t}U_{ij}^{k}

is an affinoid open neighbourhood of
x
. We then have
\widetilde{\rho}_{V}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(A^{+})

and
|f_{ij}^{k}|=|f_{ij}^{k}(y)|\leqslant\epsilon<1
for some
y\in\mathrm{Sp}(A)
,
thus
f_{ij}^{k}\in A^{++}
for all
i,j=1,\dots,n
and
k=1,\dots,t
by the same
approximation argument as before.
In a very similar way to the one used above, it is possible to conclude that

\widetilde{\rho}_{U}(g)\equiv\rho(g)\text{ mod }A^{++}
for all
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.
∎

In what follows we will outline a strategy that we will use in order to show that
trianguline representations appear as points of the trianguline variety.
The underlying idea for what follows is that, given a trianguline representation
\rho
,
we want to construct a rigid space
X
and a family of trianguline
Galois representations on
X
reducing to
\rho
at some pont
x\in X
and having regular
parameters in a dense subset of a neighbourhood of
x
.
In this way, we can show that
\rho
is in the closure of a regular family, hence
in the trianguline variety.

The following Theorem makes the above idea more precise in terms of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules.

Theorem 3.3
.

Let
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
be a trianguline representation
with parameters
\underline{\delta}\coloneqq(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)

and let
\bar{\rho}
be the reduction of
\rho
to the residue field of
L
:

\bar{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\xrightarrow{\rho}GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\twoheadrightarrow GL_{n}(k_{L}).

Assume that there exist

•

a rigid space
X
over
L
;

•

a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}
over
X
;

•

a map
(\widetilde{\delta_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\delta_{n}}):X\rightarrow\mathcal{T}^{n}

such that there are

(1)

a point
x\in X(L)
such that
\underline{\widetilde{\delta}}\widehat{\otimes}k(x)=\underline{\delta}

and
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\otimes}k(x)=\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
;

(2)

a Zariski-open dense subset
U\xhookrightarrow{j}X
such that

j^{*}\mathbb{D}
has a filtration with graded pieces
\mathcal{R}_{U}(j^{*}\widetilde{\delta_{i}})

and
(j^{*}\widetilde{\delta_{1}},\dots,j^{*}\widetilde{\delta_{n}})\in\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}(U)
.

Then
(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})(L)
.

Proof.

By
[
21
, Theorem 0.2]
, there exists a neighbourhood

V=\mathrm{Sp}(B)\xhookrightarrow{i}X
of
x
and a family of representations on
V

\widetilde{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(B^{+})

such that
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{\dagger}(\widetilde{\rho})=i^{*}\mathbb{D}
.
By Proposition
3.1
, eventually shrinking
V
, we can
choose a basis of
\mathcal{V}
such that the following diagram commutes:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{n}(B^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{n}(k_{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{n}(B^{+}/B^{++}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}}

Thus
\widetilde{\rho}
induces a morphism

\psi\coloneqq(\widetilde{\rho},i^{*}\widetilde{\underline{\delta}}):V\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}^{\square}_{\bar{\rho}}\times\mathcal{T}^{n}.

We now show that
\psi_{|U\cap V}
factors through
U_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})^{\mathrm{reg}}
:
first of all let
u:U\cap V\hookrightarrow V
and notice that it is sufficient to prove
that the family of representations
u^{*}\widetilde{\rho}
is trianguline with regular
parameters
(i\circ u)^{*}\underline{\widetilde{\delta}}
.
By hypothesis and by Theorem
2.3
,

\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(u^{*}\widetilde{\rho})=u^{*}\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\widetilde{\rho})\cong(i\circ u)^{*}\mathbb{D}
.
Hence
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(u^{*}\widetilde{\rho})
has parameters

(i\circ u)^{*}\underline{\widetilde{\delta}}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{n}(U\cap V)
.
Thus
\psi(U\cap V)\subset U_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})
.
Notice that
U_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})\cap\mathrm{im}(\psi)
is dense in
\mathrm{im}(\psi)
:
let
W
be an open in
\mathrm{im}(\psi)
, so that
\psi^{-1}(W)
is open in
V
.
Then
\psi^{-1}(W)\cap U\neq\emptyset
, which implies that
W\cap U_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})\neq\emptyset
.
Thanks to the density of
U_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})\cap\mathrm{im}(\psi)
in
\mathrm{im}(\psi)
,
we can conclude that

\mathrm{im}(\psi)\subset\overline{\mathrm{im}(\psi)}=\overline{U_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\bar{\rho})\cap\mathrm{im}(\psi)}\subset X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}).

In particular,
\psi(x)=(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})
.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(L)
be a representation and let
\underline{\delta}\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
.
Let us assume that there is a finite extension
L\xhookrightarrow{i}L^{\prime}
such that
(\rho^{\prime},\underline{\delta}^{\prime})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})(L^{\prime})
,
where
\rho^{\prime}\coloneqq i^{*}\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\xrightarrow{\rho}GL_{n}(L)\hookrightarrow GL_{n}(L^{\prime})
and

\underline{\delta}^{\prime}\coloneqq i^{*}\underline{\delta}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\underline{\delta}}(L^{\times})^{n}\hookrightarrow(L^{\prime\times})^{n}
.
Then
(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})(L)
.

Proof.

We have that the point
(\rho^{\prime},\underline{\delta}^{\prime})\in(\mathfrak{X}_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}^{n})(L^{\prime})

corresponds by construction to the map

(\rho^{\prime},\underline{\delta}^{\prime}):\mathrm{Sp}(L^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}(L)\xrightarrow{(\rho,\underline{\delta})}\mathfrak{X}_{\bar{\rho}}^{\square}\times\mathcal{T}^{n}.

The fact that
(\rho^{\prime},\underline{\delta}^{\prime})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})(L^{\prime})
implies that
\mathrm{im}(\rho^{\prime},\underline{\delta}^{\prime})\subset X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})
;
hence in particular we can deduce that
\mathrm{im}(\rho,\underline{\delta})\subset X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})
.
This means
(\rho,\underline{\delta})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})(L)
.
∎

4.
Trianguline representations with no
\mathcal{T}^{+}
-type irregularity

In this section we prove Theorem
A
, which states that any trianguline representation whose parameters
don’t admit quotients in
\mathcal{T}^{+}
are in the trianguline variety.
More precisely, we show the following:

See
A

4.1.
Constructing extensions

We want to use Theorem
3.3
to prove Theorem
A
.
In order to do so, it is essential to construct a rigid space
X
of extensions
of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules.
In this section we will show how to do so in the case the second
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology of
these
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules is a locally free sheaf.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
D
be a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over an affinoid ring
A
and let
B
be an affinoid
A
-algebra.
If
H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)
is a locally free
A
-module,
then

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)\otimes_{A}B\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D\widehat{\otimes}_{A}B).

Proof.

By Theorem
1.18
, there is a complex of projective
A
-modules

M^{0}\xrightarrow{d^{0}}M^{1}\xrightarrow{d^{1}}M^{2}

universally computing the cohomology of the Herr complex of
D
.
This means that
H^{1}(D\widehat{\otimes}_{A}B)\cong H^{1}(M^{\bullet}\otimes_{A}B)
for any
A
-algebra
B
.
Being
H^{2}(M^{\bullet})
locally free, we have
M^{2}\cong H^{2}(M^{\bullet})\oplus\mathrm{im}(d^{1})
.
Since both
M^{2}
and
H^{2}(M^{\bullet})
are projective modules, we can conclude
\mathrm{im}(d^{1})
is as well,
implying that
M^{1}
also splits as the direct sum of
\mathrm{im}(d^{1})
and
\mathrm{ker}(d^{1})
.
Again we can deduce that
Z^{1}:=\mathrm{ker}(d^{1})
is a projective
A
-module.
Now notice that the complex

M^{0}\xrightarrow{d^{0}}Z^{1}

universally computes
H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)
and
H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)
.
Moreover

M^{0}\xrightarrow{d^{0}}Z^{1}\rightarrow H^{1}(M^{\bullet})\rightarrow 0

is a projective resolution of
H^{1}(M^{\bullet})
.
Therefore

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)\otimes_{A}B

\displaystyle\cong H^{1}(M^{\bullet})\otimes_{A}B\cong\mathrm{Tor}_{0}(H^{1}(M^{\bullet}),B)\cong(Z^{1}\otimes_{A}B)/\mathrm{im}(d^{0}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{B})

\displaystyle\cong H^{1}(M^{\bullet}\otimes_{A}B)\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D\widehat{\otimes}_{A}B).

∎

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
D_{1},D_{2}
be two families of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over some rigid analytic affinoid space
\mathrm{Sp}(A)
.
Assume that
H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}}D_{2}^{\vee})
is locally free.
There exists a vector bundle
X
over
\mathrm{Sp}(A)
(of dimension equal to the minimal
number of generators of the module
H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}}D_{2}^{\vee})
over
A
)
and a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules

\mathbb{D}
over
X
such that for any rigid space
Y\xrightarrow{g}\mathrm{Sp}(A)

over
\mathrm{Sp}(A)
, we have a surjective map

\displaystyle\Psi_{Y}:X(Y)

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(g^{*}D_{2},g^{*}D_{1})(Y)

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto f^{*}\mathbb{D}

which is functorial in
Y
.
We moreover can choose
X
such that the above map
\Psi_{Y}
is bijective for all rigid spaces

Y\xrightarrow{g}\mathrm{Sp}(A)
if the module
H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}}D_{2}^{\vee})

is free over
A
.

Remark 4.3
.

In
[
13
, §5.4]
Emerton and Gee explain how to construct a family
of extensions of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules with coefficients in characteristic
p
in case
the second
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology has constant fiber rank.
In fact Theorem
4.2
is the analogue in characteristic 0 of that construction.

Proof.

By Proposition
1.9
, we know
H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{A}D_{2}^{\vee})

is a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sp}(A)}
-module.
Let
n
be the minimal number of generators of
H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{A}D_{2}^{\vee})

and let
F
be a finitely generated free
A
-module of rank
n
; in particular, we have a
surjective map of
A
-modules

F\twoheadrightarrow H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{A}D_{2}^{\vee}).

We define the rigid space

X\coloneqq\mathrm{Sp}(A)\times\mathbb{A}^{n,\mathrm{an}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\xrightarrow{h}\mathrm{Sp}(A),

which is a vector bundle over
\mathrm{Sp}(A)
.
Let
Y\to X
be a map of rigid spaces over
\mathrm{Sp}(A)

and let
g
be the map
Y\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}(A)
. Let us study the space
X(Y)
:

\displaystyle X(Y)=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sp}(A)}(Y,X)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sp}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(Y,\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{n,\mathrm{an}}).

For any rigid
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-space
Y
there is a bijection

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sp}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(Y,\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{n,\mathrm{an}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sp}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(Y,\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{n})\cong\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})^{n}

between the set of rigid morphisms
Y\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{n,\mathrm{an}}

and the set of morphisms between locally
G
-ringed spaces
Y\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{n}
.
We then have the map

\displaystyle X(Y)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sp}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(Y,\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{n,\mathrm{an}})\cong\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})^{n}

\displaystyle\cong\Gamma(Y,g^{*}F)\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma(Y,g^{*}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}}D_{2}^{\vee})).

Since
H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}}D_{2}^{\vee})
is locally free by hypothesis,
we get as a consequence that

g^{*}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}}D_{2}^{\vee})\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(g^{*}(D_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}}D_{2}^{\vee}))

by Theorem
4.1
.
Finally, we have

\displaystyle\Psi_{Y}:X(Y)\twoheadrightarrow g^{*}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}}D_{2}^{\vee})(Y)

\displaystyle\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(g^{*}(D_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}}D_{2}^{\vee}))(Y)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(g^{*}D_{2},g^{*}D_{1})(Y).

Let us now define the universal family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
X
.
We know we have a surjective map

\displaystyle\Psi_{X}:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sp}(A)}(X,X)\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(h^{*}D_{2},h^{*}D_{1})(X).

Let
\mathbb{D}\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(h^{*}D_{2},h^{*}D_{1})
be the image of

\mathrm{id}_{X}
through
\Psi_{X}
.
By the Yoneda Lemma it follows immediately that
\mathbb{D}
satisfies the universal property specified above.
In case the module
H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}}D_{2}^{\vee})
is free over
A
,
it is sufficient to take the module
F
exactly equal to
H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}}D_{2}^{\vee})
;
it is then obvious that all the above maps composing
\Psi_{Y}
are isomorphisms.
∎

4.2.
Proof of Theorem
A

In order to prove Theorem
A
, we proceed by induction; hence let us first prove the
2-dimensional case.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\in\mathcal{T}^{2}(L)
such that
\delta_{1}\delta_{2}^{-1}\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)

and let
D\in H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}))
.
Then there exist

•

a rigid space
X
over
L
which is also a vector bundle of dimension

\dim_{L}(H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{1}/\delta_{2})))

over an appropriate
neighbourhood of
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
in
\mathcal{T}^{2}
;

•

a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}
over
X
;

•

a map
(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\widetilde{\delta}_{2}):X\rightarrow\mathcal{T}^{2}

such that there are

(1)

x\in X
such that
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\otimes}k(x)=D
and

(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\widetilde{\delta}_{2})\widehat{\otimes}k(x)=(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
;

(2)

a Zariski-open dense subset
U\xhookrightarrow{j}X
such that
j^{*}\mathbb{D}

has a filtration of sub-
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules with graded pieces
\mathcal{R}_{U}(j^{*}\widetilde{\delta}_{1})

and
\mathcal{R}_{U}(j^{*}\widetilde{\delta}_{2})
and

(j^{*}\widetilde{\delta}_{1},j^{*}\widetilde{\delta}_{2})\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{2}(U)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{U}=\mathrm{Sp}(A)\subset\mathcal{T}^{2}
be an open affinoid
neighbourhood of
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
.
Let
(\widetilde{\delta_{1}},\widetilde{\delta_{2}})\in\mathcal{T}^{2}(A)
be the
characters corresponding to
\mathcal{U}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{T}^{2}
.
Since
\mathcal{T}^{+}
is a closed subspace of
\mathcal{T}
, we can choose
\mathcal{U}

in such a way so that
\widetilde{\delta_{1}}/\widetilde{\delta_{2}}\widehat{\otimes}k(u)\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(k(u))

for any
u\in\mathcal{U}
.
By Theorem
1.15
, we have

H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{A}(\widetilde{\delta_{1}}/\widetilde{\delta_{2}}))=0
,
in particular it is a locally free sheaf over
\mathcal{U}
.
By Theorem
4.2
, there exists a vector bundle

Y\rightarrow\mathcal{U}
of rank
\dim_{L}H^{1}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}))

and a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
on
Y
such that there exists a surjective map

\displaystyle Y(k(\delta_{1},\delta_{2}))=Y(L)

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{A}(\widetilde{\delta_{1}}/\widetilde{\delta_{2}})\widehat{\otimes}_{A}L)=\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{2}),\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{1}))

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle\mapsto y^{*}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}.

As the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D
is an extension of
\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{2})

by
\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{1})
,
there is a point
y\in Y(L)
such that
y^{*}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}=D
.
Let us define the rigid
L
-space
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\coloneqq Y\times\mathrm{Sp}(L)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}(L)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}

and let
\mathbb{D}\coloneqq p_{1}^{*}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
.
Let
x
be any point of
X
such that
p_{1}(x)=y
, so that

x^{*}\mathbb{D}=(p_{1}\circ x)^{*}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}=y^{*}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}=D
.
Now we only have to show that there is a Zariski-open dense subset

U\xhookrightarrow{j}Y
such that
j^{*}\mathbb{D}
has regular parameters.
Let
\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{reg}}:=\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{T}_{2}^{\mathrm{reg}}
which is a
Zariski-open dense in
\mathcal{U}
.
The rigid space
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\coloneqq X\times_{\mathcal{U}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{reg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{reg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}

is Zariski-open dense in
X
: in fact
Y
is a vector bundle over
\mathcal{U}
,
so in particular the map
v
is open and preimages of dense subsets through an open map are dense.
Moreover
q_{1}^{*}\mathbb{D}
has parameters

u^{*}(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\widetilde{\delta}_{2})\in\mathcal{T}^{2}(\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{reg}})
, hence regular.
∎

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n+1})\in\mathcal{T}^{n+1}(L)
such that

\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)
for
i<j
and let

D_{n+1}\in H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n}(\delta_{n+1}^{-1}))
where
D_{n}
is a triangulable

(\phi,\Gamma)
-module with parameters
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
.
Let us denote by
D_{i}
the
i
-th piece of the filtration with graded pieces

\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{1}),\dots,\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{n})
.
Then there exist

•

a rigid space
X_{2}
over
L
which is also a vector bundle over an
appropriate neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}
of
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
in
\mathcal{T}^{2}
;

•

rigid spaces
X_{i}
over
L
which are also vector bundles over

X_{i-1}\times\mathcal{U}_{i}
, where
\mathcal{U}_{i}
is an
appropriate neighbourhood of
\delta_{i}
in
\mathcal{T}
for all
3\leqslant i\leqslant n+1
;

•

families of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{i}
over
X_{i}
for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n+1
;

•

maps
(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{i}):X_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}^{i}
for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n+1

such that there are

(1)

x_{i}\in X_{i}
such that
\mathbb{D}_{i}\widehat{\otimes}k(x_{i})=D_{i}
and

(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{i})\widehat{\otimes}k(x_{i})=(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{i})

for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n+1
;

(2)

Zariski open dense subsets
U_{i}\xhookrightarrow{j_{i}}X_{i}
such that
j_{i}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{i}

has a filtration of sub-
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules with graded pieces
\mathcal{R}_{U_{i}}(\widetilde{\delta_{1}}),\dots,\mathcal{R}_{U_{i}}(\widetilde{\delta}_{i})
and
j_{i}^{*}(\widetilde{\delta_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\delta_{i}})\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{i}(U_{i})

for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n+1
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
i
.
Proposition
4.4
shows that there exist

•

a rigid space
X_{2}
over
L
which is also a vector bundle over an
appropriate neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}
of
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
in
\mathcal{T}^{2}
;

•

a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{2}
over
X_{2}
;

•

a map
(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\widetilde{\delta}_{2}):X_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}^{2}

such that there are

(1)

x_{2}\in X_{2}
such that
\mathbb{D}_{2}\widehat{\otimes}k(x_{2})=D_{2}
and

(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\widetilde{\delta}_{2})\widehat{\otimes}k(x_{2})=(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})
;

(2)

a Zariski open dense subset
U_{2}\xhookrightarrow{j_{2}}X_{2}
such that
j_{2}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{2}

has a filtration of sub-
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules with graded pieces
\mathcal{R}_{U_{2}}(\widetilde{\delta_{1}}),\mathcal{R}_{U_{2}}(\widetilde{\delta}_{2})
and
j_{2}^{*}(\widetilde{\delta_{2}},\widetilde{\delta_{2}})\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{2}(U_{2})
.

Hence the statement is true for
i=2
.
Assume now that the statement of the Proposition is true for any
i<n+1
.
Let
X_{n}
be the vector bundle over
X_{n-1}\times\mathcal{U}_{n}
as described in the statement
of the Proposition, which means in particular that
it is a vector bundle over the neighbourhood

\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\coloneqq\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\dots\times\mathcal{U}_{n}
of

(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})
inside
\mathcal{T}^{n}
; let moreover
\mathbb{D}_{n}
be the family
of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
X_{n}
satisfying the properties above.
Let
\mathcal{U}_{n+1}
be a neighbourhood of
\delta_{n+1}
in
\mathcal{T}

and similarly let
\widetilde{\delta}_{n+1}
be the character corresponding to

\mathcal{U}_{n+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}
.
Let us define the open neighbourhood
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\times\mathcal{U}_{n+1}

of
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n+1})
in
\mathcal{T}^{n+1}
.
We intersect
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}
with the open neighbourhood of
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n+1})

defined as in Lemma
1.16
.
In particular we can assume that

\widetilde{\delta}_{i}/\widetilde{\delta}_{n+1}\widehat{\otimes}k(u)\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(k(u))
for
all
i<n+1
and for all
u\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}
.
Let us consider the space
X_{n}\times\mathcal{U}_{n+1}
, which is a vector bundle over
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}
.
For simplicity of notation, we denote by
\mathbb{D}_{n}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{U}_{n+1}}(\widetilde{\delta}_{n+1})

the pullback of these two
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules to
X_{n}\times\mathcal{U}_{n+1}
.
By the way we have chosen the neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}_{n+1}
, we have
that the sheaf
H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathbb{D}_{n}(\widetilde{\delta}_{n+1}^{-1}))

is locally free over
X_{n}\times\mathcal{U}_{n+1}
.
Hence by Theorem
4.2
, there is a vector bundle

Y_{n+1}\rightarrow X_{n}\times\mathcal{U}_{n+1}

and a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{n+1}
on
Y_{n+1}

such that there exists a surjective map

\displaystyle Y_{n+1}(k(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n+1}))=Y_{n+1}(L)

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathbb{D}_{n}(\widetilde{\delta}_{n+1}^{-1})\widehat{\otimes}L)=\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{n+1}),D_{n})

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle\mapsto y^{*}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{n+1}.

This implies that there is
y_{n+1}\in Y_{n+1}(L)
such that
y_{n+1}^{*}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{n+1}=D_{n+1}
.
It is now sufficient to condider the space
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{n+1}\coloneqq Y_{n+1}\times\mathrm{Sp}(L)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}(L)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}

and let
\mathbb{D}_{n+1}\coloneqq p_{1}^{*}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{n+1}
.
Any point
x_{n+1}\in X_{n+1}(L)
such that
p_{1}(x_{n+1})=y_{n+1}
has the property that

x_{n+1}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{n+1}=(p_{1}\circ x_{n+1})^{*}\mathbb{D}_{n+1}^{\prime}=y_{n+1}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{n+1}^{\prime}=D_{n+1}
.
Finally, let
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}^{\mathrm{reg}}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}\cap\mathcal{T}_{n+1}^{\mathrm{reg}}
which is a
Zariski-open dense in
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}
.
The rigid space
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{n+1}\coloneqq X_{n+1}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}^{\mathrm{reg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}^{\mathrm{reg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}

is Zariski-open dense in
X_{n+1}
(in fact
Y_{n+1}
is a vector bundle over
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}
,
making the map
v
open).
Moreover
q_{1}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{n+1}
has parameters

u^{*}(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{n+1})\in\mathcal{T}^{n+1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}^{\mathrm{reg}})
,
therefore they are regular.
∎

Proof of Theorem
A
.

Using Theorem
3.3
, the Propositions
4.4
and
4.5
above
prove that for any trianguline representation
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})

satisfying the hypothesis of Theorem
A
,
we have
(\rho,(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}))\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho})(L)
.
∎

5.
Some preparation

Before approaching the proof of Theorem
B
, we need some more preparation.

5.1.
The Beauville-Laszlo Theorem

The results presented in this section are mainly taken from Section 5.2 in
[
27
]
.
Fix
r\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
.
Then there exists
m_{0}=m_{0}(r)\in\mathbb{N}
(depending on
r
)
such that for every
m\geqslant m_{0}
, the point
1-\zeta_{p^{m}}

lies in the half open annulus
\mathbb{B}^{r}
, where
\zeta_{p^{m}}
is a
p^{m}
-th root of unity.
This means that the minimal polynomial of
1-\zeta_{p^{m}}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}

defines a point
\alpha_{m}
in
\mathbb{B}^{r}
with residue field
K_{m}\coloneqq\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{m}})
.
Therefore for all
m\geqslant m_{0}
, there exists a surjective morphism

\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{r}\twoheadrightarrow K_{m}.

The element
t\coloneqq\mathrm{log}(1+T)
is a uniformizer of the completed local ring
at each
\alpha_{m}
; hence we have that
\widehat{(\mathcal{R}^{r}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})}_{\alpha_{m}}
is isomorphic
to a power series ring in one variable and with coefficients in
K_{m}
.
Therefore this gives a map

\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{r}\rightarrow K_{m}\llbracket t\rrbracket.

Let us then fix
m\geqslant m_{0}
and let us assume that
L
contains
K_{m}
.
For any affinoid
L
-algebra
A
, we have a morphism

\iota_{r,m}:\mathcal{R}^{r}_{A}=\mathcal{R}^{r}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A\rightarrow K_{m}\llbracket t\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A\cong\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket.

Let
r\leqslant s<1
such that
s\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
.

Notation
.

From now on for a ring
R
, we denote by
\mathrm{Mod}(R)
the category of modules over
R
.

Definition 5.1
.

We define

\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}[1/t])\times_{\mathrm{Mod}(\prod A((t)))}\mathrm{Mod}(\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket)

to be the category of glueing data whose objects are tuples
(M^{[r,s]},\Lambda^{[r,s]},f^{[r,s]})
where

•

M^{[r,s]}\in\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}[1/t])
;

•

\displaystyle\Lambda^{[r,s]}\in\mathrm{Mod}(\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket)
;

•

f^{[r,s]}:M^{[r,s]}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}[1/t]}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A((t))\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda^{[r,s]}[1/t]

is an isomorphism of
\displaystyle\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A((t))
-modules.

The following Theorem can be found in
[
27
, (5.4)]
and it is an analogue for

(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules of the Theorem of Beauville-Laszlo (cf.
[
9
]
).

Theorem 5.2
.

Let us assume that
\alpha_{m}\in\mathbb{B}^{[r,s]}
and no other point
\alpha_{n}

obtained from the minimal polynomial of
1-\zeta_{p^{n}}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}

is contained in
\mathbb{B}^{[r,s]}
(for example
s=r^{1/p}
).
There is an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]})\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}[1/t])\times_{\mathrm{Mod}(\prod A((t)))}\mathrm{Mod}(\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket)

\displaystyle D^{[r,s]}\mapsto

\displaystyle(D^{[r,s]}[1/t],D^{[r,s]}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket,f^{[r,s]}),

where the isomorphism
f^{[r,s]}
of
\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A((t))
-modules is given by

(5.1)

(D^{[r,s]}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket)[1/t]\cong D^{[r,s]}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}[1/t]}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A((t))\\

via
t^{i}(m\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}}1)\mapsto(m\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}}t^{i})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}[1/t]}1
for
m\in D^{[r,s]}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The inverse of this equivalence is given by

\ker(\overline{f^{[r,s]}})\mapsfrom(M^{[r,s]},\Lambda^{[r,s]},f^{[r,s]}),

where

\overline{f^{[r,s]}}:M^{[r,s]}\rightarrow M^{[r,s]}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}[1/t]}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A((t))\xrightarrow{f^{[r,s]}}\Lambda^{[r,s]}[1/t]\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda^{[r,s]}[1/t]/\Lambda^{[r,s]}.

5.2.
\phi
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}

We’ll see that any
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over the Robba ring can also be represented by a
(\phi,\Gamma)

-module over the subring
\mathcal{R}^{r}
for some appropriate
r
depending on the module.
In this section we define the notion of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}^{r}
and see some properties.
Let
0\leqslant r\leqslant s<1
such that
r,s\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
and let
A
be an affinoid

L
-algebra.
The rings
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}
and
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}
are both equipped with a Frobenius morphism

\displaystyle\phi:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r^{1/p},s^{1/p}]},

\displaystyle\phi:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r^{1/p}}

which is simply the restriction of the Frobenius on the Robba ring
\mathcal{R}_{A}
.
Let us define the restriction map

\mathrm{res}^{r}_{r^{1/p}}:\mathbb{B}_{A}^{r^{1/p}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{B}_{A}^{r}.

Definition 5.3
.

•

A
\mathcal{R}^{r}_{A}
-module
D
is called a
\phi
-module
if
D
is projective,
of finite presentation and there exists an isomorphism

\phi^{*}D\cong(\mathrm{res}^{r}_{r^{1/p}})^{*}D.

•

A
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}^{r}_{A}
is a
\phi
-module
D
over
\mathcal{R}^{r}_{A}

endowed with a continuous action of
\Gamma
commuting with
\phi
.

Definition 5.4
.

Let
A
be an affinoid
L
-algebra and let
r_{0}\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
.

(i)

A
coherent sheaf
on
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}
consists of a family of finite

\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}
-modules
D^{[r,s]}
for all
r_{0}\leqslant r\leqslant s<1

together with isomorphisms

D^{[r^{\prime},s^{\prime}]}\cong D^{[r,s]}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}}\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r^{\prime},s^{\prime}]}

for all
r_{0}\leqslant r\leqslant r^{\prime}\leqslant s^{\prime}\leqslant s<1
satisfying the cocycle conditions.

(ii)

A
vector bundle
on
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}
is a coherent sheaf
(D^{[r,s]})_{r_{0}\leqslant r\leqslant s<1}

on
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}
such that each
D^{[r,s]}
is a projective module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]}
.

The following Theorem is due to
[
22
, Proposition 2.2.7.]
.

Theorem 5.5
.

For every affinoid
L
-algebra
A
and for any
r_{0}\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
,
there exists an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\{\phi\text{-modules over }\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}\}\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\text{vector bundles }(D^{[r,s]})_{r_{0}\leqslant r\leqslant s<1}\text{ on }\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}\text{ together with }\\
\phi^{*}D^{[r,s]}\cong D^{[r^{1/p},s^{1/p}]}\text{ compatible with restrictions}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle D^{r_{0}}\mapsto

\displaystyle(D^{r_{0}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}}\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r,s]})_{r_{0}\leqslant r\leqslant s<1}.

As already mentioned in the beginning of this section, any
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D
over the Robba ring can be seen as
a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}^{r}
for some
r
depending on
D
.
More precisely, we have the following Theorem proved in
[
2
, Lemma 2.2.8]
.

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
D
be any
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{L}
. There exists a unique

r(D)\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
such that for all
r\geqslant r(D)
, there is a unique
\phi
-module
D^{r}

over
\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}
such that
D=D^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{L}
.
Moreover
D^{r}
is stable under the action of
\Gamma
induced by
D
on
D^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{L}
.

More generally, let
\mathbb{D}
be a family of free
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over a rigid affinoid space

\mathrm{Sp}(A)
and let
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{d}\}
be a basis of
\mathbb{D}
.
Let us consider the matrix
M
of
\Phi
in this basis.
Then there exists
r(D)\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
such that
M\in GL_{d}(\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r})

for all
r\geqslant r(D)
.
We can then take
\mathbb{D}^{r}\coloneqq\oplus_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}e_{i}
and
we have the following Remark.

Remark 5.7
.

Let
\mathbb{D}
be a family of locally free
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over a rigid space
X
.
Then there exists
r\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
and a
\phi
-module
\mathbb{D}^{r}
over
\mathcal{R}_{X}^{r}

such that
\mathbb{D}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{X}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{X}=\mathbb{D}
.

5.3.
From
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}
to
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}[1/t]

We will show how to view a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{L}
as a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over

\mathcal{R}_{L}[1/t]
and a lattice without losing any information.

Let
A
be an affinoid
L
-algebra.
First of all notice that the morphism

\phi:\mathcal{R}_{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{A},T\mapsto(1+T)^{p}-1

defined in (
1.1
) can be extended to

\phi:\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]

simply by
\frac{1}{t}\mapsto\frac{1}{pt}
.

Definition 5.8
.

A
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]
is a locally free
\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]
-module
M

of finite presentation endowed with

•

a
\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]
-semilinear endomorphism
\Phi:M\rightarrow M
such that

\Phi:\phi^{*}M\rightarrow M
is an isomorphism;

•

a continuous
\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]
-semilinear
\Gamma
-action commuting with
\Phi
.

Let
(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r})
denote the category of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}
and
(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t])

the category of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]
.

Definition 5.9
.

A
lattice
\Lambda
in
A((t))^{n}
is a
finitely generated projective
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-module such that

\Lambda\otimes_{A\llbracket t\rrbracket}A((t))\cong A((t))^{n}.

Definition 5.10
.

For
r\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
satisfying the conditions presented in the beginning of §
5.1
,
let us denote by

(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}^{r}_{A}[1/t])\times_{\mathrm{Mod}(\prod A((t)))^{\Gamma}}\mathrm{Mod}(\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket)^{\Gamma}

the category whose objects are tuples
(M,\Lambda,f)
where

•

M\in(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}^{r}_{A}[1/t])
;

•

\displaystyle\Lambda\in\mathrm{Mod}(\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket)
;

•

\displaystyle f:M\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A((t))\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda[1/t]
is an isomorphism of
\prod A((t))
-modules

and
\Lambda
is stable under the action of
\Gamma
induced on
\Lambda[1/t]
under
f

from the diagonal action of
\Gamma
on
M\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A((t))
.

Theorem 5.11
.

Let
r_{0}\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
such that
\mathbb{B}^{[r_{0},r_{0}]}
