does not contain any zero for
t

(i.e.
r_{0}\neq|1-\zeta_{p^{m}}|
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
).
There is an equivalence of categories

(5.2)

\displaystyle(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}})\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}^{r_{0}}_{A}[1/t])\times_{\mathrm{Mod}(\prod A((t)))^{\Gamma}}\mathrm{Mod}(\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket)^{\Gamma}

\displaystyle D^{r_{0}}\mapsto

\displaystyle(D^{r_{0}}[1/t],D^{r_{0}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket,f),

where

f:D^{r_{0}}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}[1/t]}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A((t))\xrightarrow{\sim}(D^{r_{0}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket)[1/t]

via
e_{i}^{\prime}\otimes 1\mapsto e_{i}\otimes 1
with
e_{1},\dots,e_{n}
basis of
D^{r_{0}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}

and
e_{1}^{\prime}=e_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}}1,\dots,e_{n}^{\prime}=e_{n}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}}1
basis
of
D^{r_{0}}[1/t]
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}[1/t]
.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda\coloneqq D^{r_{0}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket
.
First of all, notice that the
\Gamma
-action of
D^{r_{0}}[1/t]
extends diagonally to

f:D^{r_{0}}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}[1/t]}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A((t))\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda[1/t]

and stabilizes
\Lambda
by definition.
In order to show that the above is an equivalence, we describe the inverse functor:
let
(M,\Lambda,f)
be an object of the right hand side of (
5.2
).
We denote by
M_{0}
and
\Lambda_{0}
the restriction of
M
and
\Lambda
respectively to
th annulus
\mathbb{B}^{[r_{0},r_{1}]}
, where
r_{1}\coloneqq r_{0}^{1/p}
; moreover
let us denote by
\Lambda_{i}\coloneqq\phi^{*}\Lambda_{i-1}
and
M_{i}\coloneqq\phi^{*}M_{i-1}
for
i\geqslant 1
.
If we let
r_{i}\coloneqq r_{0}^{1/p^{i}}
for
i\geqslant 1
, this yields to a family of objects

((M_{i},\Lambda_{i},f_{i})\in\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r_{i},r_{i+1}]}[1/t])\times_{\mathrm{Mod}(\prod A((t)))}\mathrm{Mod}(\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket))_{i}

for
i\geqslant 0
such that
M_{i}
is a
\phi
-module
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{[r_{i},r_{i+1}]}[1/t]
endowed with an action of
\Gamma
.
Thanks to Theorem
5.2
,
this corresponds to a family of vector bundles
(D^{[r_{i},r_{i+1}]})_{i\geqslant 0}
with a

\Gamma
-action and such that
\phi^{*}D^{[r_{i},r_{i+1}]}\cong D^{[r_{i+1},r_{i+2}]}
.
We will now show that we can glue
D^{[r_{i-1},r_{i}]}
and
D^{[r_{i},r_{i+1}]}

along the intersection
\mathbb{B}^{[r_{i},r_{i}]}
for all
i\geqslant 1
.
By assumption, we have that

D^{[r_{i},r_{i+1}]}_{|\mathbb{B}^{[r_{i},r_{i+1}]}\setminus V(t)}=M_{i|\mathbb{B}^{[r_{i},r_{i+1}]}\setminus V(t)}

for all
i\geqslant 0
.
By hypothesis, we have that
V(t)
does not intersect
\mathbb{B}^{[r_{i},r_{i}]}
for all
i\geqslant 0
, thus

D^{[r_{i-1},r_{i}]}_{|\mathbb{B}^{[r_{i},r_{i}]}}\cong M_{i-1|\mathbb{B}^{[r_{i},r_{i}]}}\cong\phi^{*}M_{i-1|\mathbb{B}^{[r_{i},r_{i}]}}\cong M_{i|\mathbb{B}^{[r_{i},r_{i}]}}\cong D^{[r_{i},r_{i+1}]}_{|\mathbb{B}^{[r_{i},r_{i}]}}.

Therefore we have that the family
(D^{[r_{i},r_{i+1}]})_{i\geqslant 0}
is compatible with restrictions and
by Theorem
5.5
, this gives a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D^{r_{0}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r_{0}}
.
∎

Remark 5.12
.

Let
K_{\infty}:=\bigcup_{m}K_{m}
and let
\Gamma_{m}:=\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K_{m})\subset\Gamma=\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Let
(M,\Lambda,f)
be an object of
(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}^{r}_{A}[1/t])\times_{\mathrm{Mod}(\prod A((t)))^{\Gamma}}\mathrm{Mod}(\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket)^{\Gamma}
.
Notice that the action of
\Gamma_{m}
on

M\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A((t))\cong\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A((t))^{n}

preserves each factor of the product.
Moreover if we fix an embedding
\tau:K_{m}\hookrightarrow L
and a
\Gamma_{m}
-stable
lattice
\Lambda_{\tau}
, then it is possible to recover
\Lambda
from it:
let
g_{m}
be a generator of
\mathrm{Gal}(K_{m}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, then we have

g_{m}^{n}\cdot\Lambda_{\tau}=\Lambda_{\tau_{n}}

where
\tau_{n}:K_{m}\xrightarrow{g_{m}^{n}}K_{m}\xhookrightarrow{\tau}L
.
Thus

\Lambda=\prod_{n}g_{m}^{n}\cdot\Lambda_{\tau},

where the product ranges over
n\in\{0,\dots,|\mathrm{Gal}(K_{m}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})|-1\}
and

\Lambda_{\tau}
is a
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice in
A((t))^{n}
.
Hence a
\Gamma
-stable lattice in
\prod A((t))^{n}
is equivalent to a
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice in
one of the factors
A((t))^{n}
, as the
\Gamma
-action translates the lattice to the other factors.

Notice furthermore that we have the following bijection.

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
M\in(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t])
for some affinoid algebra
A
.
Let
r\in[0,1)\cap\mathbb{Q}
such that
\mathbb{B}^{[r,r]}
does not contain any zero for
t

and such that there exists a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
M^{r}
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]

such that
M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]}\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]=M
(this is always possible by Theorem
5.6
)
and let us take
m\in\mathbb{N}

big enough so that there is a surjective morphism
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{r}\twoheadrightarrow K_{m}

as explained at the beginning of Section
5.1
.
Let us assume that
L
is big enough so that

K_{m}\subset L
(we can always do that thanks to Lemma
3.4
)
and let us fix an embedding
\tau:K_{m}\hookrightarrow L
.
Let
\mathrm{Gr}_{M}^{\Gamma}
be the set

(5.3)

\mathrm{Gr}_{M}^{\Gamma}\coloneqq\left\{\Lambda\colon\Lambda\text{ is a }\Gamma_{m}\text{-stable lattice of }M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))\right\}

and let
M^{(\phi,\Gamma)}
be the set

(5.4)

M^{(\phi,\Gamma)}\coloneqq\{D\in(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A})\colon D\subset M\text{ and }D[1/t]=M\}.

Then the functor described in Theorem
5.11
induces the
bijection

\displaystyle M^{(\phi,\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{M}^{\Gamma}

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle\mapsto(D^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r},\tau}A\llbracket t\rrbracket),

where
D^{r}
is a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}
such that

D=D^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{A}
.

Remark 5.14
.

Notice that for a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D\in M^{(\phi,\Gamma)}
,
we have a morphism of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{A}

\iota:D\hookrightarrow M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{i}d}M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]}\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t],

whose image is obviously
D
and of the form
D^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{A}

for some sub-
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}
-module
D^{r}
of
M^{r}
.
Let
\gamma
be a topological generator of
\Gamma
.
The fact that
\iota
is a morphism of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules implies that the image of
D^{r}
through

\gamma
is contained in
D^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{A}
, therefore (after choosing a basis for
D^{r}
)
the matrix of
\gamma
has coefficients in
\mathcal{R}_{A}
.
At the same time, the module
M^{r}
is stable under
\gamma
by hypothesis, thus the matrix
of
\gamma
in the same basis has coefficients in
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]
.
This implies that the coefficients of the matrix of the action of
\gamma
on
D^{r}

has coefficients in
\mathcal{R}^{r}_{A}
and therefore
D^{r}
is
\Gamma
-stable.
The fact that
M^{r}
is a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}^{r}_{A}[1/t]
implies that

\phi^{*}D^{r}\cong(\mathrm{res}_{r}^{r^{1/p}})^{*}D^{r}
.
Thus we have that such a
D^{r}
is a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}

and
D=D^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{A}
.

Proof.

Let

\mathcal{A}\coloneqq\left\{(\Lambda,f)\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\Lambda\text{ is a }\Gamma_{m}\text{-stable projective }A\llbracket t\rrbracket\text{-module and}\\
f:\Lambda[1/t]\xrightarrow{\sim}M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))\text{ isomorphism of }A((t))\text{-modules}\end{array}\right\},

where the action of
\Gamma_{m}
on
\Lambda[1/t]
is induced by the isomorphism
f
.
Let moreover

(5.5)

\mathcal{B}\coloneqq\left\{(D^{r},g)\colon\begin{array}[]{c}D^{r}\text{ is a }(\phi,\Gamma)\text{-module over }\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}\text{ and}\\
g:D^{r}[1/t]\xrightarrow{\sim}M^{r}\text{ isomorphism of }(\phi,\Gamma)\text{-modules over }\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]\end{array}\right\}.

By Remark
5.12
and since the functor of Theorem
5.11
is an equivalence,
we obviously have that the functor induces a bijection between the sets of isomorphism classes of the above
two sets.
If we denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
the sets of isomorphism classes
of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
respectively, we just have to show that
we have bijections

\begin{array}[]{lcr}\begin{aligned} \mathrm{Gr}_{M}^{\Gamma}&\leftrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\\
\Lambda&\mapsto(\Lambda,\mathrm{id}_{\Lambda[1/t]})\end{aligned}&\text{ and }&\begin{aligned} M^{(\phi,\Gamma)}&\leftrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\\
D&\mapsto(D^{r},\mathrm{id}_{D^{r}[1/t]}).\end{aligned}\end{array}

•

Let’s start showing the injectivity of the first map: assume that
\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{M}^{\Gamma}

such that
(\Lambda,\mathrm{id}_{\Lambda[1/t]})\cong(\Lambda^{\prime},\mathrm{id}_{\Lambda^{\prime}[1/t]})
.
This means that there is an isomorphism
\xi:\Lambda\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda^{\prime}
of
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-modules
such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda[1/t]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\prime}[1/t]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

commutes.
It is then obvious that
\xi
must be the identity and so
\Lambda=\Lambda^{\prime}
.
As for surjectivity, let
(\Lambda,f)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
; notice then that

f(\Lambda)\subset M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))
is a lattice and it is
stable under the action of
\Gamma_{m}
induced by
M^{r}
by definition of the
\Gamma
-action on
\Lambda

and by the fact that
\Lambda
is stable under
\Gamma_{m}
.
Finally, we have that
(\Lambda,f)\xrightarrow{f}(f(\Lambda),\mathrm{id}_{f(\Lambda)[1/t]})

is an isomorphism in the category
\mathcal{A}
.

•

The bijectivity of the second map is proved in an analogous way.
More precisely, let
D,D^{\prime}\in M^{(\phi,\Gamma)}
such that

(D^{r},\mathrm{id}_{D^{r}[1/t]})\cong(D^{\prime r},\mathrm{id}_{D^{\prime r}[1/t]})
.
In other words, we have an isomorphism
\xi:D^{r}\xrightarrow{\sim}D^{\prime r}
of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}
and a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{\prime r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{r}[1/t]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{\prime r}[1/t].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

Thus
D^{r}=D^{\prime r}
and
D=D^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{A}=D^{\prime r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{A}=D^{\prime}
,
hence the map is injective.
On the other hand, let
(D^{r},f)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
; notice that the
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}
-module

f(D^{r})\subset M^{r}
is stable under the action of
\Gamma
inherited by
M^{r}
:
in fact by definition of morphism of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules, we have that the action of

\Gamma
commutes with
f
.
Moreover, the fact that
\phi^{*}M^{r}\cong(\mathrm{res}_{r}^{r^{1/p}})^{*}M^{r}
implies that

\phi^{*}f(D^{r})\cong(\mathrm{res}_{r}^{r^{1/p}})^{*}f(D^{r})
, therefore
f(D^{r})
is a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}
.
Obviously
f(D^{r})[1/t]=M^{r}
, hence
f(D^{r})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{A}\in M^{(\phi,\Gamma)}
.
Finally

(D^{r},f)\xrightarrow{f}(f(D^{r}),\mathrm{id}_{f(D^{r})[1/t]})
is an isomorphism in the category
\mathcal{B}
,
thus the map
M^{(\phi,\Gamma)}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
is surjective.

∎

5.4.
The
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}[1/t]

In this section we explore some nice results of how to compute the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology
of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{L}[1/t]
, which is defined using the Herr complex as in
Definition
1.8
.

Remark 5.15
.

Let
D
be a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}
for some affinoid
L
-algebra
A
.
Since direct limits are exact, we have

H^{i}_{\phi,\Gamma}\left(D\left[1/t\right]\right)=\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{n}}}H^{i}_{\phi,\Gamma}(t^{-n}D)=\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{n}}}H^{i}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D(x^{-n})).

for
i=0,1,2
.

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
\delta\in\mathcal{T}(L)

and let
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathrm{wt}(\delta)\notin\{1,\dots,k\}
.
Then

(i)

the morphism

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta))\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta x^{-k}))

is an isomorphism;

(ii)

H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(t^{-k}\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta)/\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta))=0
.

The reader can find Lemma
5.16
and its proof in

[
12
, Théorème 2.22 (i), Proposition 2.18 (i)]
.

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
\delta\in\mathcal{T}(L)
such that
\mathrm{wt}(\delta)\notin\{1,\dots,k\}
.
Then the map

H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta))\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta\cdot x^{-k}))

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Consider the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta)\rightarrow t^{-k}\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta)\rightarrow t^{-k}\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta)/\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta)\rightarrow 0,

which induces the long exact sequence of cohomologies

0\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta))\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(t^{-k}\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta))\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(t^{-k}\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta)/\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta))\rightarrow\dots.

By Lemma
5.16
, we have that
H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(t^{-k}\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta)/\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta))=0
,
which implies the wanted isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 5.18
.

Let
D_{n}
be a triangulable
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{L}
with parameters

(\delta_{1},\dots\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
and let
\delta\in\mathcal{T}(L)
such that

\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}/\delta)\notin\mathbb{N}_{>0}
for
i=1,\dots,n
.
We have

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n}(\delta^{-1}))\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n}(\delta^{-1}x^{-k})).

is an isomorphism for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
.

n=1:

This case is proved by Lemma
5.16
.

n\geqslant 2:

We want to show that the map

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n}(\delta^{-1}))\rightarrow H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n}(\delta^{-1}\cdot x^{-k}))

is an isomorphisms for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
D_{n-1}
be the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{L}
such that

D_{n}\in H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))
.
This means we have a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow D_{n-1}\rightarrow D_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{n})\rightarrow 0,

which induces the long exact horizontal rows of the following diagram
(where all cohomologies are
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomologies):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{n}/\delta))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(D_{n-1}(\delta^{-1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(D_{n}(\delta^{-1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{n}/\delta))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{n}\delta^{-1}x^{-k}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(D_{n-1}(\delta^{-1}x^{-k}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(D_{n}(\delta^{-1}x^{-k}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{n}\delta^{-1}x^{-k}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

Notice first of all that, since
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}/\delta)\notin\mathbb{N}_{>0}
, we have that

H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta^{-1}))=H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta^{-1}x^{-k}))=0
.
This explains why the two long exact sequences above are right-exact.
Furthermore the first vertical arrow is an isomorphism by Lemma
5.17
,
the second vertical arrow is an isomorphism by inductive hypothesis and the last
verical arrow is an isomorphism by Lemma
5.16
.
We can therefore conclude by the five lemma that the middle vertical arrow is an isomorphism.

∎

5.5.
The affine Grassmannian

There exists a "space" with a universal family of lattices: the affine Grassmannian.
In this section we recall some basic notions about it.

Recall that a
lattice
in
A((t))^{n}
is a finitely generated projective

A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-module
\Lambda\subset A((t))^{n}
such that
\Lambda[1/t]\cong A((t))^{n}
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
.
The
affine Grassmannian
is the functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Gr}_{n}:\underline{Rings}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\underline{Sets}

sending a ring
A
to the set of
lattices in
A((t))^{n}
.

Theorem 5.19
.

The affine Grassmannian is representable by an ind-projective scheme.
More precisely, let
\mathrm{Gr}_{n,i}
be the subfunctor of the affine Grassmannian
sending a ring
A
to the set
consisting of those lattices
\Lambda
such that

t^{i}A\llbracket t\rrbracket^{n}\subset\Lambda\subset t^{-i}A\llbracket t\rrbracket^{n}

for
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
We have that
\mathrm{Gr}_{n,i}
is representable by a projective scheme and
the affine Grassmannian is the direct limit of the subfunctors
\mathrm{Gr}_{n,i}
.

In particular, we have that each
\mathrm{Gr}_{n,i}
is locally of finite type, hence
we can consider their rigidification through the GAGA functor and obtain rigid spaces

\mathrm{Gr}_{n,i}^{\mathrm{rig}}
.
Hence we define

\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{rig}}\coloneqq\lim_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathrm{Gr}_{n,i}^{\mathrm{rig}}.

This is the functor on rigid spaces

\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{rig}}:\underline{Rig}\rightarrow\underline{Sets}

sending an affinoid rigid space
\mathrm{Sp}(A)
to the set of lattices in
A((t))^{n}
.
Moreover, we denote by
\mathbb{L}
the universal object of
\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mathrm{rig}}
.

Another description of the affine Grassmannian is through loop groups: given an algebraic group
G
,
the
loop group of
G

is the presheaf
LG
defined by
LG(R)\coloneqq G(R((t)))
and the
positive loop group of
G

is the presheaf
L^{+}G
defined by
L^{+}G(R)\coloneqq G(R\llbracket t\rrbracket)
.
Then the affine Grassmannian is the fpqc quotient
LGL_{n}/L^{+}GL_{n}
.

In this paragraph we study a bit more the geometry of the affine Grassmannian:
let
B_{n}\subset GL_{n}
be the Borel subgroup of upper triangular matrices of
GL_{n}
,

T_{n}\subset B_{n}
the maximal torus of diagonal matrices and
U_{n}\subset B_{n}
the unipotent
radical of
B_{n}
.
Let
X_{*}(T_{n})
be the coweight group of
T_{n}
, so the group of all algebraic homomorphisms
\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow T_{n}
,
which can also be identified with tuples
\mu=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
.
Moreover, let
X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}
be the group of dominant coweights of
T_{n}
; recall that we say that a coweight
(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}

is
dominant
if
\mu_{1}\leqslant\mu_{2}\leqslant\dots\leqslant\mu_{n}
.
For dominant coweights
\lambda,\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}
, we say
\lambda\leqslant\mu
if

\lambda-\mu
is a sum of simple co-roots and this defines a partial order on
X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}
.
For a ring
R
we have

\mathrm{Gr}_{n}(R)=\coprod_{\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}}L^{+}GL_{n}(R)\cdot t^{\mu}\cdot L^{+}GL_{n}(R)/L^{+}GL_{n}(R),

where
t^{\mu}
is the image of
t
through the coweight
\mu:R((t))^{\times}\rightarrow T_{n}(R((t)))
.
For a coweight
\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})
, we define the
Schubert cell

\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mu}\coloneqq LGL_{n}^{+}\cdot t^{\mu}\cdot LGL_{n}^{+}/LGL_{n}^{+};

as a consequence, we have the following
Cartan decomposition
:

\mathrm{Gr}_{n}=\coprod_{\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}}\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mu}.

Moreover the closure relations of the Schubert cells are given by the partial order on dominant coweights:

\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\leqslant\mu}\coloneqq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mu}}=\bigcup_{\lambda\leqslant\mu}\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\lambda}.

The space
\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\leqslant\mu}
is called
Schubert variety
and the Schubert cell
\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mu}

is open in
\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\leqslant\mu}
.
For a coweight
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
, let
P_{\mu}
be the parabolic subgroup of
GL_{n}

corresponding to
\mu
, i.e. the parabolic subgroup generated by
B_{n}
and by the
root subgroups
U_{\alpha}
for those roots
\alpha
satisfying
\langle\alpha,\mu\rangle\leqslant 0
.
For a ring
R
, let us fix the standard lattice
\Lambda_{0}\coloneqq R\llbracket t\rrbracket^{n}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{n}(R)

and a basis
e_{1},\dots,e_{n}
of
\Lambda_{0}
over
R\llbracket t\rrbracket
.
Denote by
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(V)
the standard filtration of
V\coloneqq\Lambda_{0}[1/t]

given by
0\subset R((t))e_{1}\subset R((t))e_{1}\oplus R((t))e_{2}\subset\dots\subset V
.
There is a natural projection

(5.6)

\displaystyle\pi_{\mu}:\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mu}=L^{+}GL_{n}\cdot t^{\mu}\cdot L^{+}GL_{n}/L^{+}GL_{n}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow GL_{n}/P_{\mu}

\displaystyle(g\cdot t^{\mu}\text{ mod }L^{+}GL_{n})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\bar{g}\text{ mod }P_{\mu}),

where
\bar{g}\coloneqq g\text{ mod }t
.
Recall that
GL_{n}/P_{\mu}
is a flag variety and
for a lattice
\Lambda\in\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mu}(R)
, we have that the flag
\pi_{\mu}(\Lambda)\in GL_{n}/P_{\mu}(R)

is given by the filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(R^{n})
where

(5.7)

\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(R^{n})\coloneqq(t^{-\mu_{i}}\Lambda\cap\Lambda_{0})/(t^{-\mu_{i}}\Lambda\cap t\Lambda_{0}).

We moreover have a section

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mu}:GL_{n}/P_{\mu}\rightarrow L^{+}GL_{n}\cdot t^{\mu}\cdot L^{+}GL_{n}/L^{+}GL_{n}=\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mu},

which sends a decreasing filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(R^{n})
of
R^{n}
to the lattice

(5.8)

\sigma_{\mu}(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(R^{n}))\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(R^{n})\otimes_{R}t^{\mu_{i}}R\llbracket t\rrbracket.

We define the maps

(5.9)

\mathrm{Gr}_{n}=\coprod_{\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}}\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mu}\mathrel{\mathop{\rightleftarrows}^{\pi}_{\sigma}}\coprod_{\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}}GL_{n}/P_{\mu}

taking
\Lambda\in\mathrm{Gr}_{n}
to
\pi_{\mu}(\Lambda)
if
\Lambda\in\mathrm{Gr}_{n}^{\mu}

and taking
gP_{\mu}\in GL_{n}/P_{\mu}
to
\sigma_{\mu}(gP_{\mu})
.

Finally, given
\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})
, we define the locally closed ind-scheme

Y_{\mu}\coloneqq LU_{n}\cdot t^{\mu}\cdot L^{+}GL_{n}/L^{+}GL_{n}:\underline{Rings}\rightarrow\underline{Sets}

of
\mathrm{Gr}_{n}
.

Lemma 5.20
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})\in X_{*}(T_{n})
and let
\Lambda\in Y_{\mu}(A)
for some affinoid algebra
A
.
Then
\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\Lambda)\coloneqq\Lambda\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(V)
is a locally free module over
A\llbracket t\rrbracket

for all
i=1,\dots,n
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{M}
be the universal lattice on
Y_{\mu}
, hence locally on the
affinoid
\mathrm{Sp}(A)
the lattice
\mathbb{M}

has a basis of the form

\{t^{\mu_{1}}e_{1},t^{\mu_{2}}e_{2}+x_{(1,2)}e_{1},\dots,t^{\mu_{n}}e_{n}+x_{(n-1,n)}e_{n-1}+\dots+x_{(1,n)}e_{1}\}

with
x_{(i,j)}\in A((t))
for all
i<j
.
Then we have that
\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\mathbb{M})
is the free
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-module generated
by the first
i
vectors of the above basis.
∎

Hence for a lattice
\Lambda\in Y_{\mu}(A)
, let us define a filtration of
\Lambda
as

\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\Lambda)\coloneqq\Lambda\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(V)
(in fact we know this is a filtration
by Lemma
5.20
).
Then we have that

Y_{\mu}(A)=\{\Lambda\in\mathrm{Gr}_{n}(A):\mathrm{gr}^{i}(\Lambda)=t^{\mu_{i}}\mathrm{gr}^{i}(\Lambda_{0})\text{ for }i=1,\dots,n\},

where the equality
\mathrm{gr}^{i}(\Lambda)=t^{\mu_{i}}\mathrm{gr}^{i}(\Lambda_{0})
is
intended as lattices of
\mathrm{gr}^{i}A((t))^{n}
.

Lemma 5.21
.

We have
\mathrm{Gr}=\coprod_{\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})}Y_{\mu}
as sets.

Proof.

First of all notice that
\Lambda\in Y_{\mu}\cap Y_{\lambda}
if and only if there exist

V,V^{\prime}\in LU
such that
Vt^{\mu}L^{+}G=\Lambda=V^{\prime}t^{\lambda}L^{+}G
; hence
\Lambda\in Y_{\mu}\cap Y_{\lambda}

if and only if there exists
V\in LU
such that
t^{-\lambda}Vt^{\mu}\in L^{+}G
:
it is obvious that this is possible only if
\lambda=\mu
.
Now let
\Lambda\in\mathrm{Gr}_{n}(K)
for some field
K
: we show that
\Lambda\in Y_{\mu}
for some
\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})
.
Let
\Lambda^{i}\coloneqq\Lambda\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(V)
for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n

(as
K\llbracket t\rrbracket
is a PID,
\Lambda^{i}
is a finite projective
K\llbracket t\rrbracket
-module)
and let us consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{i+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{i+1}/\Lambda^{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}^{i+1}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gr}^{i+1}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

Then
\Lambda^{i+1}/\Lambda^{i}
is a
K\llbracket t\rrbracket
-lattice of
\mathrm{gr}^{i+1}(V)=K((t))e_{i+1}
,
which means that there exists
\mu_{i+1}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

\Lambda^{i+1}/\Lambda^{i}=t^{\mu_{i+1}}e_{i+1}\cdot K\llbracket t\rrbracket=t^{\mu_{i+1}}\mathrm{gr}^{i+1}(\Lambda_{0})
.
It is then obvious that
\Lambda\in Y_{\mu}
, where
\mu=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})
constructed as described.
∎

Lemma 5.22
.

Let
\lambda\in X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}
,
\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})
and
w_{0}\in W_{n}
the longest element
of the Weyl group.
If
\mu=ww_{0}\lambda
for some
w\in W_{n}
,
we have that the diagram

(5.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{n}wP_{\lambda}/P_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{n}/P_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\mu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{\lambda}}

is a fiber product.

Proof.

We will show that

\sigma_{\lambda}(B_{n}wP_{\lambda}/P_{\lambda})=Y_{\mu}\cap\sigma_{\lambda}(GL_{n}/P_{\lambda}).

Notice first of all that
\sigma_{\lambda}(GL_{n}/P_{\lambda})=Gt^{\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G=Gt^{w_{0}\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G
, where
w_{0}\in W_{n}
is the longest element;
moreover
\sigma_{\lambda}(B_{n}wP_{\lambda}/P_{\lambda})=B_{n}wt^{\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G
by definition of
\sigma_{\lambda}
.
As
B_{n}wt^{\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G=U_{n}wt^{\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G
,
we prove that

Y_{\mu}\cap\sigma_{\lambda}(GL_{n}/P_{\lambda})=U_{n}wt^{\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G
.
As both schemes are reduced and locally closed subschemes of
LG/L^{+}G
, it’s enough
to work on
L
-points for some field
L
.
Observe that
B_{n}t^{w_{0}\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G=t^{w_{0}\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G
, in fact:
for any
(b_{ij})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant n}\in B_{n}
, we have

(b_{ij})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant n}\cdot t^{w_{0}\lambda}=t^{w_{0}\lambda}\cdot(b_{ij}t^{\lambda_{n-j+1}-\lambda_{n-i+1}})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant n};

since
(b_{ij})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant n}
is upper-triangular, we have that
b_{ij}=0
for
i>j
.
As a consequence of this fact and of the fact that
\lambda
is dominant, we have that the matrix

(b_{ij}t^{\lambda_{n-j+1}-\lambda_{n-i+1}})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant n}\in L^{+}G
.
Moreover by the Bruhat decomposition, we have

GL_{n}=\bigsqcup_{w\in W}U_{n}wB_{n},

hence

\displaystyle Gt^{\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G

\displaystyle=Gt^{w_{0}\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G=\bigsqcup_{w\in W_{n}}U_{n}wB_{n}t^{w_{0}\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{w\in W_{n}}U_{n}wt^{w_{0}\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G=\bigsqcup_{w\in W_{n}}U_{n}t^{ww_{0}\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G.

Obviously we have
U_{n}t^{ww_{0}\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G\subset LU_{n}t^{ww_{0}\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G

and

U_{n}wt^{w_{0}\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G\cap LU_{n}t^{\mu}L^{+}G/L^{+}G=\emptyset

for all
\mu\neq ww_{0}\lambda
.
This shows that

Y_{\mu}\cap\sigma_{\lambda}(GL_{n}/P_{\lambda})=U_{n}t^{ww_{0}\lambda}L^{+}G/L^{+}G=\sigma_{\lambda}(B_{n}wP_{\lambda}/P_{\lambda}),

where
ww_{0}\lambda=\mu
.
∎

Lemma 5.23
.

Let us fix the standard lattice
\Lambda_{0}
and a standard basis
\{b_{1},\dots,b_{n}\}
of
\Lambda_{0}
.
Let us decompose
\Lambda_{0}
as
\Lambda_{0}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{l}\Lambda_{0,i}
,
where
\Lambda_{0,i}
is a lattice of rank
n_{i}
; let us moreover assume that

\Lambda_{0,i}
has a basis consisting of a
subset of the standard basis for
\Lambda_{0}
and let us fix the standard basis of
\Lambda_{0,i}

to be such basis for all
i
.
Notice we can order the basis elements in such a way so that they respect the order
in the standard basis of
\Lambda_{0}
.
Let
\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})
and
\lambda_{i}\in X_{*}(T_{n_{i}})^{+}
for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant l
.
We have

Y_{\mu}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{l}GL_{n_{i}}/P_{\lambda_{i}}=\prod_{i=1}^{l}Y_{\mu_{i}}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}_{n_{i}}}GL_{n_{i}}/P_{\lambda_{i}}

for some
\mu_{i}\in X_{*}(T_{n_{i}})
, where the map
\prod_{i=1}^{l}GL_{n_{i}}/P_{\lambda_{i}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{n}=\prod_{i=1}^{l}\mathrm{Gr}_{n_{i}}

is given by

(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}})_{i=1,\dots,l}\mapsto(\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}}))_{i=1,\dots,l}.

Proof.

We show that

Y_{\mu}\cap\prod_{i=1}^{l}\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(GL_{n_{i}}/P_{\lambda_{i}})=\prod_{i=1}^{l}(Y_{\mu_{i}}\cap\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(GL_{n_{i}}/P_{\lambda_{i}}))

for some
\mu_{i}\in X_{*}(T_{n_{i}})
.
Observe that, as all schemes are reduced locally closed subspaces of
LG/L^{+}G
,
we can work on
L
-points for
L
a field.
For all
i
, we denote by
\{e_{1,i},\dots,e_{n_{i},i}\}
the standard basis of
\Lambda_{0,i}

consisting of elements of the standard basis
\{b_{1},\dots,b_{n}\}
of
\Lambda_{0}

such that if
e_{j,i}=b_{j^{\prime}}
,
e_{k,i}=b_{k^{\prime}}
and
j\leqslant k
, then
j^{\prime}\leqslant k^{\prime}
.
So let
\Lambda\in(Y_{\mu}\cap\prod_{i=1}^{l}\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(GL_{n_{i}}/P_{\lambda_{i}}))(L)
.
This means that
\Lambda=(\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}}))_{i=1,\dots,l}

for some
g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}}\in GL_{n_{i}}/P_{\lambda_{i}}
.
We will show that for all
1\leqslant j\leqslant n
we have

\prod_{i=1}^{l}\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}})\cap\prod_{k=1}^{j}L((t))b_{k}=\prod_{i=1}^{l}(\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}})\cap(L((t))e_{1,i}\oplus\dots\oplus L((t))e_{k_{(j,i)},i})),

where
\{b_{1},\dots,b_{j}\}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{l}\{e_{1,i},\dots,e_{k_{(j,i)},i}\}
.
Let

x\in\prod_{i=1}^{l}\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}})\cap\prod_{k=1}^{j}L((t))b_{k},

in particular
x=\sum_{i=1}^{l}x_{i}
with
x_{i}\in\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}})
.
As
\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}})\subset\prod_{k=1}^{n_{i}}L((t))e_{k,i}
,
we have that
x_{i}=\sum_{h=1}^{n_{i}}y_{h}e_{h,i}
with
y_{h}\in L((t))
.
As a consequence, we have

x=\sum_{i=1}^{l}\sum_{h=1}^{n_{i}}y_{h}e_{h,i}\in\prod_{k=1}^{j}L((t))b_{k}.

This proves the first inclusion. The inverse inclusion is obvious.
As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{l}\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}})\cap\prod_{k=1}^{j}L((t))b_{k}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{l}(\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}})\cap(L((t))e_{1,i}\oplus\dots\oplus L((t))e_{k_{(j,i)},i}))

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{l}\mathrm{Fil}^{k_{(j,i)}}(\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}})).

In particular observe that for all
1\leqslant j\leqslant n

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{j}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(\Lambda)/\mathrm{Fil}^{j-1}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{l}\mathrm{Fil}^{k_{(j,i)}}(\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}}))\right)/\left(\prod_{i=1}^{l}\mathrm{Fil}^{k_{(j-1,i)}}(\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(g_{i}P_{\lambda_{i}}))\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Fil}^{k_{(j,i^{\prime})}}(\sigma_{\lambda_{i^{\prime}}}(g_{i^{\prime}}P_{\lambda_{i^{\prime}}}))/\mathrm{Fil}^{k_{(j-1,i^{\prime})}}(\sigma_{\lambda_{i^{\prime}}}(g_{i^{\prime}}P_{\lambda_{i^{\prime}}}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{gr}^{k_{(j,i^{\prime})}}(\sigma_{\lambda_{i^{\prime}}}(g_{i^{\prime}}P_{\lambda_{i^{\prime}}})),

where
i^{\prime}
is such that
b_{j}=e_{k_{j,i^{\prime}}}
.
This proves that

\Lambda\in Y_{\mu}\cap\prod_{i=1}^{l}\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(GL_{n_{i}}/P_{\lambda_{i}})\Leftrightarrow\Lambda\in\prod_{i=1}^{l}(Y_{\mu_{i}}\cap\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}(GL_{n_{i}}/P_{\lambda_{i}})),

where
(\mu_{i}\colon 1\leqslant i\leqslant l)
is a permutation of
\mu
.
∎

Definition 5.24
.

Let us fix
n\geqslant 2
and
let
\mathfrak{g}_{n}
be the Lie algebra of
GL_{n}
.

(i)

We define the closed subspace

(5.11)

\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathrm{st}}\coloneqq\{(N,gP_{\mu})\in\mathfrak{g}_{n}\times\coprod_{\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}}GL_{n}/P_{\mu}\colon NgP_{\mu}\subseteq gP_{\mu}\}

of
\mathfrak{g}_{n}\times\coprod_{\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}}GL_{n}/P_{\mu}
.

(ii)

Let us fix
\lambda\in X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}
, we define the fiber product

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}_{n}\times GL_{n}/P_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathrm{st}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}_{n}\times\coprod_{\mu\in X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}}GL_{n}/P_{\mu},}

which is a closed subspace of
\mathfrak{g}_{n}\times GL_{n}/P_{\lambda}
.

Proposition 5.25
.

The codimension of
\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\lambda}
inside
\mathfrak{g}_{n}\times GL_{n}/P_{\lambda}

is at most
\dim GL_{n}/P_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

Let
(N,gP_{\lambda})\in\mathfrak{g}_{n}\times GL_{n}/P_{\lambda}
.
We have that
(N,gP_{\lambda})\in\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\lambda}
if and only if
NgP_{\lambda}\subseteq gP_{\lambda}

by construction of
\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\lambda}
;
this means that

(N,gP_{\lambda})\in\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\lambda}\Leftrightarrow g^{-1}Ng\in P_{\lambda}.

Then the space
\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\lambda}
is cut out by the condition
g^{-1}Ng\in P_{\lambda}
,
which corresponds to the equations annihilating certain entries of the matrix
g^{-1}Ng
;
the number of these equations is exactly
\dim\overline{U}_{P_{\lambda}}
,
where
\overline{U}_{P_{\lambda}}
is the
unipotent radical of the opposite parabolic of
P_{\lambda}
.
Hence we have

\operatorname{codim}\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\lambda}\leqslant\dim\overline{U}_{P_{\lambda}}=\dim GL_{n}/P_{\lambda}.

∎

Definition 5.26
.

For
w\in W_{n}
and
\lambda\in X_{*}(T_{n})^{+}
, let us define
V_{w}^{\lambda}
to be the fiber product

(5.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{w}^{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{b}_{n}\times B_{n}wP_{\lambda}/P_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}_{n}\times GL_{n}/P_{\lambda},}

where
\mathfrak{b}_{n}
is the Lie algebra of the Borel
B_{n}
of upper triangular matrices.

Remark 5.27
.

The space
V_{w}^{\lambda}
is smooth of dimension
\dim\mathfrak{b}_{n}
by
[
8
]
[Proposition 2.2.1].
As the space
\mathfrak{b}_{n}\times B_{n}wP_{\lambda}/P_{\lambda}
is smooth of dimension
\dim\mathfrak{b}_{n}+\dim B_{n}wP_{\lambda}/P_{\lambda}
,
we have that
V_{w}^{\lambda}
inside
\mathfrak{b}_{n}\times B_{n}wP_{\lambda}/P_{\lambda}
is cut out
by
\dim B_{n}wP_{\lambda}/P_{\lambda}
equations by
[
26
,
Tag 0H1G
]
.

5.6.
Almost de Rham representations

In what follows,
B_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
are topological rings for the so called "natural topology"
(
[
14
, §3.2]
).
Let us choose a compatible system of primitive
p^{n}
-th roots of unity
(\epsilon^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
;
we have
[\epsilon]\in B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
, where
[\cdot]
denotes the Teichmüller map.
Moreover
\log[\epsilon]\coloneqq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{([\epsilon]-1)^{n}}{n}
converges in
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}

and is a uniformizer; we will denote it by
t
and we have that
B_{\mathrm{dR}}\coloneqq B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}[1/t]
.
Let
C\coloneqq B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}/tB_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
be the residue field of
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
.
Recall that both the rings
B_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
are endowed with a continuous action of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.
We now recall the definition of
almost de Rham representations
as defined in

[
14
, §3.7]
.

Definition 5.28
.

(i)

A
B_{\mathrm{dR}}
-representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is a finite dimensional free
B_{\mathrm{dR}}
-module
with a continuous semi-linear action of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.
We denote by
\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
the category of
B_{\mathrm{dR}}
-represetations of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

(ii)

Let
W\in\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
, we say that
W
is
almost de Rham
if it contains
a
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-lattice
W^{+}
that is stable under
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and
such that the Sen weights of the
C
-represetation
W^{+}/tW^{+}

are all integers.
We denote by
\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{pdR}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
the full subcategory of almost de Rham representations.

Remark 5.29
.

(i)

By compactness of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, we have that any
W\in\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
contains
a
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-lattice.

(ii)

If
W
is a Hodge-Tate representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
over
C
, then the Sen weights
coincide with the Hodge-Tate weights.

We have a functor

(5.13)

\begin{split}D_{\mathrm{pdR}}:\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})&\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathbb{G}_{a})\\
W&\mapsto(B_{\mathrm{dR}}[\log t]\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}W)^{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\end{split}

defined in
[
14
, §4.3]
with the property that

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W)\leqslant\dim_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}W
.

The following Theorem is proved in
[
14
, Théorème 4.1.]

and
[
8
, Proposition 3.1.1]
.

Theorem 5.30
.

A represetation
W\in\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
is almost de Rham if and only if

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W)=\dim_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}W
.
Moreover the functor
D_{\mathrm{pdR}}
induces an equivalence of categories between

\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{pdR}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
and
\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
.

Remark 5.31
.

If
E
is a field of characteristic 0, we have that the category
\mathrm{Rep}_{E}(\mathbb{G}_{a})

is equivalent to the category whose objects are pairs
(V,N_{V})
of finite-dimensional
E
-vector
spaces
V
endowed with an
E
-linear nilpotent endomorphism
N_{V}
and whose morphisms
are linear maps of
E
-vector spaces commuting with the nilpotent endomorphism
N_{V}
.

The following is
[
8
, Proposition 3.2.1.]
.

Proposition 5.32
.

Let
W\in\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{pdR}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
. We have a bijection

\displaystyle\{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\text{-stable }B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-lattices of }W\}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\{\text{filtrations of }D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W)\}

\displaystyle W^{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W^{+}}(D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W)),

where
\mathrm{Fil}^{i}_{W^{+}}(D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W))\coloneqq(t^{i}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}[\log t]\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}}W^{+})^{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}

for
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Here a filtration of a representation
V\in\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
is a
decreasing, exhaustive and separated filtration by subobjects in
\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
.

Thanks to Remark
5.31
and Proposition
5.32
,
given
W\in\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{pdR}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
we have a bijection

(5.14)

\begin{split}\{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\text{-stable }B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-lattices of }W\}&\leftrightarrow\{N\text{-stable filtrations of }D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W)\}\\
W^{+}&\mapsto\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W^{+}}(D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W)),\end{split}

where the filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W^{+}}(D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W))
is defined as in Proposition
5.32
.

In
[
4
]
, Berger defines a topological ring
B_{e}
endowed with
a continuous
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-action and introduces the notion of
B
-pairs
,
proving that there exists an equivalence of categories of such objects with
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules.

Definition 5.33
.

A
B
-pair
is a pair
(W_{e},W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})
where
W_{e}
is a free
B_{e}
-module of
finite rank endowed with a semi-linear and continuous action of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and

W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
is a
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-lattice of
W_{\mathrm{dR}}\coloneqq B_{\mathrm{dR}}\otimes_{B_{e}}W_{e}
stable under
the action of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

The following result is
[
4
, Théorème 2.2.7]
.

Theorem 5.34
.

There is an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle W:(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\{B\text{-pairs}\}

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle\mapsto(W_{e}(D),W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)).

Moreover the definition of the map
W_{e}(D)
actually only depends on
D[1/t]
.

The functoriality of these constructions make it possible to consider representations
with different coefficients.
From now on until the end of the section, we let
A
be a finite dimensional algebra over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
;
we introduce the following notations for the
rings
B_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
with coefficients in
A
:

\begin{array}[]{cc}B_{\mathrm{dR},A}\coloneqq B_{\mathrm{dR}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A,&B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}\coloneqq B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A.\end{array}

The following notions are defined in
[
8
, Section 3.1 and 3.2]
.

Definition 5.35
.

(1)

A
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}
-representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is a
B_{\mathrm{dR}}
-representation

W
of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
together with a morphism of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebras

A\rightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})}(W)
which makes
W
a finite free

B_{\mathrm{dR},A}
-module.
We denote by
\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{\mathrm{dR},A}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
the category of
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}
-representations.

(2)

A
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is a
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-representation
W^{+}
of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}

together with a morphism of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebras
A\rightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})}(W^{+})

which makes
W^{+}
a finite free
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-module.

(3)

We say that a
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}
-representation is
almost de Rham
if the underlying

B_{\mathrm{dR}}
-representation is.
We denote by
\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{pdR},A}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
the category of almost de Rham
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}
-representations.

(4)

An
(A,B)
-pair of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is a pair
(W_{e},W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})
such that

(i)

W_{e}
is a finite
B_{e}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A
-module with a continuous semi-linear
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-action
which is free as
B_{e}
-module;

(ii)

W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\subset W_{\mathrm{dR}}\coloneqq B_{\mathrm{dR}}\otimes_{B_{e}}W_{e}
is a
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-
lattice of
W_{\mathrm{dR}}
.

We denote by
\mathrm{Rep}_{A}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
the category of pairs
(V_{A},N_{A})
where
N_{A}
is
a nilpotent endomorphism of a finite free
A
-module
V_{A}
.

The following is
[
8
, Lemma 3.1.4]
.

Theorem 5.36
.

The functor
D_{\mathrm{pdR}}
induces an equivalence of categories between

\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{pdR},A}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
and
\mathrm{Rep}_{A}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
.

By
[
8
, Lemma 3.2.2]
, we have that given
W\in\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{pdR},A}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
,
there is a bijection

(5.15)

\begin{split}\left\{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\text{-stable }B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}\text{-lattices of }W\right\}&\leftrightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}N_{A}\text{-stable exhaustive}\\
\text{filtrations of }D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W)\end{array}\right\}\\
W^{+}&\mapsto\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W^{+}}(D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W)),\end{split}

where the filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W^{+}}(D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W))
is defined as in Proposition
5.32
.

We moreover have the following result.

Theorem 5.37
.

Let
A
be a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra.
There is an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle W:(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(A,B)\text{-pairs}

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle\mapsto(W_{e}(D),W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)),

where the functor
W_{e}(D)
only depends on
D[1/t]
and

W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)\coloneqq B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{r}}D^{r}
is independent of
r
, where

r\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
such that there exists
a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D^{r}
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}
such that
D^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{A}=D
.

Nakamura proved the above Theorem when
A
is a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in
[
25
]
,
but since the same proof holds also for finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebras,
we report it here almost verbatim.

Proof.

As already stated in Theorem
5.34
,
for the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-coefficient case it was proved by Berger in
[
4
, Theorem 2.2.7]
.
Let
D
be a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}
.
First of all observe that, as
A
is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra,
we have that
\mathcal{R}_{A}=\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A
and
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}=\mathcal{R}^{r}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A
;
hence we have in particular that
D
is also a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}
and

D=D\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A
.
It is then obvious that
W_{e}(D)
is a finite
B_{e}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A
-module
with a continuous semi-linear
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-action which is also free as
B_{e}
-module.
Now let
W(D)
be the
B
-pair obtained by Theorem
5.34

and let
a\in A
; multiplication by
a
gives an endomorphism of
D
as
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
over
\mathcal{R}
.
From the functoriality, this gives an endomorphism of
W(D)
.
Thus we have shown that there is an
A
-action on
W(D)
, in particular there is
a morphism of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebras

A\rightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})}(W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)).

Hence
W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)
is a
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}

and obviously
W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)
is a
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattice of
W_{e}(D)\otimes_{B_{e}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}
.
This proves that
(W_{e}(D),W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D))
is an
(A,B)
-pair.
On the other hand, let
W=(W_{e},W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})
be an
(A,B)
-pair.
In particular notice that
W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}[1/t]=B_{\mathrm{dR}}\otimes_{Be}W_{e}
.
Then we have a morphism of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebras

A\rightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})}(W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)).

Since the action of
A
is
B_{\mathrm{dR}}
-linear, we have that the above morphism translates
into actions of
A
on the
(A,B)
-pair
W
.
Let us denote by
D(W)
the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}
obtained through the
equivalence proved by Berger.
By functoriality, this implies that there is a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-linear action of
A
on the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D(W)

over
\mathcal{R}
.
Moreover, since
D(W)
is a free module over
\mathcal{R}
, we have that

D(W)=D(W)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A
is a free module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}=\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A
.
Then the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D(W)
is a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A=\mathcal{R}_{A}
.
∎

Let us define the functor
W_{\mathrm{dR}}
as in
[
8
]
:

\displaystyle W_{\mathrm{dR}}:(\phi,\Gamma)-\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t])

\displaystyle\rightarrow\{B_{\mathrm{dR},A}\text{-representations of }G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\}

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapsto W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}}B_{\mathrm{dR},A}=W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)[1/t],

where
D
is a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}
such that
D[1/t]=M
.
It is possible to show that
W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)
doesn’t depend on
D
.

Theorem 5.38
.

Let
M
be a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]
.
We have a bijection

\displaystyle M^{(\phi,\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\text{-stable }B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}\text{-lattices of }W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)\}

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle\mapsto W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D),

where
M^{(\phi,\Gamma)}
is defined as in (
5.4
).

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be the set (
5.5
) and let

\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\left\{(W^{+},f)\colon\begin{array}[]{c}W^{+}\text{ is a }G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\text{-stable projective }B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}\text{-module and}\\
f:W^{+}[1/t]\xrightarrow{\sim}W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)\text{ isomorphism of }B_{\mathrm{dR},A}\text{-modules}\end{array}\right\};

here the
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-action on the module
W^{+}
is induced by the action on
W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)
through
the isomorphism
f
.
Observe that the equivalence of Theorem
5.37

implies that the functor
W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
induces a bijection between the set
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
of isomorphism
classes of
\mathcal{B}
and the set of isomorphism classes
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}
of

\mathcal{C}
.
In order to prove the wanted bijection, we will proceed in a very similar way to the proof
of Theorem
5.13
: we already proved
that
M^{(\phi,\Gamma)}
is in bijection with
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
, so what is left to prove
is that we have the following bijection

\displaystyle\{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\text{-stable }B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}\text{-lattices of }W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)\}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle W^{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto(W^{+},\mathrm{id}_{W^{+}[1/t]}).

For injectivity, let us take

W^{+}_{1},W^{+}_{2}\in\{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\text{-stable }B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}\text{-lattices of }W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)\}

such that
(W^{+}_{1},\mathrm{id}_{W^{+}_{1}[1/t]})\cong(W^{+}_{2},\mathrm{id}_{W^{+}_{2}[1/t]})
.
This means that there is an isomorphism
\xi:W^{+}_{1}\xrightarrow{\sim}W^{+}_{2}
of
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-modules
such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{+}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{+}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{+}_{1}[1/t]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{+}_{2}[1/t]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

commutes.
It is then easy to see that
W^{+}_{1}=W^{+}_{2}
.
Now let
(W^{+},f)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}
and consider
f(W^{+})
, which is a
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
lattice of
W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)
.
Moreover
(W^{+},f)\xrightarrow{f}(f(W^{+}),\mathrm{id}_{f(W^{+})[1/t]})
is an isomorphism in
the category
\mathcal{C}
, which proves surjectivity.
∎

Corollary 5.39
.

Let
M
be a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]
such that
W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)
is almost deRham.
We have a bijection

(5.16)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Gr}_{M}^{\Gamma}&\rightarrow\{N\text{-stable filtrations of }D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M))\}\end{split}

where

\mathrm{Gr}_{M}^{\Gamma}
is defined as in (
5.3
)
and
N
is the nilpotent endomorphism associated to
D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M))
.

Proof.

The result follows from Proposition
5.32
,
Proposition
5.13

and Theorem
5.38
.
∎

5.7.
B_{\mathrm{dR}}
-representations of non-integral weights

In this section,
A
will be an Artin local
L
-algebra, where
L
is a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
hence in particular it is a finite-dimensional
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra.
Moreover throughout this section, we fix a triangulable
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
M
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]
,

r\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
such that there exists a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
M^{r}
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]

such that
M=M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t]}\mathcal{R}_{A}
.
We fix moreover
m_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every
m\geqslant m_{0}
, the point
1-\zeta_{p^{m}}

lies in the half open annulus
\mathbb{B}^{r}
and an embedding
\tau:K_{m}\hookrightarrow L

for
m\geqslant m_{0}
.

In the previous section we have proved that if
W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)
is almost deRham,
then we have a bijection between the
\Gamma_{m}
-stable
lattices of
M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))
and the
N
-stable filtrations
of
D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M))
, as shown in Corollary
5.39
.
In this section we want to show that a similar result is true even in the case
W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)
is not
almost deRham.

First we need some preparation:
let
W_{0}^{+}
be a
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.
Let us denote by
\mathcal{A}
the set of the Sen weights of the
A\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}C
-representation

W_{0}^{+}/tW_{0}^{+}
of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.
Let us assume that
\mathcal{A}\subset A
and let us denote by
\bar{\mathcal{A}}
the set of
equivalence classes
(\mathcal{A}+\mathbb{Z})/\mathbb{Z}\subset A/\mathbb{Z}
.
Let us denote by
W
the
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}
-representation
W_{0}^{+}[1/t]
of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.
Assume that for every
a\in\mathcal{A}
there exists a continuous character

\delta:\mathbb{Q}_{p}\times\rightarrow A^{\times}
such that
\mathrm{wt}(\delta)=a
.

Remark 5.40
.

Notice that the above assumption is always satisfied in case
W_{0}^{+}=W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)

for some triangulable
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}
; in fact in this case
the Sen weights of the
A\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}C
-representation
W_{0}^{+}/tW_{0}^{+}
are exactly
the weights of the parameters of
D
.

In the following, for a continuous character
\delta:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
, we
denote by
W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\delta)\coloneqq W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\mathcal{R}_{A}(\delta))
and by

W_{\mathrm{dR}}(\delta)\coloneqq W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\delta)[1/t]
.

Theorem 5.41
.

With the same notation as above, let
W_{\bar{a}}
be the unique maximal subspace of
W

such that
W_{\bar{a}}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})\coloneqq W_{\bar{a}}\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}W_{\mathrm{dR}}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})

is almost de Rham for some continuous character
\delta_{\bar{a}}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}

with
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{\bar{a}})+\mathbb{Z}=\bar{a}
.
We have that

W=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}W_{\bar{a}}

as
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}
-representations.
Moreover if
W^{+}
is a
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattice of
W
such that
the set of mod
\mathbb{Z}
classes of the Sen weights of
W^{+}
is equal to
\bar{\mathcal{A}}

counting multiplicities, then we have

W^{+}=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}W_{\bar{a}}^{+}

where
W_{\bar{a}}^{+}\coloneqq W^{+}\cap W_{\bar{a}}
is a
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattice of
W_{\bar{a}}
.
Moreover we have that for every
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}
, the

A\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}C
-representation
W_{\bar{a}}^{+}/tW_{\bar{a}}^{+}
has Sen weights in
\bar{a}
.

The above Theorem is proved in
[
28
, § 5.2,5.3]
.

Notice then that if
W_{\bar{a}}^{+}
is a
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
with Sen weights
in
\bar{a}
and
\delta_{\bar{a}}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
is a continuous
character with
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{\bar{a}})+\mathbb{Z}=\bar{a}
,
then
W_{\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})\coloneqq W_{\bar{a}}^{+}\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}}W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})

has integral Sen weights, hence it is almost deRham.
Let
W_{\bar{a}}
be a
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}
-representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}

and let
W_{\bar{a}}^{+}
be a
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattice of
W_{\bar{a}}

as in Theorem
5.41
;
we then define

D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})\coloneqq D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W_{\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})[1/t]),

which is a finite free
A
-module endowed with an
A
-linear nilpotent endomorphism
N_{\bar{a}}
.
In
[
28
, § 5.3]
it is proved that the module
D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})

does not depend on the choice of the character
\delta_{\bar{a}}
.

For simplicity from now on, when we say that a
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattice
W^{+}

has Sen weights in
\bar{\mathcal{A}}
,
we mean that the set of mod
\mathbb{Z}
classes of the Sen weights of
W^{+}
is equal to
\bar{\mathcal{A}}

counting multiplicities.

Lemma 5.42
.

Let
W
be a
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}
-representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
such that there exists a

G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattice
W_{0}^{+}
with Sen weights in
\mathcal{A}\subset A
.
For every
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}
, let us fix a continuous character
\delta_{\bar{a}}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}

such that
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{\bar{a}})+\mathbb{Z}=\bar{a}
and let
W_{\bar{a}}
,
W_{0,\bar{a}}^{+}
as
in Theorem
5.41
.
There is a bijection

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\text{-stable }\\
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}\text{-lattices of }W\\
\text{with Sen weights in }\bar{\mathcal{A}}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left\{(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})))_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}}))\text{ is an }\\
N_{\bar{a}}\text{-stable filtration of}\\
D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})\text{ for all }\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle W^{+}=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}W_{\bar{a}}^{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W_{\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})))_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}},

where the filtrations
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W_{\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}}))

are defined as in Proposition
5.32
.

Proof.

Let us prove injectivity: assume
W_{1}^{+},W_{2}^{+}
are
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable

B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattices of
W
with Sen weights in
\mathcal{A}
such that for all
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}

\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W_{1,\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}}))=\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W_{2,\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})).

By the bijection (
5.15
),
we have that
W_{1,\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})=W_{2,\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})

for all
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}
.
Then
W_{1,\bar{a}}^{+}=W_{1,\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}}W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}})=W_{2,\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}}W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}})=W_{2,\bar{a}}^{+}
.
Moreover by Theorem
5.41
, we have

W_{1}^{+}=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}W_{1,\bar{a}}^{+}=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}W_{2,\bar{a}}^{+}=W_{2}^{+}
and this proves injectivity.
Now let
(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})))_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}

be a tuple of filtrations of
D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})
such that each filtration is stable under

N_{\bar{a}}
for every
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}
.
By the bijection (
5.15
), we have that
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}}))=\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W_{\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}}))

for some
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable

B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattice
W_{\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})
of the
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}
-representation

W_{0,\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})[1/t]
.
It is then easy to see that the tuple
(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})))_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}

is the image of
\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}W_{\bar{a}}^{+}
through the map described in
the Lemma, where
W_{\bar{a}}^{+}\coloneqq W_{\bar{a}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}}W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\delta_{\bar{a}})
.
Finally, we have that
\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}W_{\bar{a}}^{+}
is a
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable

B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattice of
W
, since
W_{\bar{a}}^{+}
is
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable for all
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}

and
W_{\bar{a}}^{+}[1/t]=W_{\bar{a}}
by construction.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.43
.

Let
D,D^{\prime}
be two triangulable
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{A}

of parameters
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})
and
(\delta_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\delta_{n}^{\prime})
.
Let
0\subset D_{1}\subset\dots\subset D_{n}
and
0\subset D_{1}^{\prime}\subset\dots\subset D_{n}^{\prime}

be the filtrations of
D
and
D^{\prime}
respectively giving rise to the parameters

\underline{\delta}
and
\underline{\delta}^{\prime}
.
If for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
we have that
D_{i}[1/t]=D_{i}^{\prime}[1/t]
, then

\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i})\equiv\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})\mod\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Observe that for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
we have that

\mathcal{R}_{A}(\delta_{i})[1/t]\cong D_{i}[1/t]/D_{i-1}[1/t]=D_{i}^{\prime}[1/t]/D_{i-1}^{\prime}[1/t]\cong\mathcal{R}_{A}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})[1/t].

As a consequence we have that
\mathcal{R}_{A}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})=t^{m_{i}}\mathcal{R}_{A}(\delta_{i})=\mathcal{R}_{A}(x^{m_{i}}\delta_{i})

for some
m_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
.
This implies that
\delta_{i}^{\prime}=x^{m_{i}}\delta_{i}
by Theorem
1.12

and thus
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})=\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i})+m_{i}\equiv\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i})\mod\mathbb{Z}
.
∎

The following result is
[
28
, Lemma A.2.1]
.

Lemma 5.44
.

Let
V
be a continuous semi-linear
K_{m}((t))
-representation of
\Gamma
and let
\gamma

be a topological generator of
\Gamma
.
Let
V^{(\gamma-1)\text{-nil}}
be the space of elements
x
in
V
such that
\gamma-1
acts
nilpotently on
x
.
There is an isomorphism of
A
-modules

\displaystyle(K_{m}[\log(t)]\otimes_{K_{m}}V)^{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\rightarrow V^{(\gamma-1)\text{-nil}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{l}a_{i}\log(t)^{i}

\displaystyle\mapsto a_{0}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{l}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{i!}\nabla^{i}(a)\log(t)^{i}

\displaystyle\mapsfrom a,

where
\nabla^{l+1}(a)=0
.

Lemma 5.45
.

Let
\Lambda
be a free
\prod_{\tau:K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-module endowed with
a
\Gamma
-action with integral Sen weights.
Then

D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(\Lambda\otimes_{K_{m}\llbracket t\rrbracket}B_{\mathrm{dR}})=(\Lambda[1/t]\otimes_{K_{m}}K_{m}[\log(t)])^{\Gamma}.

The proof of this Lemma is the same as the proof of
[
28
, Lemma A.2.5]
,
even though the statement is slightly different.

Remark 5.46
.

Let
M
be the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}[1/t]
fixed at the beginning of this Section
and let us fix a triangulable
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D
over
\mathcal{R}_{A}

such that
D[1/t]=M
.
Moreover let
\mathcal{A}
be the set of Sen weights of
W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)
and let

W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}W_{\bar{a}}

and
W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}W_{\bar{a}}^{+}
as in Theorem
5.41
.
Let
\Lambda\coloneqq D^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r},\tau}A\llbracket t\rrbracket
, then
through an argument similar to
[
28
, Remark 5.2.5]
, we can get a decomposition of
\Lambda
:
let
\nabla\coloneqq\frac{\log(\gamma^{p^{s}})}{\log(\chi(\gamma^{p^{s}}))}
for
s
big enough
be the Sen operator as in
[
3
, Proposition III.1.1]

acting on
\Lambda

such that
\nabla(t^{n})=nt^{n}
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, where
\gamma
is a topological generator of
\Gamma_{m}
.
For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that
\nabla
acts on
\Lambda/t^{n}\Lambda
,
hence we have a decomposition

\Lambda/t^{n}\Lambda=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}(\Lambda/t^{n}\Lambda)_{\bar{a}}

as
A
-modules consisting of (direct sums of) generalized eigenspaces for the
A
-linear operator
\nabla

(the generalized eigenspaces are the eigenspaces for
\nabla
seen as an endomorphism of an
L
-vector space,
but are easily seen to be stable under the
A
-action).
Since the action of
\Gamma_{m}
and
\nabla
commute, we have that each

(\Lambda/t^{n}\Lambda)_{\bar{a}}
is stable under the
\Gamma_{m}
-action,
thus
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\coloneqq\varprojlim_{n}(\Lambda/t^{n}\Lambda)_{\bar{a}}

is an
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-representation of
\Gamma_{m}
and we obviously have a decomposition

\Lambda=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}.

Notice that we can choose a basis of generalized eigenvectors for
\nabla
that respects the
triangulation of
D
.
Observe moreover that

\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}W_{\bar{a}}^{+}=W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(D)=\Lambda\otimes_{K_{m}\llbracket t\rrbracket}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}(\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\otimes_{K_{m}\llbracket t\rrbracket}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}).

It is then obvious that
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}[1/t]\otimes_{K_{m}((t))}B_{\mathrm{dR}}=W_{\bar{a}}[1/t]
by the uniqueness in Theorem
5.41
,
since they have the same Sen weights;
hence

W_{\bar{a}}^{+}=\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\otimes_{K_{m}\llbracket t\rrbracket}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}.

For simplicity of notations, let

\Lambda_{\bar{a}}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})\coloneqq\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\otimes_{A\llbracket t\rrbracket}(\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}}A\llbracket t\rrbracket)

for some character
\delta_{\bar{a}}
such that
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{\bar{a}})+\mathbb{Z}=\bar{a}
,
so that
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})
has integral Sen weights.
By Lemma
5.45
, we have

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})

\displaystyle=D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(W_{\bar{a}}\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}}W_{\mathrm{dR}}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1}))=D_{\mathrm{pdR}}(\Lambda_{\bar{a}}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})\otimes_{K_{m}\llbracket t\rrbracket}B_{\mathrm{dR}})

\displaystyle=(\Lambda_{\bar{a}}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})[1/t]\otimes_{K_{m}}K_{m}[\log(t)])^{\Gamma}.

Therefore by Lemma
5.44
, we have an isomorphism of
A
-modules

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\Lambda_{\bar{a}}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})[1/t])^{(\gamma-1)\text{-nil}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{l}a_{i}\log(t)^{i}

\displaystyle\mapsto a_{0}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{l}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{i!}\nabla^{i}(a)\log(t)^{i}

\displaystyle\mapsfrom a,

where
\nabla^{l+1}(a)=0
.
Let
\{x_{\bar{a},1}^{\prime},\dots,x^{\prime}_{\bar{a},n_{\bar{a}}}\}
be an
A
-basis of
D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})
and
let
\{y_{\bar{a},1}^{\prime},\dots,y_{\bar{a},n_{\bar{a}}}^{\prime}\}
be the
A
-basis of

(\Lambda_{\bar{a}}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})[1/t])^{(\gamma-1)\text{-nil}}

obtained as image of the basis
\{x_{\bar{a},1}^{\prime},\dots,x^{\prime}_{\bar{a},n_{\bar{a}}}\}
.
Let
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}
be the
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-module spanned by

\{y_{\bar{a},1}^{\prime},\dots,y_{\bar{a},n_{\bar{a}}}^{\prime}\}
.
By construction we have that
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}[1/t]=\Lambda_{\bar{a}}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})[1/t]
,
hence
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}
is a sub-
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-module of

M^{r}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r},\tau}A\llbracket t\rrbracket
and it is stable under the action of

\Gamma_{m}
inherited by
M^{r}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})
, as
(\gamma-1)
acts nilpotently
on the basis
\{y_{\bar{a},1}^{\prime},\dots,y_{\bar{a},n_{\bar{a}}}^{\prime}\}
.
Consider the isomorphism of
A((t))
-modules

M^{r}(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))\rightarrow M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))

and let
\{y_{\bar{a},1},\dots,y_{\bar{a},n_{\bar{a}}}\}
be the elements obtained as the image of
the
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-basis
\{y^{\prime}_{\bar{a},1},\dots,y^{\prime}_{\bar{a},n_{\bar{a}}}\}

of
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}
through the above map.
We denote by
\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
the
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-module spanned by the
elements
\{y_{\bar{a},1},\dots,y_{\bar{a},n_{\bar{a}}}\}
in
M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))
;
we then have that
\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
is
\Gamma_{m}
-stable and
\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}[1/t]=\Lambda_{\bar{a}}[1/t]
.
Let us define

\Lambda_{0}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}},

which is a
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice of
M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))
.
We have that

\{y_{\bar{a},1},\dots,y_{\bar{a},n_{\bar{a}}}\colon\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}\}

is an
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-basis of
\Lambda_{0}
and hence we can choose it as
an
A
-basis of
\Lambda_{0}/t\Lambda_{0}
.
Observe that we can then identify the two
A
-modules
\Lambda_{0}/t\Lambda_{0}
and

\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})
through the isomorphism

(5.17)

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}:\Lambda_{0}/t\Lambda_{0}&\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})\\
y_{\bar{a},i}&\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{l}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{i!}\nabla^{i}(y^{\prime}_{\bar{a},i})\log(t)^{i},\end{split}

where
\nabla^{l+1}(y^{\prime}_{\bar{a},i})=0
.
Obviously for all
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}
, we have that
\mathcal{F}
restricts to an isomorphism

\mathcal{F}:\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}}).

Lemma 5.47
.

Let
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}
be the lattice constructed in Remark
5.46

for some
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}
.
Then
\nabla:t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}\rightarrow t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}
is an
A
-linear isomorphism.

Proof.

Observe that
\nabla
acts nilpotently on the basis
\{y^{\prime}_{\bar{a},1},\dots,y^{\prime}_{\bar{a},n_{\bar{a}}}\}

of
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}
by construction.
Hence there is an
A
-basis of
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}
such that

\nabla
has upper triangular matrix with nilpotent elements on the diagonal.
Then the claim of the Lemma is a direct consequence of
[
27
, Lemma 3.1]
.
∎

Lemma 5.48
.

Let us choose a trivialization of
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}

and let
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{n_{\bar{a}}}(A)
stable under the action of
\Gamma_{m}
.
Then we have

\nabla:t^{i}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}\cap t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}\rightarrow t^{i}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}\cap t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}

is an isomorphism for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

If
i<<0
, then we have
t^{i}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}\cap t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}=t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}

and the claim is true by Lemma
5.47
.
Now it’s enough to observe that

\nabla(t^{i}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}\cap t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}})\subseteq t^{i}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}\cap t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}

for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
because both
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}
and
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}
are
\Gamma_{m}
-stable,
as showed in Remark
5.46
.
Therefore
\nabla_{|t^{i}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}\cap t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}}

is an automorphism.
∎

Lemma 5.49
.

Let
\Lambda_{0}
be the
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice of
M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))

defined in Remark
5.46
.
We have that
\Lambda_{0}
decomposes as
\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
, where
each
\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
is a free
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-module stable under the action of
\Gamma_{m}

Then the action of
\Gamma_{m}
on
\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
induces a
\Gamma_{m}
-action
on the
A
-module
\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
for all
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}
.
There exists a bijection

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\Gamma_{m}\text{-stable }A\llbracket t\rrbracket\text{-lattices}\\
\text{of }M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))\\
\text{ with Sen weights in }\bar{\mathcal{A}}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\rightleftarrows}^{f}_{g}}\left\{(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}))_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})\text{ is a}\\
\Gamma_{m}\text{-stable filtration}\\
\text{of }\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}\\
\text{for all }\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\Lambda=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\pi(\Lambda_{\bar{a}}))_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\sigma(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}))

\displaystyle\mapsfrom(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}))_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}},

where the maps
\pi
and
\sigma
are defined as in (
5.9
), while the decomposition of
\Lambda

as direct sum of
\Gamma_{m}
-stable sub-
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
-modules
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}
is given
by the same argument in Remark
5.46
.

Proof.

First of all notice that if
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{n_{\bar{a}}}^{\mu_{\bar{a}},\mathrm{rig}}(A)

is a
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice of
\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}[1/t]
,
then
\pi_{\mu_{\bar{a}}}(\Lambda_{\bar{a}})
is
\Gamma_{m}
-stable:
recall the definition of
\pi_{\mu_{\bar{a}}}(\Lambda_{\bar{a}})
in (
5.7
)
and let us fix a topological generator
\gamma
of
\Gamma_{m}
,
then we have
\gamma(\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})\subseteq\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
and
\gamma(t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})\subseteq t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}

because
\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
and
t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
are
\Gamma_{m}
-stable by Remark
5.46
.
Moreover
\gamma(t^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}})=\chi(\gamma)^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}t^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}\gamma(\Lambda_{\bar{a}})\subseteq t^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}

by the fact that
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}
is
\Gamma_{m}
-stable; therefore

\gamma(t^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\cap\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})\subseteq t^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\cap\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}

and
\gamma(t^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\cap t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})\subseteq t^{-\mu_{,\bar{a},i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\cap t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
,
hence

\gamma((t^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\cap\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})/(t^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\cap t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}))\subseteq(t^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\cap\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})/(t^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\cap t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}).

We already know that
\pi_{\mu_{\bar{a}}}\circ\sigma_{\mu_{\bar{a}}}=\mathrm{id}

because
\sigma_{\mu_{\bar{a}}}
is a section of
\pi_{\mu_{\bar{a}}}
,
which implies that
f\circ g=\mathrm{id}
.
Let now
\Lambda
be a
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice with Sen weights in
\bar{\mathcal{A}}
and
let us decompose
\Lambda
as
\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}

as in Remark
5.46
.
By the assumption of
\Lambda
having the same mod
\mathbb{Z}
classes of Sen weights as
\Lambda_{0}

counted with multiplicity, we have that
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}
is free of rank
n_{\bar{a}}

over
A\llbracket t\rrbracket
for all
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}
.
Let
\mu_{\bar{a}}\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
such that
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{n_{\bar{a}}}^{\mu_{\bar{a}},\mathrm{rig}}(A)

and let
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})=\pi_{\mu_{\bar{a}}}(\Lambda_{\bar{a}})
.
We consider
\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})
as a submodule of
\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
and we show that

\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})\subset t^{-\mu_{\bar{a},i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}
.
This will end the proof, since then we have

\sigma_{\mu_{\bar{a}}}(\pi_{\mu_{\bar{a}}}(\Lambda_{\bar{a}}))=\sum_{i}\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})\otimes_{A}t^{\mu_{\bar{a},i}}A\llbracket t\rrbracket\subseteq\Lambda_{\bar{a}};

moreover
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}
and
\sigma_{\mu_{\bar{a}}}(\pi_{\mu_{\bar{a}}}(\Lambda_{\bar{a}}))

have the same elementary divisors, hence they are exactly the same.
So let
x\in\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})=(t^{-\mu_{i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\cap\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})/(t^{-\mu_{i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\cap t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}})
.
Let us consider the isomorphism of
A((t))
-modules

M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))\xrightarrow{\sim}M(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))

and let
x^{\prime}
be the image of
x
through the above isomorphism.
As in Remark
5.46
we have that
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}

is the image of
\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
and let

\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}
be the image of
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}
through the above isomorphism.
Then
x^{\prime}\in(t^{-\mu_{i}}\Lambda^{\prime}_{\bar{a}}\cap\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}})/(t^{-\mu_{i}}\Lambda^{\prime}_{\bar{a}}\cap t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}})
,
so we can write
x^{\prime}=x_{1}^{\prime}+tx_{2}^{\prime}
where
x_{1}^{\prime}\in t^{-\mu_{i}}\Lambda^{\prime}_{\bar{a}}
and
x_{2}^{\prime}\in\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}
.
Moreover
(\gamma-1)
is nilpotent on
x^{\prime}
, since

(\gamma-1)
is nilpotent on the
A
-module
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}

by the construction in Remark
5.46

and this implies that
\nabla
is nilpotent too.
Therefore we have

0=\nabla^{k}(x^{\prime})=\nabla^{k}(x_{1}^{\prime})+\nabla^{k}(tx_{2}^{\prime})

for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
As both
\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}
and
\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}
are
\Gamma_{m}
-stable, we have that they are stable
also for the action of
\nabla
, hence

-\nabla^{k}(tx_{2}^{\prime})=\nabla^{k}(x_{1}^{\prime})\in t^{-\mu_{i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}\cap t\Lambda(\delta_{\bar{a}}^{-1})_{0,\bar{a}}
.
By Lemma
5.48
, we have
tx_{2}^{\prime}\in t^{-\mu_{i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}

and thus
x^{\prime}=x_{1}^{\prime}+tx_{2}^{\prime}\in t^{-\mu_{i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}^{\prime}
.
By definition of
x^{\prime}
, this implies that
x\in t^{-\mu_{i}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}
.
∎

Theorem 5.50
.

Let
D
be the triangulable
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}
such that
D[1/t]=M
as
fixed at the beginning of Remark
5.46
,
moreover let
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(A)
be its parameters
and let
\mathcal{A}\coloneqq\{\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i})\colon 1\leqslant i\leqslant n\}
.
Let us define the space

(5.18)

\mathrm{Gr}^{\Gamma}_{M,\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\subset\mathrm{Gr}^{\Gamma}_{M}

of
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattices in
M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r},\tau}A((t))
with Sen weights
in
\bar{\mathcal{A}}
.
For every
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}\coloneqq(\mathcal{A}+\mathbb{Z})/\mathbb{Z}
, let
W_{\bar{a}}
be the
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}
-representations of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}

such that
\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}W_{\bar{a}}=W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)
as in Theorem
5.41
.
There exists a bijection

\displaystyle\mathrm{Gr}_{M,\bar{\mathcal{A}}}^{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\left\{(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})))_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}}))\text{ is an }N_{\bar{a}}\text{-stable}\\
\text{filtration of }D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})\text{ for all }\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}\end{array}\right\},

which sends a
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice
\Lambda=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}

to
(\pi(\Lambda_{\bar{a}}))_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}
,
where
\pi(\Lambda_{\bar{a}})
is defined as in (
5.9
).
The claim makes sense, since we can identify
\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}/t\Lambda_{0,\bar{a}}
and

D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})
through the isomorphism (
5.17
).

Proof.

First of all we show that the set
\mathrm{Gr}_{M,\bar{\mathcal{A}}}^{\Gamma}
is in bijection with the
set of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattices of
W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)
with Sen weights in
\bar{\mathcal{A}}
.
We already know that there is a bijection

\mathrm{Gr}_{M}^{\Gamma}\leftrightarrow\{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\text{-stable }B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}\text{-lattices of }W_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)\}

thanks to Proposition
5.13
and Theorem
5.38
.
Then it is sufficient to show that if
\Lambda\in\mathrm{Gr}_{M,\bar{\mathcal{A}}}^{\Gamma}
, then
the corresponding
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattice has modulo
\mathbb{Z}
equivalence classes of its Sen weights in
\bar{\mathcal{A}}
.
Let
E
be the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A}
associated to
\Lambda
through the bijection of Proposition

5.13
; then the associated
B_{\mathrm{dR},A}^{+}
-lattice
is
W_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(E)=\Lambda\otimes_{K_{m}\llbracket t\rrbracket}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
, which has the same Sen weights as
\Lambda
.
By Lemma
5.42
,
this proves that the stated bijection exists and it is given by

\Lambda=\bigoplus_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\mapsto(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W_{\bar{a}}^{+}}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}})))_{\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}},

where
W_{\bar{a}}\coloneqq W_{\bar{a}}^{+}[1/t]
and

W_{\bar{a}}^{+}\coloneqq\Lambda_{\bar{a}}\otimes_{K_{m}\llbracket t\rrbracket}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
.
Now we show that for all
\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}
, the flag given by the filtration

\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{W_{\bar{a}}^{+}}(D_{\mathrm{pdR},\bar{a}}(W_{\bar{a}}))

corresponds to
\pi(\Lambda_{\bar{a}})
through the isomorphism
