F(V)\cap\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\mathrm{reg}}=\emptyset
:
if
\underline{\eta}\in F(V)\cap\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\mathrm{reg}}
, then
Z^{\prime}\cap V\neq\emptyset
,
which is a contradiction by definition of
V
.
Therefore we have that
F(V)
is closed in
\mathcal{W}^{2}
and

\dim F(V)<\dim\mathcal{W}^{2}=2.

Using Remark
6.23
, notice that for
\underline{\eta}\in F(V)
,
we have that every irreducible component of

F^{-1}(\underline{\eta})
has dimension that is at least
\dim V-\dim F(V)
.
Finally, recall that by Theorem
6.20
,

\dim Z\geqslant\dim X^{\prime}
; in particular
\dim V=\dim Z\geqslant\dim X^{\prime}=\dim F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{2})

by Remark
6.11
.
We then have

\dim F^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\geqslant\dim V-\dim F(V)>\dim V-2\geqslant\dim F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\mathrm{reg}})-2=3.

We have then a contradiction, since
\dim F^{-1}(\underline{\eta})=3
for all

\underline{\eta}\in\mathrm{im}(F)
by Theorem
6.10
.
∎

Corollary 6.25
.

The subspace
F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{2})
is dense in the irreducible component of
X
containing the point
x
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Theorem
6.24
.
∎

7.
Construction of family of extensions for trianguline

representations of higher dimension

We would like to generalize the strategy from section 5 in order for it to work
for trianguline representations of dimension
n>2
.
The goal is to prove the following Theorem.

See
B

We again want to use the following strategy:

See
3.3

Thus in the first section we will see how to construct such a space
X
.
More precisely, given a trianguline representation
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})

with parameters
\underline{\delta}\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
, we will show that there exist

•

a rigid space
X
over
L
;

•

a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}
over
X
;

•

a map
(\widetilde{\delta_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\delta_{n}}):X\rightarrow\mathcal{T}^{n}

such that there is a point
x\in X(L)
such that

\underline{\widetilde{\delta}}\widehat{\otimes}k(x)=\underline{\delta}

and
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\otimes}k(x)=\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
.
In the second section we will show that such a family has regular parameters in a dense subspace
locally at
x
.

7.1.
Construction of space of extensions

Let
n\geqslant 2
and fix
\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
a trianguline representation of
parameters
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
such that
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)
for some
i<j
.
For simplicity, let us denote by
D_{i}
the
i
-th piece of the filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho))

of
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
giving rise to the parameters
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})
.
Let
\bar{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow GL_{n}(k_{L})
be the reduction of
\rho
to the residue field of
L
.
In this section we want to construct a space
X
with a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
specializing to
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
.

The following is a generalization of Lemma
6.2
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
such that
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)

for some
i<j
and let
D_{n}
be a triangulable
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{L}

of parameters
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})
.
Let us denote by
D_{i}
the
i
-th piece of the filtration of
D_{n}
giving rise to parameters

(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})
, so that
D_{i}
is an extension of

\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{1}),\dots,\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{i})
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
There exist

(1)

characters
(\delta_{1}^{\prime},\delta_{2}^{\prime},\dots,\delta_{n}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)
where
\delta_{i}^{\prime}\in\delta_{i}x^{\mathbb{Z}}

and such that if
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})\equiv\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{j}^{\prime})\pmod{\mathbb{Z}}
for some
i,j
,
then
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})=\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{j}^{\prime})
;
moreover if
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}
, then
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})=0
;

(2)

a triangulable
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D_{n}^{\prime}
over
\mathcal{R}_{L}
of parameters
(\delta_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\delta_{n}^{\prime})

such that (if we let
D_{i}^{\prime}
denote the
i
-th piece of the filtration of
D_{n}^{\prime}
giving rise
to the parameters
(\delta_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\delta_{n}^{\prime})
)

•

\delta_{i}^{\prime}/\delta_{j}^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)
for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n
;

•

if
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(L)
then
\delta_{i}^{\prime}/\delta_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(L)
for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n
;

•

H_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{i-1}(\delta_{i}^{-1})[1/t])\cong H^{1}_{(\phi,\Gamma)}(D_{i-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{i}^{\prime-1}))

for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
.

Proof.

We will prove the statement by induction.

n=2
:

This case is proved by Lemma
6.2
.

n>2:

Assume by inductive hypothesis that there are
(\delta_{1}^{\prime},\delta_{2}^{\prime},\dots,\delta_{n-1}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(L)

such that
\delta_{i}^{\prime}/\delta_{j}^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)
for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n-1
;
there is moreover an extension
D_{n-2}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{1}^{\prime}),\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{2}^{\prime}),\dots,\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{n-2}^{\prime})

such that

(7.1)

H_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n-1}^{-1})[1/t])\cong H^{1}_{(\phi,\Gamma)}(D_{n-2}^{\prime}(\delta_{n-1}^{\prime-1})).

We consider different cases:

-

if
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}
, then let
\mu_{n}\coloneqq\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{n})
;

-

if
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{n})\notin\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}/\delta_{n})\notin\mathbb{Z}

for all
i<n
, then let
\mu_{n}\coloneqq 0
;

-

finally if
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{n})\notin\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}/\delta_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}

for some
i<n
, then let
j\coloneqq\min\{i<n\colon\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}/\delta_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}\}

and let
\mu_{n}\coloneqq-\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{j}/\delta_{n})
.

Let now
\delta_{n}^{\prime}\coloneqq\delta_{n}x^{-\mu_{n}}
.
Observe that either
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}^{\prime}/\delta_{n}^{\prime})\notin\mathbb{Z}
or
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}^{\prime}/\delta_{n}^{\prime})=0

for all
i<n
;
in particular
\delta_{i}^{\prime}/\delta_{n}^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)
and
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}^{\prime}/\delta_{n}^{\prime})\notin\mathbb{N}_{>0}

for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
.
Let us denote by
D_{n-1}^{\prime}\in H^{1}_{(\phi,\Gamma)}(D_{n-2}^{\prime}(\delta_{n-1}^{\prime-1}))
the extension
corresponding to
D_{n-1}[1/t]\in H_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n-1}^{-1})[1/t])

through the isomorphism (
7.1
).
In particular we have that
D_{n-1}[1/t]=D_{n-1}^{\prime}[1/t]
.
Then we have that

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1})[1/t])

\displaystyle=H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{-1})[1/t])=\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{k}}}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{-1}x^{-k})).

Notice that for all
k\geqslant-\mu_{n}
, we have

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1}))\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{-1}x^{-k}))

is an isomorphism thanks to Proposition
5.18
.
We then have

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1})[1/t])=\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{k}}}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{-1}x^{-k}))\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})).

Finally what is left to prove is that if
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(L)
,
then
x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(L)
for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n
.
By inductive hypothesis, this is true for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n-1
.
Now it is enough to notice that if
\delta_{i}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}
, then

\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i})\equiv\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{n})\pmod{\mathbb{Z}}
and by the way we chose
\mu_{i},\mu_{n}

we have that
\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})-\mathrm{wt}(\delta_{n}^{\prime})=0
; as a consequence,

\delta_{i}^{\prime}/\delta_{n}^{\prime}=x^{0}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}
.

∎

By Lemma
7.1
, there exists a tuple

\mu\coloneqq(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n})
of integers such that

x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(L)
,

\mathrm{wt}(x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}\delta_{j}})\notin\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}

and if
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(L)
then
x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}=x^{0}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(L)
for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n
.
Moreover there exists an extenion
D_{n}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}}),\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{2}x^{-\mu_{2}}),\dots,\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{n}x^{-\mu_{n}})

such that there are isomorphisms

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{i-1}(\delta_{i}^{-1})[1/t])\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}_{(\phi,\Gamma)}(D_{i-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{i}^{-1}x^{\mu_{i}}))

for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
, where
D_{i}^{\prime}
is the
i
-th piece of the filtration of
D_{n}^{\prime}
.
In particular we have
D_{n}^{\prime}[1/t]=\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)[1/t]
and
D_{i}^{\prime}[1/t]=D_{i}[1/t]

for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
.
We are going to generalize Proposition
6.4
in higher dimensions.

Proposition 7.2
.

There exist

•

a rigid space
X_{2}^{\prime}
over
L
which is also a vector bundle over an
appropriate neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}
of
(\delta_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\delta_{2}x^{-\mu_{2}})
in
\mathcal{T}^{2}
;

•

rigid spaces
X^{\prime}_{i}
over
L
which are also vector bundles over

X_{i-1}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{U}_{i}^{\prime}
, where
\mathcal{U}_{i}^{\prime}
is an
appropriate neighbourhood of
\delta_{i}x^{-\mu_{i}}
in
\mathcal{T}
for all
3\leqslant i\leqslant n
;

•

families of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}
over
X^{\prime}_{i}
for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
;

•

maps
(\widetilde{\delta}_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{i}^{\prime}):X_{i}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}^{i}
for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n

such that there are

(1)

x^{\prime}_{i}\in X_{i}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(x^{\prime}_{i})=D^{\prime}_{i}
and

(\widetilde{\delta}_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{i}^{\prime})\widehat{\otimes}k(x^{\prime}_{i})=(\delta_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\delta_{2}x^{-\mu_{2}},\dots,\delta_{i}x^{-\mu_{i}})

for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
;

(2)

Zariski-open dense subsets
U^{\prime}_{i}\xhookrightarrow{j_{i}}X^{\prime}_{i}
such that
j_{i}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}

has a filtration of sub-
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules with graded pieces
\mathcal{R}_{U^{\prime}_{i}}(\widetilde{\delta_{1}}^{\prime}),\dots,\mathcal{R}_{U^{\prime}_{i}}(\widetilde{\delta}_{i}^{\prime})
and
j_{i}^{*}(\widetilde{\delta_{1}}^{\prime},\dots,\widetilde{\delta_{i}}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{i}(U^{\prime}_{i}).

Proof.

The statement descends directly from Proposition
4.5
.
∎

Remark 7.3
.

Notice that by Lemma
1.16
, we can choose the neighbourhoods
\mathcal{U}_{i}^{\prime}

of
\delta_{i}x^{-\mu_{i}}
in such a way so that for any

u\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime}(K)\coloneqq\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{1}(K)\times\mathcal{U}_{2}^{\prime}(K)\times\dots\times\mathcal{U}_{n}^{\prime}(K)
,
we have

•

(\widetilde{\delta_{i}}^{\prime}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}^{\prime})\widehat{\otimes}k(u)=x^{a_{ij}}
for some
a_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}

only if
x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}=x^{a_{ij}}
;

•

(\widetilde{\delta_{i}}^{\prime}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}^{\prime})\widehat{\otimes}k(u)=\chi x^{a_{ij}}
for some
a_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}

only if
x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}=\chi x^{a_{ij}}
.

As in the two-dimensional case, by Theorem
5.6
, there exist
r_{0}\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
and
a unique
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D^{r_{0}}_{n}
over
\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r_{0}}
such that
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)=D^{r_{0}}_{n}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{r_{0}}_{L}}\mathcal{R}_{L}
;
moreover there is
r_{1}\in p^{\mathbb{Q}}\cap[0,1)
such that
the family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{n}
can be already defined over
\mathcal{R}_{X^{\prime}_{n}}^{r_{1}}
.
Let us denote by
r
the maximum of
r_{0}
and
r_{1}
, in such a way that both
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)

and
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{n}
are defined over
\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}
and
\mathcal{R}_{X^{\prime}_{n}}^{r}
respectively.
In other words, there exist
D^{r}_{n}
a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}^{r}_{L}
such that

\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)=D^{r}_{n}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{r}_{L}}\mathcal{R}_{L}
and a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathbb{D}^{\prime r}_{n}

over
\mathcal{R}_{X^{\prime}_{n}}^{r}
such that

\mathbb{D}^{\prime r}_{n}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{X_{n}^{\prime}}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{X_{n}^{\prime}}=\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{n}
.
We also denote by
D_{i}^{r}
and
D^{\prime r}_{i}
the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}
such that

D_{i}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{L}=D_{i}
and
D^{\prime r}_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{L}=D^{\prime}_{i}

for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
.
Finally for any
2\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
,
we let
\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime r}
be the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{X_{i}^{\prime}}
such that

\mathbb{D}^{\prime r}_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{X_{i}^{\prime}}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{X_{i}^{\prime}}=\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i}
.
Let
m(r)
be the smallest positive integer such that
p^{m-1}(p-1)\geqslant r
for all
m\geqslant m(r)
,
so that all the points of the form
1-\zeta_{p^{m}}
(where
\zeta_{p^{m}}
is a
p^{m}
-th root of unity)
lie in the open annulus
\mathbb{B}^{r}
.
Let us fix
m\geqslant m(r)
and let us assume that
L
contains
K_{m}

(by Lemma
3.4
we know we can always make this assumption).
As a consequence of Theorem
5.11
, the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D^{r}_{i}
corresponds to the
tuple
(D^{r}_{i}[1/t],D^{r}_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}L\llbracket t\rrbracket,f_{i})
through
the equivalence
5.2
for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, where

f_{i}:D^{r}_{i}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{r}_{L}[1/t]}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}L((t))\xrightarrow{\sim}(D^{r}_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}L\llbracket t\rrbracket)[1/t]

is defined as in Theorem
5.11
.
Let us denote by
\Lambda_{i}
the
\Gamma
-stable finite projective module

D^{r}_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}L\llbracket t\rrbracket

for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
By Remark
5.12
, we have

\Lambda_{i}=\prod_{K_{m}\xhookrightarrow{\tau}L}\Lambda_{i,\tau}

where
\Lambda_{i,\tau}
is a
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice over
L\llbracket t\rrbracket
.
On the other hand, given such a lattice
\Lambda_{i,\tau}
, it is possible to recover
\Lambda_{i}
.
From now on, since the two notions are equivalent,
we fix an embedding
\tau:K_{m}\hookrightarrow L
and we will denote
by
\Lambda_{i}
the
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice
\Lambda_{i,\tau}
over
L\llbracket t\rrbracket

for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
In the same way, let
\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}
be the
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice over

L\llbracket t\rrbracket
corresponding to the
\tau
-factor of the lattice associated to
D^{\prime r}_{i}
.
Let us denote by
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}_{i}
the
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice corresponding to the
factor
\tau:K_{m}\hookrightarrow L
of the lattice
\mathbb{D}^{\prime r}_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{r}_{X_{i}^{\prime}}}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}R^{\prime}_{i}\llbracket t\rrbracket
associated to
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i}
for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
After choosing a basis of
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i}
,
we choose a trivialization of
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}_{i}
and fix a basis of
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}_{i}

respecting the triangulation of
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i}
for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
.

We then have the following result.

Lemma 7.4
.

\Lambda_{i}\in Y_{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{i})}(L)
for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
.

Proof.

We have to show that

\mathrm{gr}^{j}(\Lambda_{i})=t^{\mu_{j}}\cdot\mathrm{gr}^{j}(\Lambda_{i}^{\prime})
for all
1\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
Let us denote by
\delta_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\delta_{i}x^{-\mu_{i}}
.
Since we chose a basis of
\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}
respecting the triangulation of
D^{\prime}_{i}
, we have that

\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(L((t))^{i})

is induced by the fltration of
D^{\prime}_{i}[1/t]
.
This implies that

\mathrm{gr}^{j}(\Lambda_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}(\delta_{j}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r},\tau}L\llbracket t\rrbracket

for all
1\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
Let us denote by
V_{i}
the module
L((t))^{i}
for simplicity.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{1}(\Lambda_{i})

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{1}(V_{i})=\Lambda_{i}\cap\left(\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}(\delta_{1})[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{r}_{L}[1/t],\tau}L((t))\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}(\delta_{1})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r},\tau}L\llbracket t\rrbracket=t^{\mu_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}(x^{-\mu_{1}}\delta_{1})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r},\tau}L\llbracket t\rrbracket=t^{\mu_{1}}\mathrm{gr}^{1}(\Lambda_{i}^{\prime})

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{j}(\Lambda_{i})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(\Lambda_{i})/\mathrm{Fil}^{j-1}(\Lambda_{i})=(\Lambda_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(V_{i}))/(\Lambda_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{j-1}(V_{i}))

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
Notice that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(V_{i})

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{i}\cap(D_{j}^{\prime r}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}[1/t],\tau}L((t)))=\Lambda_{i}\cap(D_{j}^{r}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}[1/t],\tau}L((t)))

\displaystyle=D_{j}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r},\tau}L\llbracket t\rrbracket

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{j}(\Lambda_{i})

\displaystyle=(\Lambda_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(V_{i}))/(\Lambda_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{j-1}(V_{i}))=(D_{j}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r},\tau}L\llbracket t\rrbracket)/(D_{j-1}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r},\tau}L\llbracket t\rrbracket)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}(\delta_{j})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r},\tau}L\llbracket t\rrbracket=\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}(\delta_{j}^{\prime}x^{\mu_{j}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r},\tau}L\llbracket t\rrbracket

\displaystyle=t^{\mu_{j}}\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r}(\delta_{j}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{L}^{r},\tau}L\llbracket t\rrbracket=t^{\mu_{j}}\mathrm{gr}^{j}(\Lambda_{i}^{\prime})

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
∎

Just like in the two-dimensional case we will construct a subspace of
X_{n}^{\prime}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}

(where
X_{n}^{\prime}
is the space defined in Proposition
7.2
)
consisting of
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattices.
Again, let us denote by
\gamma
a choice of a topological generator of
\Gamma_{m}
.

Definition 7.5
.

For all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
, let us define the map

\gamma\cdot:X^{\prime}_{i}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{rig}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{rig}}
in the following way:
for any affinoid
L
-algebra
A
and for any
(y^{\prime},\Lambda_{A})\in(X_{i}^{\prime}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{rig}})(A)
, let

\gamma\cdot(y^{\prime},\Lambda_{A})\coloneqq\gamma\cdot\Lambda_{A},

where the action of
\gamma
on
\Lambda_{A}
is induced from the
action of
\Gamma_{m}
on
y^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}
through the isomorphism

(y^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime r})[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A}^{r}[1/t],\tau}A((t))\cong\Lambda_{A}[1/t].

Notice that
\gamma\cdot\Lambda_{A}\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{i}(A)
, because
\gamma
induces an automorphism on

(y^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime r})[1/t]
, hence the map is well-defined.

Theorem 7.6
.

For all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
there exist

•

an affinoid rigid space
X_{i}=\mathrm{Sp}(R_{i})
over
L
;

•

a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{i}
over
X_{i}
;

•

a map
(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{i}):X_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}^{i}
which consists of parameters for
\mathbb{D}_{i}

such that:

(1)

there is
x_{i}\in X_{i}
such that
\mathbb{D}_{i}\widehat{\otimes}k(x_{i})=D_{i}
and

(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{i})\widehat{\otimes}k(x_{i})=(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{i})
;

(2)

(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{i})=(x^{\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\delta}_{1}^{\prime},\dots,x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\delta}_{i}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

The proof of this result is completely analogous to the proof of Theorem
6.8
,
thus we basically repeat the same proof, changing just a few details.

Let us define the ind-rigid space
Y_{i}
to be the fiber product

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{i}\coloneqq X^{\prime}_{i}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{i}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{i}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{i}}\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}_{i}^{\mathrm{rig}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{i}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{i}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\gamma\cdot{,}\mathrm{pr})}

where the rightmost map
\delta
is the diagonal map.
Notice that for any
((y^{\prime},\Lambda_{A}),\Lambda^{\prime}_{A})\in Y_{i}(A)
, we have

(\gamma\cdot(y^{\prime},\Lambda_{A}),\Lambda_{A})=(\Lambda_{A}^{\prime},\Lambda_{A}^{\prime})
.
Since
\Lambda_{i}
is stable under the action of
\Gamma_{m}
induced by the point
x_{i}^{\prime}
,
we have that
x_{i}\coloneqq((x_{i}^{\prime},\Lambda_{i}),\Lambda_{i})\in Y_{i}(L)
.
We define
X_{i}
to be the fiber product

X_{i}\coloneqq Y_{i}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{i}}Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{i})};

we have then that
x_{i}\in X_{i}(L)
because of Lemma
7.4
and,
eventually restricting
X_{i}
, we can assume that
X_{i}
is an affinoid neighbourhood
\mathrm{Sp}(R_{i})
of
x_{i}
.
Consider the pullback
p_{2}^{*}(\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime},\mathbb{L}_{i})
to
X_{i}
(where
\mathbb{L}_{i}
is the universal lattice on
\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{i}
)
and the isomorphism of
\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}R_{i}((t))
-modules

\widetilde{f}_{i}:p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime r}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}[1/t]}\prod_{K_{m}\hookrightarrow L}R_{i}((t))\xrightarrow{\sim}\left(\prod_{k}g_{m}^{k}\cdot p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i}\right)[1/t]

defined as in Theorem
5.11
, where
g_{m}
is a generator of
\mathrm{Gal}(K_{m}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})

and
k
ranges over
\{0,\dots,|\mathrm{Gal}(K_{m}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})|-1\}
.
Let us denote by
\mathbb{D}_{i}
the sheaf

\left(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i},p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i},\widetilde{f}_{i}\right)

on
X_{i}
, which can be seen as a sheaf of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules thanks to the equivalence
5.2
;
then we have that
\mathbb{D}_{i}\widehat{\otimes}k(x_{i})=(D^{r}_{i}[1/t],\Lambda_{i},f_{i})
.
We have therefore constructed a space
X_{i}
with a sheaf
\mathbb{D}_{i}
specializing at some point
x_{i}\in X_{i}
to

(D_{i}^{r}[1/t],\Lambda_{i},f_{i})
, which corresponds to the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
D_{i}
through
the equivalence
5.2
.
Moreover the family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{i}
is obviously already defined over
\mathcal{R}_{X_{i}}^{r}
,
as
\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}
is;
hence there is a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{i}^{r}
over
\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}
such that

\mathbb{D}_{i}=\mathbb{D}_{i}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}}\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}
.
Notice that the sheaf of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{i}
comes with a filtration of sub-
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules,
which is the one induced by the filtration of sub-
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules of
\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}
.
More precisely, for any affinoid
L
-algebra
A
and for
y=((y^{\prime},\Lambda_{A}),\Lambda_{A})\in X_{i}(A)
, we have

\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(y^{*}\mathbb{D}_{i})=y^{*}\mathbb{D}_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(y^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}[1/t]).

Recall that
\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}[1/t]=\mathbb{D}_{i}[1/t]
by construction, so the intersection above makes sense.
Moreover notice that
\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(y^{*}\mathbb{D}_{i})
is stable under
\Phi
and
\Gamma
,
since both
y^{*}\mathbb{D}_{i}
and
\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(y^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}[1/t])
are.
Finally, by Lemma
5.20
we have that
\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(y^{*}\mathbb{D}_{i})
is locally free.
Therefore
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}(y^{*}\mathbb{D}_{i})
provides a triangulation of
\mathbb{D}_{i}

and hence it gives parameters
(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{i})\in\mathcal{T}^{i}(X_{i})
for the family
\mathbb{D}_{i}
.
The last thing left to show is that

(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{i})=(x^{\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\delta}_{1}^{\prime},\dots,x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\delta}_{i}^{\prime})
.
We claim that

\mathrm{gr}^{j}(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i})=\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}(\widetilde{\delta}_{j})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r},\tau}R_{i}\llbracket t\rrbracket

for all
1\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
Let
q:X_{i}\rightarrow X_{i}^{\prime}
be the projection map; since we fixed a basis
of the standard lattice
\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\prime}
over
R_{i}^{\prime}
respecting
the triangulation of
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(R_{i}((t))^{i})

\displaystyle=q^{*}\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(R^{\prime}_{i}((t))^{i})=q^{*}(\mathbb{D}^{\prime r}_{j}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}^{\prime}}^{r}[1/t],\tau}R_{i}^{\prime}((t)))

\displaystyle=q^{*}\mathbb{D}_{j}^{\prime r}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}[1/t],\tau}R_{i}((t)).

Thus

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{1}(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Fil}^{1}(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i})=p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{1}(R_{i}((t))^{i})

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{D}_{i}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r},\tau}R_{i}\llbracket t\rrbracket\right)\cap\left(\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}(\widetilde{\delta}_{1}^{\prime})[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}[1/t],\tau}R_{i}((t))\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}(\widetilde{\delta}_{1})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r},\tau}R_{i}\llbracket t\rrbracket

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{j}(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i})/\mathrm{Fil}^{j-1}(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i})

\displaystyle=(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(R_{i}((t))^{i}))/(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{j-1}(R_{i}((t))^{i}))

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
Notice that

\displaystyle p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(R_{i}((t))^{i})

\displaystyle=p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i}\cap(q^{*}\mathbb{D}_{j}^{\prime r}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}[1/t],\tau}R_{i}((t)))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{D}_{j}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r},\tau}R_{i}\llbracket t\rrbracket

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{j}(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i})

\displaystyle=(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{j}(R_{i}((t))^{i}))/(p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{j-1}(R_{i}((t))^{i}))

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{D}_{j}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r},\tau}R_{i}\llbracket t\rrbracket)/(\mathbb{D}_{j-1}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r},\tau}R_{i}\llbracket t\rrbracket)=\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}(\widetilde{\delta}_{j})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r},\tau}R_{i}\llbracket t\rrbracket

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
Restricting ourselves to lattices in
Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{i})}
as in the definition of the space
X_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}(\widetilde{\delta}_{j})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r},\tau}R_{i}\llbracket t\rrbracket

\displaystyle=t^{\mu_{j}}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}(\widetilde{\delta}_{j}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r},\tau}R_{i}\llbracket t\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r}(x^{\mu_{j}}\widetilde{\delta}_{j}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{R_{i}}^{r},\tau}R_{i}\llbracket t\rrbracket

for all
1\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant n
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Exactly like in the case of dimension two, we have constructed a rigid space
X_{n}
over
L

and a family of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{n}
specializing to
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{rig}}^{{\dagger}}(\rho)
at some point
x_{n}\in X_{n}
.

Remark 7.7
.

Notice that the above construcion works in any dimension
n
and that for any

3\leqslant i\leqslant n
, we have
\mathbb{D}_{i}\in H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathbb{D}_{i-1}(\widetilde{\delta}_{i}^{-1}))
.

We still have to show that the family
\mathbb{D}_{n}
is regular in a dense subspace of a
neighbourhood of
x_{n}\in X_{n}(L)
. As in the two-dimensional case, the space
X_{n}
is not
a vector bundle over an open of
\mathcal{T}^{n}
, hence we are again going to use a more elaborate
argument to be able to conclude.

7.2.
Density of the regular locus

In order to simplify the notation, we denote by
G
the map

(\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\delta}_{n}):X_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}^{n}

sending a point
y_{n}
of
X_{n}
to
the parameters associated to the filtration of
y_{n}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{n}
.
Moreover, let

H_{n}:\mathcal{T}^{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}^{n},(\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})\mapsto(\widetilde{\eta}_{1|\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n|\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}})

be the map sending continuous characters of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
to their restriction to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.
Finally, let

(7.2)

F:X_{n}\xrightarrow{G}\mathcal{T}^{n}\xrightarrow{H_{n}}\mathcal{W}^{n}

be the composition of the maps
G
and
H_{n}
.
To be able to prove the density of the regular locus, we need again to use some argument involving
the dimension of the preimage through
F
of certain subspaces of
\mathcal{W}^{n}
.

First of all we need the following results.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
K
be an extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
D
be a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{K}
such that there exists a
short exact sequence

0\rightarrow E\rightarrow D\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta)\rightarrow 0

for some
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
E
over
\mathcal{R}_{K}
and for some continuous character
\delta:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow K^{\times}

such that
H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta))\neq 0
.
If the induced map
H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta))
is surjective,
then
D[1/t]=E[1/t]\oplus\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta)[1/t]
as
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module.

Proof.

Let us denote by
\widetilde{p}
the surjection of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules

\widetilde{p}:D\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta)

and by
p
the restriction of the map
\widetilde{p}
to

\widetilde{p}^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{K}\cdot H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta)))
,
so that

p:\widetilde{p}^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{K}\cdot H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta)))\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{R}_{K}\cdot H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta)).

We get the following map of short exact sequences:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{K}\cdot H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}_{K}\cdot H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

where
T
is some torsion
\mathcal{R}_{K}
-module.
Let
d\in H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)
such that
\widetilde{p}(d)\neq 0
, which exists by the hypothesis that

\widetilde{p}:H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta))
is surjective.
Since
E=\ker(\widetilde{p})
and
H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta))
is a 1-dimensional vector
space over
K
, we have that

\widetilde{p}^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{K}\cdot H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta)))=E\oplus\mathcal{R}_{K}\cdot d

as a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{K}
.
Observe in fact that
E
is obviously stable under the action of
\Phi
and
\Gamma
,
since it is a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module itself; on the other hand,
\mathcal{R}_{K}\cdot d
is also
stable under
\Phi
and
\Gamma
, since
d\in H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D)
and
the 0-th
(\phi,\Gamma)
-cohomology is the submodule of fixed elements by
\Phi
and
\Gamma
by definition.
After inverting
t
, the torsion
\mathcal{R}_{K}
-module
T[1/t]
gets annihilated, hence (since localization is flat)
we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E[1/t]\oplus(\mathcal{R}_{K}\cdot d)[1/t]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D[1/t]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}_{K}[1/t]\cdot H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta)[1/t],}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the horizontal arrows are isomorphisms of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules.
∎

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
K
be an extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
D_{n},D_{n}^{\prime}
be two triangulable
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{K}
of the same rank.
Let us denote by
D_{i}
and
D_{i}^{\prime}
the
i
-th piece of the filtration of
D_{n}
and
D_{n}^{\prime}
respectively
and let
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}),(\delta_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\delta_{n}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(K)
be the parameters
of
D_{n}
and
D_{n}^{\prime}
associated to the fixed filtration respectively.
Assume that

(1)

\delta_{i}^{\prime}=\delta_{i}x^{-\mu_{i}}
for some
\mu_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
;

(2)

if
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)
, then
\delta_{i}^{\prime}/\delta_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)
for all
i<j
;

(3)

D_{i}[1/t]=D_{i}^{\prime}[1/t]
for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
;

(4)

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{i}^{\prime}(\delta_{j}^{\prime-1})[1/t])\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{i}^{\prime}(\delta_{j}^{\prime-1}))

for all
i<j
.

Then

\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})).

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
.

n=2:

We distinguish two cases:

–

if
\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)
, then
\delta_{1}^{\prime}/\delta_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)
and we have

\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}))=1=\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{1}^{\prime}/\delta_{2}^{\prime})).

–

If
\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}\notin\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)
, then

\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}))=0\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{1}^{\prime}/\delta_{2}^{\prime})).

n>2:

We consider two different cases too:

–

First assume that

\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))=\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))
.
By inductive hypothesis we have that

\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D^{\prime}_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1}))

so we have

\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D^{\prime}_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})).

On the other hand, from the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow D_{n-2}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})\rightarrow D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}^{\prime}/\delta_{n}^{\prime})\rightarrow 0,

we get the long exact sequence

0\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D^{\prime}_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1}))\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D^{\prime}_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1}))\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}^{\prime}/\delta_{n}^{\prime}))\rightarrow\dots.

Hence we have

\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D^{\prime}_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1}))\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D^{\prime}_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})).

Combining all of the inequalities above, we get

\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})).

–

If instead
\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\neq\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))
,
then by the long exact sequence

0\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\rightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}))\rightarrow\dots,

we must have that
\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}))\neq 0

and the map

H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\twoheadrightarrow H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}))

is surjective, in fact
\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}))=1
.
By Lemma
7.8
,
D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1})[1/t]
is the split extension in

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n-1}^{-1})[1/t])
.
We moreover have that by hypothesis

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n-1}^{-1})[1/t])\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}^{\prime}(\delta_{n-1}^{\prime-1})[1/t])\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}^{\prime}(\delta_{n-1}^{\prime-1})).

We have that
D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})
is the image of
D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1})[1/t]

through the above isomorphism, hence this in particular implies that

D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})=D_{n-2}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})\oplus\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}^{\prime}/\delta_{n}^{\prime})

is also split.
Observe that

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1}))

\displaystyle=(D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1}))^{(\Phi,\Gamma)}

\displaystyle=(D_{n-2}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1}))^{(\Phi,\Gamma)}\oplus(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}^{\prime}/\delta_{n}^{\prime}))^{(\Phi,\Gamma)}

\displaystyle=H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1}))\oplus H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}^{\prime}/\delta_{n}^{\prime})).

By the second hypothesis, we have that

\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}))\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{i}^{\prime}/\delta_{j}^{\prime}))

for all
i<j
.
Finally, by inductive hypothesis we have

\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D^{\prime}_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})),

thus

\displaystyle\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))

\displaystyle=\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))+\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}))

\displaystyle\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D^{\prime}_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1}))+\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}^{\prime}/\delta_{n}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}^{\prime-1})).

∎

Lemma 7.10
.

Let
y=(y^{\prime},\Lambda_{y})\in X_{n}(K)
for some extension
K
of
L
.
We have that the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{n}\widehat{\otimes}k(y)

and
\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime})
satisfy the hypothesis 1,2 and 3 of Lemma
7.9
.

Proof.

(1)

Let us denote by
(\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}),(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{T}^{n}(K)

the parameters of
\mathbb{D}_{n}\widehat{\otimes}k(y)
and
\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime})
respectively.
By Theorem
7.6
we have that

\widetilde{\eta}_{i}=\widetilde{\delta}_{i}\widehat{\otimes}k(y)=x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\delta}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime})=x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime}

for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
.

(2)

Let us assume that
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=x^{-a}
for some
a\in\mathbb{N}
and for some
i<j
.
Then
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\prime}=\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}\cdot x^{-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}=x^{-a-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}
.
Recall that by Lemma
1.16
we chose the neighbourhood
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime}

of
(\delta_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\dots,\delta_{n}x^{-\mu_{n}})
in such a way so that

\widetilde{\delta}_{i}^{\prime}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(u)=x^{a_{ij}}
for some
a_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
only if

x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}=x^{a_{ij}}
.
Thus we have that
x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}=x^{-a-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}
, which implies that

\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=x^{-a}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(L)
.
By Lemma
7.1
, we have if
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(L)

then
x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}=x^{0}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(L)
, hence
-a-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}=0
.
This concludes that
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\prime}=x^{-a-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)
.

(3)

Let
E_{i}\coloneqq\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\mathbb{D}_{n}\widehat{\otimes}k(y))
and
E_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}))
.
We have by definition

\displaystyle E_{i}

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{D}_{n}\widehat{\otimes}k(y))\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{i}((\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}))[1/t])

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{D}_{n}\widehat{\otimes}k(y))\cap E_{i}^{\prime}[1/t].

Hence

E_{i}[1/t]=(\mathbb{D}_{n}\widehat{\otimes}k(y))[1/t]\cap E_{i}^{\prime}[1/t]=E_{i}^{\prime}[1/t]

because
(\mathbb{D}_{n}\widehat{\otimes}k(y))[1/t]=(\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}))[1/t]
.

∎

Recall the definition of the open neighbourhoods
\mathcal{U}_{i}^{\prime}
of
\delta_{i}x^{-\mu_{i}}
as
in Proposition
7.2
.
Notice that the map
G:X_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}^{n}
factors through

G:X_{n}\xrightarrow{G_{n}}X_{n-1}\times\mathcal{U}_{n}\xrightarrow{G_{n-1}}\dots\xrightarrow{G_{3}}X_{2}\times\mathcal{U}_{3}\times\dots\times\mathcal{U}_{n}\xrightarrow{G_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\coloneqq\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\dots\times\mathcal{U}_{n}

where the spaces
\mathcal{U}_{i}\coloneqq x^{\mu_{i}}\mathcal{U}_{i}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{T}

are the open subspaces defined by the characters
\widetilde{\delta}_{i}
, as in Theorem
7.6
.
From now on, for all
3\leqslant i\leqslant n
we denote by
d_{i}

the dimension of the vector bundle
X_{i}^{\prime}
over
X_{i-1}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{U}_{i}^{\prime}

and by
d_{2}
the dimension of the vector bundle
X_{2}^{\prime}
over
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{2}
.
As explained in Theorem
4.2
, we have that
d_{i}\coloneqq\dim_{L}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{i-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{i}^{-1}x^{\mu_{i}}))

and
d_{2}\coloneqq\dim_{L}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}}/\delta_{2}x^{-\mu_{2}}))
.

Definition 7.11
.

We can partition the space
\mathcal{T}^{n}

into subspaces consisting of
(\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}

satisfying compatible conditions of the form

\begin{array}[]{lcr}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=x^{-b_{ij}},&\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=\chi x^{b_{ij}},&\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}\end{array}

for some
b_{ij}\in\mathbb{N}
.
More precisely, for any
2\leqslant j\leqslant n
, let us fix
\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j}\subset\{1,\dots,j-1\}
.
Moreover for every
i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}\cup\mathcal{M}_{j}
we fix
b_{ij}\in\mathbb{N}
.
We define

(7.3)

\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}\coloneqq\left\{\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in\mathcal{T}^{n}\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=x^{-b_{ij}}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{L}_{j},\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=\chi x^{b_{ij}}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}\\
\text{ and }\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}\Leftrightarrow i\notin\mathcal{L}_{j}\cup\mathcal{M}_{j}\end{array}\right\}.

We will now give a bound for the dimension of the preimage through
G
of the
spaces of the form
\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}
.
We need first some preliminary results.

Lemma 7.12
.

Let
K
be an extension of
L
.
We have that
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}(K)

is covered by spaces of the form
\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}

where
\mathcal{L}_{j}\subseteq\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}
,

\mathcal{M}_{j}\subseteq\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}\}

and
a_{ij}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=x^{-a_{ij}}
or
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=\chi x^{a_{ij}}
.

Proof.

We only need to show that for
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}(K)

we have

\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=x^{-b_{ij}}\text{ for some }b_{ij}\in\mathbb{N}\Rightarrow\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=x^{-b_{ij}}

and

\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=\chi x^{b_{ij}}\text{ for some }b_{ij}\in\mathbb{N}\Rightarrow\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=\chi x^{b_{ij}}.

Notice that

\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=x^{-b_{ij}}\Leftrightarrow x^{-\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=x^{-b_{ij}-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}},

which implies by Remark
7.3
that

x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}=x^{-b_{ij}-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}\Leftrightarrow\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=x^{-b_{ij}}.

On the other hand,

\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=\chi x^{b_{ij}}\Leftrightarrow x^{-\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=\chi x^{b_{ij}-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}.

Observe that in the proof of Proposition
4.5
, we have chosen
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}

in such a way so that for every
i<j
and every
u\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}
we have

(\widetilde{\delta}_{i}^{\prime}/\widetilde{\delta}_{j}^{\prime})\widehat{\otimes}k(u)\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}
.
Hence
b_{ij}-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}<0
, which implies always by Remark
7.3
that

x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}=\chi x^{b_{ij}-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}\Leftrightarrow\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=\chi x^{b_{ij}}.

∎

Lemma 7.13
.

Let
y_{i}^{\prime}=(y_{i-1}^{\prime},\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime},e)\in X_{i}^{\prime}(K)=(X_{i-1}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{U}_{i}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{d_{i}})(K)

for some extension
K
of
L
(when
i=2
, we let
y_{i-1}^{\prime}\coloneqq x^{-\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
and
X_{i-1}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}
).
Let us denote by
E_{i}^{\prime}
and
E_{i-1}^{\prime}
the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules
\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i}^{\prime})

and
\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i-1}^{\prime})
respectively.
If
i=2
, then we denote by
\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}
the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{1}}(x^{-\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\delta}_{1})
.
We have that

\displaystyle\dim_{K}(\{z_{i}^{\prime}\in X_{i}^{\prime}(K)\colon\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(z_{i}^{\prime})=E_{i}^{\prime}\})=d_{i}-\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime-1})).

Proof.

Notice that

\displaystyle\{z_{i}^{\prime}\in X_{i}^{\prime}(K)\colon\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(z_{i}^{\prime})=E_{i}^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle=\{(y_{i-1}^{\prime},\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime},e^{\prime})\in X_{i}^{\prime}(K)\colon\Psi_{K}(y_{i-1}^{\prime},\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime},e^{\prime})=E_{i}^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle=(y_{i-1}^{\prime},\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime})\times\{e^{\prime}\in\mathbb{A}^{d_{i}}_{K}\colon\Psi_{K}(y_{i-1}^{\prime},\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime},e^{\prime})=E_{i}^{\prime}\},

where
\Psi_{K}
is the surjective map for
X_{i}^{\prime}
defined in Theorem
4.2
.
Thus

\displaystyle\dim_{K}(\{z_{i}^{\prime}\in X_{i}^{\prime}(K)\colon\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(z_{i}^{\prime})=E_{i}^{\prime}\})

\displaystyle=\dim_{K}\ker(\mathbb{A}_{K}^{d_{i}}\xrightarrow{\Psi_{K}}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime-1})))

\displaystyle=d_{i}-\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime-1})).

∎

Proposition 7.14
.

For
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
, let
(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})\in\mathrm{im}(G_{i})(K)
for some extension
K
of
L

(if
i=2
, then
y_{i-1}=\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\in\mathcal{T}(K)
).
We have

\displaystyle\dim_{K}G_{i}^{-1}(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})

\displaystyle=\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i-1}))(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

\displaystyle+d_{i}-\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1}))(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1})).

When
i=2
, we put
\mathbb{D}_{i-1}=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}}(\widetilde{\delta}_{1})

and
\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}}(x^{-\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\delta}_{1})
.

Proof.

For simplicity, let us denote by
E_{i-1}\coloneqq\mathbb{D}_{i-1}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i-1})

and
E_{i-1}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1})

(in case
i=2
, then
E_{i-1}=\mathcal{R}_{K}(\widetilde{\eta}_{1})

and
E_{i-1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{R}_{K}(x^{-\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1})
).
We then have that the fiber
G_{i}^{-1}(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})
is

\displaystyle\left\{(y_{i}^{\prime},\Lambda_{y_{i}})\in X_{i}^{\prime}\times Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{i})}\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i}^{\prime})\in H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))\text{ and}\\
\Lambda_{y_{i}}\text{ is a }\Gamma_{m}\text{-stable lattice of }\\
(\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime r}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i}^{\prime}))[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r}[1/t],\tau}K((t))\end{array}\right\}.

We define the set

\displaystyle S\coloneqq\left\{(y_{i}^{\prime},E_{i})\in X_{i}^{\prime}\times H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i}^{\prime})\in H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))\\
\text{such that }E_{i}[1/t]=(\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i}^{\prime}))[1/t]\end{array}\right\}.

Observe that there is a bijection

\displaystyle G_{i}^{-1}(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})

\displaystyle\rightarrow S

\displaystyle(y_{i}^{\prime},E_{i}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K\llbracket t\rrbracket)

\displaystyle\mapsfrom(y_{i}^{\prime},E_{i}),

where
E_{i}^{r}
is a
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r}
such that
E_{i}=E_{i}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}\mathcal{R}_{K}
.
In fact, the map is well-defined because obviously
E_{i}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K\llbracket t\rrbracket

is a
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice of
(\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime r}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i}^{\prime}))[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r}[1/t],\tau}K((t))

and it is in
Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})}
:
in fact

\mathrm{gr}^{j}(E_{i}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K\llbracket t\rrbracket)=t^{\mu_{j}}\mathrm{gr}^{j}(E_{i}^{\prime r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K\llbracket t\rrbracket)

for all
1\leqslant j\leqslant i
by similar computations to the ones in the proof of Lemma
6.6
.
Then the bijection descends by the bijection of Proposition
5.13
.
Finally by Lemma
7.13
we have that

\displaystyle\dim_{K}G_{i}^{-1}(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})=\dim_{K}S=

\displaystyle\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i-1}))(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

\displaystyle+d_{i}-\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1}))(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1})).

∎

Lemma 7.15
.

Let
D_{n-1}
be a triangulable
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{K}
of parameters

(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n-1})\in\mathcal{T}^{n-1}(K)
.
Let
\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}(K)
and let us define the following sets

\mathcal{L}\coloneqq\{i<n\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)\}
and

\mathcal{M}\coloneqq\{i<n\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)\}
.
We have

\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant n-1+|\mathcal{L}|+|\mathcal{M}|.

Proof.

We prove this result by induction.

n=2:

We distinguish two cases:

–

if
\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}(K)
, then
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{M}=\emptyset

and in fact

\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}))=1.

–

If instead
\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}(K)
, then
|\mathcal{L}|+|\mathcal{M}|=1

and

2=\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}))=1+|\mathcal{L}|+|\mathcal{M}|.

n>2:

Let
D_{n-2}
be the
n-2
-th piece of the filtration of
D_{n-1}
, so that

D_{n-1}\in H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n-1}^{-1}))
.
We have the following long exact sequence

\dots\rightarrow H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\rightarrow H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\rightarrow H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}))\rightarrow\dots.

Thus

\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))+\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n})).

Now we distinguish three cases:

–

if
\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}(K)
, then
\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}))=1
;
moreover
\mathcal{L}=\{i<n-1\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)\}
and

\mathcal{M}=\{i<n-1\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)\}
.
By inductive hypothesis then

\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant n-2+|\mathcal{L}|+|\mathcal{M}|

and hence

\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant n-2+|\mathcal{L}|+|\mathcal{M}|+1=n-1+|\mathcal{L}|+|\mathcal{M}|.

–

If
\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)
, then
\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}))=2
.
On the other hand, we have
\{i<n-1\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)\}=\mathcal{L}\setminus\{n-1\}

and
\mathcal{M}=\{i<n-1\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)\}
.
By inductive hypothesis we have

\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant n-2+|\mathcal{L}|-1+|\mathcal{M}|,

thus

\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant n-2+|\mathcal{L}|-1+|\mathcal{M}|+2=n-1+|\mathcal{L}|+|\mathcal{M}|.

–

Finally if
\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)
, we have again that

\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}))=2,

\mathcal{L}=\{i<n-1\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)\}
and

\{i<n-1\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)\}=\mathcal{M}\setminus\{n-1\}
.
Therefore by inductive hypothesis

\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant n-2+|\mathcal{L}|+|\mathcal{M}|-1.

Consequently

\dim_{K}H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant n-2+|\mathcal{L}|+|\mathcal{M}|-1+2=n-1+|\mathcal{L}|+|\mathcal{M}|.

∎

Proposition 7.16
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}\subset\mathcal{T}^{n}
be a subspace
defined as in (
7.3
).
We have that for
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}(K)

\dim_{K}G^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})\leqslant\sum_{i=2}^{n}d_{i}+\sum_{j=2}^{n}(|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|).

Proof.

Let
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}(K)
.
For
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
, let
(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})\in\mathrm{im}(G_{i})(K)

(if
i=2
we let
y_{i-1}=\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
,

\mathbb{D}_{i-1}=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}}(\widetilde{\delta}_{1})

and
\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}}(x^{-\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\delta}_{1})
).
By Proposition
7.14
and Lemma
7.15
we have

\displaystyle\dim_{K}G_{i}^{-1}(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})

\displaystyle=\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i-1}))(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

\displaystyle+d_{i}-\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1}))(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

\displaystyle\leqslant i-1+|\mathcal{L}_{i}|+|\mathcal{M}_{i}|

\displaystyle+d_{i}-\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1}))(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1})).

On the other hand, by Theorem
1.17
, we have

\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1}))(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))\geqslant i-1,

thus

\displaystyle\dim_{K}G_{i}^{-1}(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})

\displaystyle\leqslant i-1+|\mathcal{L}_{i}|+|\mathcal{M}_{i}|

\displaystyle+d_{i}-\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1}))(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

\displaystyle\leqslant d_{i}+|\mathcal{L}_{i}|+|\mathcal{M}_{i}|.

This proves the wanted inequality.
∎

It is possible to compute the exact dimension of the preimage of the regular locus through
F
.

Proposition 7.17
.

We have

\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})=2n+\sum_{i=2}^{n}d_{i}.

Proof.

It is easy to see that

\dim_{K}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}\cap\mathrm{im}(H_{n}))=n=\dim_{K}(H_{n}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})).

Notice that
H_{n}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})\subset\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}
by definition of
\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}
.
Fix
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in H_{n}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}(K)
.
For
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
, let
(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})\in\mathrm{im}(G_{i})(K)\cap G_{i-1}^{-1}\circ\dots\circ G_{2}^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})

(if
i=2
, we have
y_{i-1}=\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\in\mathcal{T}(K)
).
By Proposition
7.14
we have

\displaystyle\dim G_{i}^{-1}(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})

\displaystyle=\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i-1}))(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

\displaystyle+d_{i}-\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1}))(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1})).

Since
(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})\in G_{i-1}^{-1}\circ\dots\circ G_{2}^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})
,
we have that the parameters of the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathbb{D}_{i-1}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i-1})

are exactly
(\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{i-1})
.
Since
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}
, we have that

\mathcal{L}_{i}\coloneqq\{j<i\colon\widetilde{\eta}_{j}/\widetilde{\eta}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}(K)\}=\emptyset

and

\mathcal{M}_{i}\coloneqq\{j<i\colon\widetilde{\eta}_{j}/\widetilde{\eta}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)\}=\emptyset

for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
Thus by Lemma
7.15
, we have

\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i-1}))(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))\leqslant i-1.

On the other hand, by Theorem
1.17
, we know that

\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i-1}))(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))\geqslant i-1,

allowing us to conclude that the above inequalities are actually equalities.
Furthermore we have that
\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1})

has parameters
(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{i-1}x^{-\mu_{i-1}})
.
Since
\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}\notin\{x^{a}\colon a\in\mathbb{Z}\}\cup\{\chi x^{a}\colon a\in\mathbb{Z}\}

for all
i<j

(because
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in H_{n}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})
),
we have that
x^{-\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}(K)
for all
i<j
.
Therefore the same exact argument as above applies to

\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1}))(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

and we can conclude that

\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1}))(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))=i-1.

Finally, we have that

\displaystyle\dim G_{i}^{-1}(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})

\displaystyle=\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i-1}))(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

\displaystyle+d_{i}-\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1}))(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

\displaystyle=d_{i}.

This ends the proof.
∎

Remark 7.18
.

In particular observe that

\displaystyle\dim F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})=2n+\sum_{i=2}^{n}d_{i}=\dim(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}})=\dim X^{\prime}_{n}.

Now our goal is to show that the dimension of the preimage through
F
of certain
subspaces of
\mathcal{W}^{n}
is strictly less than the dimension of the preimage of the regular
locus
\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}
.

Like in the case of
\mathcal{T}^{n}
, we define subspaces of
\mathcal{W}^{n}
consisting of characters
(\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{n})

satisfying conditions of the form:

\begin{array}[]{lcr}\eta_{i}/\eta_{j}=x^{b_{ij}},&\eta_{i}/\eta_{j}=x^{-b_{ij}}\end{array}

for some
b_{ij}\in\mathbb{N}
.

Definition 7.19
.

For any
2\leqslant j\leqslant n
, fix
\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j}\subset\{1,\dots,j-1\}
;
for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant n
and for all
i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}\cup\mathcal{M}_{j}

let us fix
b_{ij}\in\mathbb{N}
.
We define

(7.4)

\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}\coloneqq\left\{\underline{\eta}\in\mathcal{W}^{n}\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\eta_{i}/\eta_{j}=x^{-b_{ij}}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}\text{ and }\\
\eta_{i}/\eta_{j}=x^{b_{ij}+1}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}\end{array}\right\}.

We then have that the union of such spaces covers the whole
\mathcal{W}^{n}
.
Since we are again interested in the dimension of
F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})})
,
we want to consider only those spaces such that

H_{n}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})})\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\neq\emptyset
.

Lemma 7.20
.

Let
\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}
be the set defined in (
7.4
).
We have that the space
H_{n}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})})
is covered
by those
\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}

such that
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{j}
and
\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{j}

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant n
.

Proof.

Let
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in H_{n}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})})
.
Using the isomorphism
\mathcal{T}\cong\mathcal{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
, we write

\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}=(\eta_{i},a_{i})_{i=1,\dots,n}
where
\eta_{i}\coloneqq\widetilde{\eta_{i}}_{|\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}

and
a_{i}\coloneqq\widetilde{\eta_{i}}(p)
.
Then for any
2\leqslant j\leqslant n
and any
i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}
, we let
i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}

if and only if
a_{i}/a_{j}=p^{-b_{ij}}
.
In the same way, for any
2\leqslant j\leqslant n
and any
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}
, we let
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}

if and only if
a_{i}/a_{j}=p^{b_{ij}}
.
It is easy to see that
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}
.
∎

Lemma 7.21
.

Let
K
be an extension of
L
and let
\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}

be as in (
7.4
).
Then

H_{n}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})})\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}(K)
is covered
by spaces of the form
\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}
,
where

\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{j}\cap\{i<j\colon b_{ij}=a_{ij}\}\cap\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}

and

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{j}\cap\{i<j\colon b_{ij}=a_{ij}\}\cap\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}\}.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{\underline{\eta}}\in H_{n}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})})\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}(K)
.
As proved in Lemma
7.20
, we have

\widetilde{\underline{\eta}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}
for some

\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{j}
and
\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{j}
.
By Lemma
7.12
, we have that

\widetilde{\underline{\eta}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}
for some

\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{j}\subseteq\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}
and

\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}_{j}\subseteq\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}\}
.
As the sets of the form
\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}
are disjoint,
we must have that
\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}=\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}
; in particular
we have
\widetilde{\underline{\eta}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}

with
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{j}\cap\{i<j\colon b_{ij}=a_{ij}\}\cap\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}
and

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{j}\cap\{i<j\colon b_{ij}=a_{ij}\}\cap\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}\}

as wanted.
∎

Lemma 7.22
.

Let us fix
\underline{\eta}\in\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}(K)
.
We have

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}=\dim_{K}(H_{n}^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}).

Proof.

Recall that

\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}\coloneqq\left\{\underline{\eta}\in\mathcal{W}^{n}(K)\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\eta_{i}/\eta_{j}=x^{-b_{ij}}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}\text{ and }\\
\eta_{i}/\eta_{j}=x^{b_{ij}+1}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}\end{array}\right\}.

Using the isomorphism
\mathcal{T}\cong\mathcal{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
, we get

\displaystyle H_{n}^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}\cong\left\{\underline{a}\in\mathbb{A}^{n}(K)\colon\begin{array}[]{c}a_{i}/a_{j}=p^{-b_{ij}}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}\text{ and}\\
a_{i}/a_{j}=p^{b_{ij}}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}\end{array}\right\}.

It is then obvious that these two spaces are isomorphic and hence have the same dimension.
∎

Theorem 7.23
.

If
\sum_{j=2}^{n}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|\leqslant 5
, then

\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

Proof.

Let us fix
\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}
.
We are going to show that for any
\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}

with
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{j}\cap\{i<j\colon b_{ij}=a_{ij}\}\cap\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}

and
\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{j}\cap\{i<j\colon b_{ij}=a_{ij}\}\cap\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}\}

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant n
such that
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}\neq\emptyset
,
for any
\underline{\eta}\in\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}(K)

and for any

\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in(H_{n}^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})})(K)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}
,
we have

(7.5)

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\dim_{K}(H_{n}^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})})+\dim_{K}G^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

By Lemma
7.21
this will prove the claim of the Theorem.
By Proposition
7.16
, we have

\dim G^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})\leqslant\sum_{i=2}^{n}d_{i}+\sum_{j=2}^{n}(|\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}|)

and by Lemma
7.22
, we have

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}=\dim_{K}(H_{n}^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}^{\prime}}^{(a_{ij})}).

Hence it is enough to show

(7.6)

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}+\sum_{i=2}^{n}d_{i}+\sum_{j=2}^{n}(|\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}|)<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

Observe that
\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}=\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(c_{ij})}

for any
b_{ij},c_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
, in fact the dimension of these spaces only depends on
the sets
\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j}
.
We claim now that it is enough to show the above inequality only for
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}=\mathcal{L}_{j}

and
\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}_{j}
:
assume in fact that for any
\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}
we have proved

2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\sum_{i=2}^{n}d_{i}+\sum_{j=2}^{n}(|\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}|)<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

If
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{j}
and
\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{j}

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant n
, then

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}\leqslant\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(c_{ij})}

for any
b_{ij},c_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We thus get the wanted inequality (
7.6
) for any
\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}

such that
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}\subsetneq\mathcal{L}_{j}
and
\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}\subsetneq\mathcal{M}_{j}
.
It is then left to prove the inequality (
7.6
) when

\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}=\mathcal{L}_{j}
and
\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}_{j}
.
In other words, we want to show

(7.7)

2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\sum_{i=2}^{n}d_{i}+\sum_{j=2}^{n}(|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|)<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})=2n+\sum_{i=2}^{n}d_{i}.

Now we proceed by cases:

•

if
\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|=1
, then we have

\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}
is cut out by only one equation
inside
\mathcal{W}^{n}
, hence
\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}=n-1
.
Therefore we have

\displaystyle 2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\sum_{j=2}^{n}(|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|)=2n-1<2n.

•

If
\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|=2
, then

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}=n-2

because we are cutting out two independent equations.
As a consequence

\displaystyle 2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\sum_{j=2}^{n}(|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|)\leqslant 2n-2<2n.

•

If
\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|=3
, then

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}\leqslant n-2
,
because it could be that there are only two independent equations and that one of the three
is a consequence of the other two.
More precisely, we could have for example
\eta_{i}/\eta_{j},\eta_{k}/\eta_{j}\in x^{\mathbb{Z}}

for some
i<k<j
: in this case, we will also have the equation
\eta_{i}/\eta_{k}\in x^{\mathbb{Z}}
,
but it depends on the previous two.
Thus we have

\displaystyle 2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\sum_{j=2}^{n}(|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|)\leqslant 2n-1<2n.

•

If
\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|=4
, then

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}\leqslant n-3
,
because we have at least three independent equations.
So we get

\displaystyle 2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\sum_{j=2}^{n}(|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|)\leqslant 2n-2<2n.

•

Finally, if
\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|=5
, then

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}\leqslant n-3
,
because two of the five equations could depend on the other three.
So we get

\displaystyle 2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\sum_{j=2}^{n}(|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|)\leqslant 2n-1<2n.

For all of the above cases we have then showed that the inequality (
7.7
) holds.
∎

Corollary 7.24
.

If
|\{\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}\colon i<j\}|\leqslant 5
,
then for any
\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}
we have

\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

Proof.

The claim is true if
\sum_{j=2}^{n}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|\leqslant 5
by Theorem
7.23
.
Suppose now that
\sum_{j=2}^{n}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|>5
.
Exactly as in the proof of Theorem
7.23
,
we want to show that for any
\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}

with
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{j}\cap\{i<j\colon b_{ij}=a_{ij}\}\cap\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}

and
\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{j}\cap\{i<j\colon b_{ij}=a_{ij}\}\cap\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}\}

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant n
such that
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}\neq\emptyset
,
for any
\underline{\eta}\in\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}(K)

and for any

\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in(H_{n}^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})})(K)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}
,
we have

(7.8)

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\dim_{K}(H_{n}^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})})+\dim_{K}G^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

By Lemma
7.21
this will prove the claim of the Corollary.
Since
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{j}
and
\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{j}
for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant n
,
we have
\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}\leqslant\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}
.
Now notice that by construction
\sum_{j=2}^{n}|\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}|\leqslant 5
and
in the proof of Theorem
7.23
we have showed that

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\dim_{K}(H_{n}^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})})+\dim_{K}G^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})

holds.
This implies that (
7.8
) holds too.
∎

Lemma 7.25
.

Let
D_{n-1}
be a triangulable
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{K}

of parameters
(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n-1})\in\mathcal{T}^{n-1}(K)
for some extension
K
of
L
.
Let
\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}(K)
and we define

\mathcal{M}\coloneqq\{i<n\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)\}
.
We have that

\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant|\mathcal{M}|.

Proof.

We proceed by induction:

n=2:

If
\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)
,
then
\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}))=0
, so the claim is obviously true.
If
\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)
, then
\mathcal{M}=\{1\}
and

\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}))=1=|\mathcal{M}|
.

n>2
:

Let
D_{n-2}
be the
n-2
-th piece of the filtration of
D_{n-1}
giving rise to the parameters

(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n-1})
; we have the long exact sequence

\dots\rightarrow H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\rightarrow H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\rightarrow H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}))\rightarrow 0.

As a consequence

\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))+\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n})).

By inductive hypothesis we have

\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|,

where
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\coloneqq\{i<n-1\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)\}
.

–

If
\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)
, then
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and

\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|=|\mathcal{M}|.

–

If
\delta_{n-1}/\delta_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)
, then
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cup\{n-1\}
and

\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-1}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))\leqslant\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(D_{n-2}(\delta_{n}^{-1}))+1\leqslant|\mathcal{M}^{\prime}|+1=|\mathcal{M}|.

∎

Theorem 7.26
.

Let
n=4
.
Let
\mathcal{L}_{j}\coloneqq\{i<j\colon(\delta_{i}/\delta_{j})_{|\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\in x^{-\mathbb{N}}\}
and

\mathcal{M}_{j}\coloneqq\{i<j\colon(\delta_{i}/\delta_{j})_{|\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\in x^{\mathbb{N}_{>0}}\}
for any

2\leqslant j\leqslant 4
.
If

(7.9)

2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\sum_{j=2}^{4}|\mathcal{M}_{j}|<8,

then

\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

Proof.

Again, we want to show that for any
\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}

with
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{j}\cap\{i<j\colon b_{ij}=a_{ij}\}\cap\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{-}\}

and
\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{j}\cap\{i<j\colon b_{ij}=a_{ij}\}\cap\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}\}

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant 4
such that
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{4}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}\neq\emptyset
,
for any
\underline{\eta}\in\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}

and for any

\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in(H_{4}^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{4})(K)
,
we have

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\dim_{K}(H_{4}^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})})+\dim_{K}G^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

By Lemma
7.22
, we have

\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}=\dim_{K}(H_{4}^{-1}(\underline{\eta})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}^{\prime}}^{(a_{ij})}),

thus it is enough to show

(7.10)
