\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}+\dim_{K}G^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

Now notice that by Theorem
7.23
, we have

2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\dim_{K}G^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{\mathrm{reg}})

whenever
\sum_{j=2}^{4}|\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}|\leqslant 5
.
Since
\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}\leqslant\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}
,
we have that (
7.10
) holds when
\sum_{j=2}^{4}|\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}|\leqslant 5
.
Notice that
|\{i<j\colon 2\leqslant j\leqslant 4\}|=6
, thus it is only left to show that the inequality
(
7.10
) holds when
\sum_{j=2}^{4}(|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|)=6
and

\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}=\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}_{j}
for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant 4
.
We will show that for any
y_{2}\in G_{2}^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})
,
for any
y_{3}\in G_{3}^{-1}(y_{2},\widetilde{\eta}_{3},\widetilde{\eta}_{4})
and for any

y_{4}\in G_{4}^{-1}(y_{3},\widetilde{\eta}_{4})
we have

(7.11)

\begin{split}2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}&+\dim_{K}G_{2}^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})+\dim_{K}G_{3}^{-1}(y_{2},\widetilde{\eta}_{3},\widetilde{\eta}_{4})+\dim_{K}G_{4}^{-1}(y_{3},\widetilde{\eta}_{4})\\
&<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{\mathrm{reg}}).\end{split}

By Proposition
7.14
, we have

\displaystyle\dim G_{i}^{-1}(y_{i-1},\widetilde{\eta}_{i},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n})

\displaystyle=\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i-1}))(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

\displaystyle+d_{i}-\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}((\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i-1}))(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1})).

for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant 4
.
For simplicity, let
E_{i}\coloneqq\mathbb{D}_{i}\widehat{\otimes}k(y_{i})
and
E_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}k(y^{\prime}_{i})
.
Hence the inequality (
7.11
) becomes

(7.12)

\begin{split}2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}&+\sum_{i=2}^{4}(\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}(E_{i-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))-\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1})))<2n.\end{split}

By Theorem
1.17
, we have

\displaystyle\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}(E_{i-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))-\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{1}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

\displaystyle=\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))+\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

\displaystyle-\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))-\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1})).

First of all notice that by the way we have chosen the neighbourhood
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{4}^{\prime}
, we have

x^{-\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}(K)

for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant 4
, hence
\dim_{K}H^{2}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))=0

by Lemma
7.25
.
Moreover observe that since
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}
,
we have that

\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}
for all
i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}\cup\mathcal{M}_{j}

and
2\leqslant j\leqslant 4
.
Since we are treating the case where
\sum_{j=2}^{4}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|=6
, we have that

\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}
for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant 4
.
In particular this tells us that

\mathrm{wt}(x^{-\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j})\notin\mathbb{N}_{>0}
.
By Proposition
5.18
, we have that

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1})[1/t])

for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
Moreover by Lemma
7.10
we have that the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules

E_{i-1},E_{i-1}^{\prime}
satisfy
the hypothesis of Lemma
7.9
and hence

\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))-\dim_{K}H^{0}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))\leqslant 0.

It is thus sufficient to prove that

(7.13)

\begin{split}2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}&+\sum_{i=2}^{4}\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{2}(E_{i-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))<2n.\end{split}

By Lemma
7.25
we have that

\dim_{K}H_{\phi,\Gamma}^{2}(E_{i-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))<|\mathcal{M}_{i}|

for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
.
It is then enough to show that

2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\sum_{i=2}^{4}|\mathcal{M}_{i}|<2n

holds, but this true by hypothesis.
∎

Remark 7.27
.

Let

\begin{array}[]{l l l}\mathcal{L}_{j}\coloneqq\{i<j\colon(\delta_{i}/\delta_{j})_{|\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\in x^{-\mathbb{N}}\}&\text{ and }&\mathcal{M}_{j}\coloneqq\{i<j\colon(\delta_{i}/\delta_{j})_{|\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\in x^{\mathbb{N}_{>0}}\}\end{array}

for any
2\leqslant j\leqslant 4
.
Notice in the proof of Theorem
7.26
that the condition
(
7.9
) is necessary only when
\sum_{j=2}^{4}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|=6

and
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}(L)
for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant 4
.
Otherwise the claim holds by Theorem
7.23
without the
need of assuming (
7.9
).

Corollary 7.28
.

For
n=3,4
and for any
\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}
we have

\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

Proof.

We treat the two cases
n=3
and
n=4
separately.

n=3
:

In this case we have that
\sum_{j=1}^{3}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|\leqslant 3

for any
\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j}
.
Hence the claim descends directly from Theorem
7.23
.

n=4:

If
\sum_{j=1}^{4}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|\leqslant 5
, then the wanted inequality
holds by Theorem
7.23
.
If
\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|=6
, then we distinguish two cases:

–

if
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}(L)
for some
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant 4
,
then by Remark
7.27
we have that

\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})

as wanted.

–

If instead
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\notin\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}(L)
for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant 4
,
then it means that there is

\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(a_{ij})}

such that

\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta_{j}}=x^{-a_{ij}}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}
and

\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta_{j}}=\chi x^{a_{ij}}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant 4
.
As a consequence we have

x^{-\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\widetilde{\eta_{j}}=x^{-a_{ij}-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}
and

x^{-\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\widetilde{\eta_{j}}=\chi x^{a_{ij}-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}\Leftrightarrow i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}

for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant 4
.
Since
(x^{-\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\dots,x^{-\mu_{4}}\widetilde{\eta_{4}})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{4}^{\prime}(K)
,
by Remark
7.3
we have

x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}=x^{-a_{ij}-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}\text{ for all }i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}

and

x^{-\mu_{i}}\delta_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\delta_{j}=\chi x^{a_{ij}-\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}\text{ for all }i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}

and for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant 4
.
Obviously we get
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=x^{-a_{ij}}
for all
i\in\mathcal{L}_{j}
and

\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=\chi x^{a_{ij}}
for all
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}
and for all
2\leqslant j\leqslant 4
.
In particular we have
\mathcal{L}_{j}=\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=x^{-a_{ij}}\}
and

\mathcal{M}_{j}=\{i<j\colon\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}=\chi x^{a_{ij}}\}
for all

2\leqslant j\leqslant 4
.
It is easy to see that there are only three possibilities
so that
\sum_{j=2}^{4}|\mathcal{L}_{j}|+|\mathcal{M}_{j}|=6
and
\sum_{j=2}^{4}|\mathcal{M}_{j}|\geqslant 1
:

(1)

\begin{array}[]{lcr}\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}=\chi x^{a_{12}},&\delta_{1}/\delta_{3}=\chi x^{a_{13}},&\delta_{1}/\delta_{4}=\chi x^{a_{14}},\\
\delta_{2}/\delta_{3}=x^{-a_{12}+a_{13}}=x^{-a_{23}},&\delta_{2}/\delta_{4}=x^{-a_{12}+a_{14}}=x^{-a_{24}},&\\
\delta_{3}/\delta_{4}=x^{-a_{13}+a_{14}}=x^{-a_{14}}.&&\end{array}

(2)

\begin{array}[]{lcr}\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}=x^{-a_{12}},&\delta_{1}/\delta_{3}=\chi x^{a_{13}},&\delta_{1}/\delta_{4}=\chi x^{a_{14}},\\
\delta_{2}/\delta_{3}=\chi x^{a_{12}+a_{13}}=\chi x^{a_{23}},&\delta_{2}/\delta_{4}=\chi x^{a_{12}+a_{14}}=\chi x^{a_{24}},&\\
\delta_{3}/\delta_{4}=x^{-a_{13}+a_{14}}=x^{-a_{14}}.&&\end{array}

(3)

\begin{array}[]{lcr}\delta_{1}/\delta_{2}=x^{-a_{12}},&\delta_{1}/\delta_{3}=x^{-a_{13}},&\delta_{1}/\delta_{4}=\chi x^{a_{14}},\\
\delta_{2}/\delta_{3}=x^{a_{12}-a_{13}}=x^{-a_{23}},&\delta_{2}/\delta_{4}=\chi x^{a_{12}+a_{14}}=\chi x^{a_{24}},&\\
\delta_{3}/\delta_{4}=\chi x^{a_{13}+a_{14}}=\chi x^{a_{14}}.&&\end{array}

For the three above cases we have

2\dim_{K}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}}^{(b_{ij})}+\sum_{j=2}^{4}|\mathcal{M}_{j}|=2+3=5<8.

This shows that the condition (
7.9
) is always satisfied,
hence we can conclude by Theorem
7.26
.

∎

Lemma 7.29
.

Recall that by construction (Theorem
4.2
) we have

X_{n}^{\prime}\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime}\times\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}
,
where
d_{i}\coloneqq\dim_{L}H^{1}_{(\phi,\Gamma)}(D_{i-1}^{\prime}(\delta_{i}^{-1}x^{\mu_{i}}))
.
Let
z^{\prime}\coloneqq(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\prime},\underline{a})\in(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime}\times\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}})(K)=X^{\prime}_{n}(K)

for some extension
K
of
L

and let

p:X_{n}\rightarrow X_{n}^{\prime}

be the projection map.
We have that the fiber
p^{-1}(z^{\prime})
is in bijection with the set

\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}(\phi,\Gamma)\text{-modules }E_{n}\text{ over }\mathcal{R}_{K}\text{ of parameters }(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime}x^{\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}_{n}x^{\mu_{n}})\\
\text{ such that }E_{n}[1/t]=z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}[1/t]\end{array}\right\}.

Proof.

By definition of
X_{n}
we have that the fiber
p^{-1}(z^{\prime})
is the set

\displaystyle\{\Lambda_{z}\in Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})}\colon\Lambda_{z}\text{ is a }\Gamma_{m}\text{-stable lattice of }z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime r}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K((t))\}.

By Proposition
5.13
, we know that there is a bijection between the two sets

\displaystyle\{\Lambda_{z}\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}\colon\Lambda_{z}\text{ is a }\Gamma_{m}\text{-stable lattice of }z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime r}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K((t))\}

and

\displaystyle\{(\phi,\Gamma)\text{-modules }E_{n}\text{ over }\mathcal{R}_{K}\text{ such that }E_{n}[1/t]=z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}[1/t]\}.

It is then enough to show that if
E_{n}
has parameters
(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime}x^{\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}_{n}x^{\mu_{n}})
,
then the corresponding
\Lambda_{z}
through the bijection in Proposition
5.13

is in
Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})}
.
Let us denote by
\Lambda_{z}^{\prime}
the
\Gamma_{m}
-stable lattice of
z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime r}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K((t))

associated to
z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime r}
.
Observe that

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{1}(\Lambda_{z})

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{z}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{1}(K((t))^{n})=(E_{n}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K\llbracket t\rrbracket)\cap(\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r}(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime})[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r}[1/t],\tau}K((t)))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r}(x^{\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K\llbracket t\rrbracket=t^{\mu_{1}}\mathrm{gr}^{1}(\Lambda_{z}^{\prime}).

Moreover for every
2\leqslant i\leqslant n
let
E_{i}
be the
i
-th piece of the filtration of

E_{n}
giving parameters
(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime}x^{\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}_{n}x^{\mu_{n}})
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\Lambda_{z})

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{z}\cap\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(K((t))^{n})=(E_{n}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K\llbracket t\rrbracket)\cap(z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime r}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r}[1/t],\tau}K((t)))

\displaystyle=E_{i}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K\llbracket t\rrbracket.

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{i}(\Lambda_{z})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Fil}^{i}(\Lambda_{z})/\mathrm{Fil}^{i-1}(\Lambda_{z})=(E_{i}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K\llbracket t\rrbracket)/(E_{i-1}^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K\llbracket t\rrbracket)

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{K}^{r},\tau}K\llbracket t\rrbracket=t^{\mu_{i}}\mathrm{gr}^{i}(\Lambda_{z}^{\prime}).

The above computations show that
\Lambda_{z}\in Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})}
and ends the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.30
.

Let
W
be the intersection of
\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}
and
\mathrm{im}(F)
in
\mathcal{W}^{n}
, let

W^{\prime}\coloneqq\{(\eta_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\dots,\eta_{n}x^{-\mu_{n}})\colon(\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{n})\in W\}\subset H_{n}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime})

and let
T^{\prime}
be the intersection of
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime}
and
H_{n}^{-1}(W^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{T}^{n}
.
The map

(7.14)

p:F^{-1}(W)\rightarrow T^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}=X^{\prime}

given by the restriction of the map
X_{n}\rightarrow X^{\prime}_{n}
to
F^{-1}(W)
is a homeomorphism.

Proof.

First of all we show that if
z=(z^{\prime},\Lambda_{z})\in F^{-1}(W)
,
then
z^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}
;
in other words we show that the parameters
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\prime}

of
z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{n}
are in
T^{\prime}
:
in fact by Theorem
7.6
, we have that
z^{*}\mathbb{D}_{n}
has parameters

(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime}x^{\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}^{\prime}x^{\mu_{n}})
;
moreover we know
z\in F^{-1}(W)
, which implies

H_{n}(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime}x^{\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}^{\prime}x^{\mu_{n}})\in W
;
as a consequence we have
H_{n}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\prime})\in W^{\prime}
, hence

\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in T^{\prime}
.
We have thus showed that the map
p
is well-defined.
Now let
T\coloneqq H_{n}^{-1}(W)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}
.
Observe that

\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in T\Leftrightarrow(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}x^{-\mu_{n}})\in T^{\prime}:

in fact by definition we have that

\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in T=H_{n}^{-1}(W)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}

if and only if
H_{n}(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}x^{-\mu_{n}})\in W^{\prime}
and

(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}x^{-\mu_{n}})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime}
,
which means that

(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}x^{-\mu_{n}})\in H_{n}^{-1}(W^{\prime})\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime}=T^{\prime}
.
We will now prove that the map

\displaystyle p:F^{-1}(W)=\bigcup_{\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in T}G^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})\rightarrow

\displaystyle\bigcup_{\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}}\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}

\displaystyle=T^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}

is a bijection.
We will show this by proving that for every
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\in T(K)
for some
extension
K
of
L
, we have
that the map

p:G^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})\rightarrow(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}x^{-\mu_{n}})\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}

given by the restriction of the map
p
to
G^{-1}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})
is a bijection.
Let
z^{\prime}\coloneqq(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}x^{-\mu_{n}})\times\underline{a}\in(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}x^{-\mu_{1}},\dots,\widetilde{\eta}_{n}x^{-\mu_{n}})\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}
.
By Lemma
7.29
, we have that
p^{-1}(z^{\prime})
is in bijection with the set

\{(\phi,\Gamma)\text{-modules }E_{n}\text{ over }\mathcal{R}_{K}\text{ of parameters }\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}\text{ such that }E_{n}[1/t]=z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}[1/t]\}

and we will now show that such set has cardinality 1, which implies the desired bijection.
Since
H_{n}(\underline{\widetilde{\eta}})\in\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}(K)
, we have that
\mathrm{wt}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/\widetilde{\eta}_{j})\notin\mathbb{N}_{>0}

for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n
.
This in particular implies that
\mathrm{wt}(x^{-\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}/x^{-\mu_{j}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j})\notin\mathbb{N}_{>0}

for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n
by Remark
7.3
.
Thus by Proposition
5.18
, we have the following isomorphism:

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}/\widetilde{\eta}_{2}))\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}/\widetilde{\eta}_{2})[1/t])\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(x^{-\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}/x^{-\mu_{2}}\widetilde{\eta}_{2})).

Let us denote by
E_{2}
the extension of
\mathcal{R}_{K}(\widetilde{\eta}_{2})
by
\mathcal{R}_{K}(\widetilde{\eta}_{1})

corresponding to the extension
z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{2}^{\prime}\in H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{R}_{K}(x^{-\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}/x^{-\mu_{2}}\widetilde{\eta}_{2}))

through the isomorphism above.
In an iterative manner, for every
3\leqslant i\leqslant n
we define the
(\phi,\Gamma)
-module
E_{i}

to be the extension of
\mathcal{R}_{K}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i})
by
E_{i-1}
corresponding to the extension

z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}\in H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))
through the
isomorphism

H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(E_{i-1}(\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1})[1/t])\cong H^{1}_{\phi,\Gamma}(z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{i-1}^{\prime}(x^{\mu_{i}}\widetilde{\eta}_{i}^{-1}))

given by Proposition
5.18
.
We have then found a unique
E_{n}
of parameters
\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}
such that

E_{n}[1/t]=z^{\prime*}\mathbb{D}_{n}^{\prime}[1/t]
and this proves that the above map is a bijection.
Finally we have that

\displaystyle F^{-1}(W)

\displaystyle=G^{-1}(H_{n}^{-1}(W)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n})=G^{-1}(T)

\displaystyle=X_{n}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}}T=X_{n}^{\prime}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}}Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}}T

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}}Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}}T

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}^{\prime}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}}T\times\mathbb{A}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}_{L}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}}Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})}

\displaystyle=T^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}_{L}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}}Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})}.

Recall that for locally ringed spaces
X
,
Y
over a field
k
, the natural projection
X\times_{k}Y\rightarrow X

is open.
In our case, this translates to the map

p:T^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}\times\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathrm{rig}}_{n}}Y_{(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})}\rightarrow T^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{2},\mathrm{an}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{A}_{L}^{d_{n},\mathrm{an}}

being open.
We have then showed that
p
is continuous, open and a bijection, hence a homeomorphism.
∎

Theorem 7.31
.

If
n=3,4
or if
|\{\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}\colon i<j\}|\leqslant 5
,
the irredeucible component
Z
of
X_{n}
containing the point
x_{n}
is equal to the closure of
F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})
.

Proof.

Let
W\coloneqq\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}\cap\mathrm{im}(F)\subset\mathcal{W}^{n}

and let
Z^{\prime}
be the closure of
F^{-1}(W)
in
X_{n}
.
By Lemma
7.30
we have that
F^{-1}(W)
is irreducible,
thus
Z^{\prime}
is irreducible, as closure of irreducible.
Now we show that
Z^{\prime}
is an irreducible component of
X_{n}
: assume
\widetilde{Z}\subset Z^{\prime}

is an irreducible component of
X_{n}
containing
Z^{\prime}
.
Let
W_{0}
be an open in
W
, then
F^{-1}(W_{0})
is open in
Z^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{Z}
, hence

\dim\widetilde{Z}=\dim F^{-1}(W_{0})=\dim Z^{\prime}.

This shows that
Z^{\prime}=\widetilde{Z}
, since they are both irreducible of the same dimension.
Now we prove that
Z^{\prime}=Z
:
let

V\coloneqq Z\setminus\bigcup_{Z^{\prime\prime}\neq Z}Z^{\prime\prime}\subset Z

where the union ranges over all the irreducible components
Z^{\prime\prime}
of
X_{n}
that are not
Z
.
Notice that
V
is open in
Z
, hence by Theorem
6.20

and Remark
7.18
we have

\dim V=\dim Z\geqslant\dim X_{n}^{\prime}=\dim F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

Finally, we have
V\subseteq F^{-1}(F(V))
, thus

\dim F^{-1}(F(V))\geqslant\dim V\geqslant\dim F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

On the other hand, by Corollary
7.28
and Corollary
7.24
, for every

\mathcal{L}_{j}\subseteq\{i<j\colon(\delta_{i}/\delta_{j})_{|\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\in x^{-\mathbb{N}}\}
and

\mathcal{M}_{j}\subseteq\{i<j\colon(\delta_{i}/\delta_{j})_{|\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\in x^{\mathbb{N}_{>0}}\}

we have that
\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}})<\dim_{K}F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})
.
Therefore we must have
F^{-1}(F(V))\cap F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}})\neq\emptyset
, which
is a contradiction, as
F(V)\cap\mathcal{W}_{n}^{\mathrm{reg}}=\emptyset
by definition of
V
.
∎

Remark 7.32
.

Theorem
7.31
concludes the proof of Theorem
B
.
Notice that the only part in which we need the assumption
n\leqslant 4
or the assumption

|\{\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\in\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{reg}}\colon i<j\}|
is for
giving a bound to the dimension of the sets of the form
F^{-1}(\mathcal{W}^{(b_{ij})}_{(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{M}_{j})_{j}})
.
